linearization repair ever transposes. Formally, VCs \ref{vc:comm} and \ref{vc:inv} are
jointly the \emph{swap oracle} of the convergence engine
(Theorem~\ref{thm:eqconv}): for a pending set $E$, a duplicate-free
$\loE{E}$-respecting prefix list $\pi$ drawn from $E$, and events
$a, b \in E \setminus \pi$ with $a \neq b$, $a$ and $b$ $\loE{E}$-incomparable, and both
\emph{enabled} at $\pi$
\[
  \bigl(\forall z \in E.\;\; z \neq a \wedge z \mathrel{\loE{E}} a \Rightarrow z \in
  \pi, \text{ and likewise for } b\bigr),
\]
the oracle must supply $\mathrm{EqSwap}(a, b, \fold(\st, \pi))$. The enabledness
restriction is clause (ii)+(iii) made precise: the oracle is consulted only at prefixes
containing both swapped events' whole $\loE{E}$-past, exactly the prefixes a repair
visits.
\end{vc}

\begin{vc}[Merge--linearization bridge --- the conditioned Join Lemma]\label{vc:merge}
For a reachable LCA state $l$ and branches $a,b$ obtained from $l$ by disjoint concurrent
event lists, $\merge\,l\,a\,b \eqobs \fold\,l\,\pi$ for a $\loE{E}$-respecting enumeration $\pi$
of the branch events. Formally: at ${\eqobs} = {=}$ this is exactly the
per-configuration ternary Join Lemma of Definition~\ref{def:join}
(\lean{JoinLemma3At}). At a nontrivial quotient it is the $\eqobs$-relaxed form
(\lean{EqJoinLemma3C}, \lean{Metatheory/GenericEqQuotient.lean}): with canonicity up
to $\eqobs$
\[
  \mathrm{Can}^{\eqobs}_C(E,\, s) \;:\Longleftrightarrow\; \exists \rho.\;\;
  \rho \text{ enumerates } E \;\wedge\;
  \rho \text{ respects } \mathsf{lo}^{E}_{\eqobs} \;\wedge\;
  \fold(\Sinit, \rho) \eqobs s
\]
(\lean{IsCanonicalStateEq}; $\mathsf{lo}^{E}_{\eqobs}$ is $\loE{E}$ with commutation
read as $\eqobs$-commutation on $\Inv$-states), the join demands: for $\Inv$-satisfying
$s_0, s_1, s_2$ and \emph{fully closed} sides $E_1, E_2$, each of which,
\emph{and their union} $E_1 \cup E_2$, satisfies the
datatype's generation-discipline predicate (a bundle parameter), canonicity of
$s_0, s_1, s_2$ at $E_1 \cap E_2, E_1, E_2$ implies
$\mathrm{Can}^{\eqobs}_C(E_1 \cup E_2,\; \mergeL(s_0, s_1, s_2))$. The fold is the
\emph{raw} update: the quotient layer's internal guard is discharged at the transfer,
so the datatype states its $\eqobs$-join over its own $\dodo$, not a guarded
surrogate.
\end{vc}

For flat datatypes $\Inv = \app = \mathsf{true}$, VC~\ref{vc:comm} degenerates to
unconditioned commutation, VC~\ref{vc:inv} to $J = \mathsf{true}$, and VC~\ref{vc:merge} to
the ordinary Join Lemma --- recovering the unconditioned methodology.

\section{The metatheorem}\label{sec:meta}

\begin{theorem}[Soundness --- conditioned VCs $\Rightarrow$ RA-linearizability
(\emph{pen and paper})]\label{thm:meta}
Any MRDT satisfying VCs~\ref{vc:disc}--\ref{vc:merge} is RA-linearizable
(Definition~\ref{def:ralin}, read up to $\eqobs$).
\end{theorem}

\emph{Status of the theorem.} This general form (arbitrary $\Inv$, $\app$, $\eqobs$)
is a paper-level statement, proved below by two paper-level lemmas; deliberately, no
single Lean declaration carries it, and none is cited on it. What is mechanized is its
\emph{instances}, which are the capstones the catalogue actually rests on: the flat
instance ($\Inv = \app = \top$, ${\eqobs} = {=}$) is Theorem~\ref{thm:joinc}
(\lean{ra\_linearizable3\_of\_joinC}); the convergence engine consumed by
Lemma~\ref{lem:conv} is Theorem~\ref{thm:eqconv} (\lean{eq\_convergence}); and the
$\eqobs$-quotient layers (\lean{Metatheory/GenericEqQuotient.lean},
\lean{GenericEqQuotient\_H.lean}, \lean{GoodConfig3H.lean}, capstone shape
\lean{IsRALinearizable3Eq}) mechanize the adequacy induction at the quotient for the
production instances of \S\ref{sec:route} and Part~II.

The proof is generic --- it never inspects a particular datatype --- and factors through two
lemmas, each proved over the abstract signature with $\eqobs$ an arbitrary congruence for
$\dodo$ and $\merge$.

\begin{lemma}[Convergence]\label{lem:conv}
VCs~\ref{vc:disc},~\ref{vc:comm},~\ref{vc:inv} imply that all $\loE{E}$-respecting admissible
enumerations of a backward-closed reachable $E$ fold from $\ini$ to $\eqobs$-equal states;
hence $\sigmacan(E)$ is well defined up to $\eqobs$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Any two $\loE{E}$-respecting enumerations of $E$ are related by repeatedly transposing adjacent
$\loE{E}$-incomparable events. Because $\loE{E}$ is non-transitive, ``respecting'' is the elementwise
\lean{Pairwise} condition rather than sortedness, so this order-repair is more delicate than
for a total order --- it is exactly what the generic engine \lean{eq\_convergence} carries out,
and we appeal to it rather than re-derive it. Each transposition preserves the fold up to
$\eqobs$ by VC~\ref{vc:comm}, provided the swap precondition holds at the transposition point;
VC~\ref{vc:inv} supplies it along the whole enumeration (base at each event's enabling fold,
preserved by each step, scoped to the enabled events --- the only ones the repair transposes).
The engine consumes only that $\eqobs$ is an equivalence, $\dodo$ is $\eqobs$-congruent, and a
swap oracle of the shape VC~\ref{vc:comm}$+$\ref{vc:inv} provides (Lean: \lean{eq\_convergence},
kernel-clean).
\end{proof}

The engine itself is a displayable theorem, and its statement is the precise form of
the two VCs' joint content:

\begin{theorem}[the generic $\eqobs$-convergence engine]\label{thm:eqconv}
Fix an update $\dodo$ over a state space with an equivalence $\eqobs$ for which the
fold is congruent, and \emph{any} binary relation $\ell$ on events (the engine is
order-agnostic; Lemma~\ref{lem:conv} instantiates $\ell \coloneqq \loE{E}$). Let $E$
be a finite event set and $\pi_1, \pi_2$ two enumerations of $E$, each respecting
$\ell$, and let $\st$ be any start state. Suppose the swap oracle holds at $\st$:
for every duplicate-free $\ell$-respecting list
$\pi$ drawn from $E$ and every pair $a, b \in E \setminus \pi$ with $a \neq b$,
$\neg\,\ell(a,b)$, $\neg\,\ell(b,a)$, and both enabled at $\pi$ (every
$\ell$-predecessor in $E$ of $a$, and of $b$, lies in $\pi$),
$\mathrm{EqSwap}(a, b, \fold(\st, \pi))$. Then
\[
  \fold(\st, \pi_1) \;\eqobs\; \fold(\st, \pi_2).
\]
\hfill\lean{eq\_convergence}
\end{theorem}

\begin{remark}[where the engine lives, and what is generic in the mechanization]
\label{rem:eqconvhome}
The declaration's home is
\lean{MRDT\_Instances/RGA\_Rehoming/RGA\_ConditionedConvergence.lean}: an instance
directory, not \lean{Framework/} or \lean{Metatheory/}, a bookkeeping accident of its
genesis in the rehoming stress test. Its statement is payload-generic (the element
type is a parameter) and order-generic in $\ell$, but it is stated over that file's
concrete sequence-state functor with its concrete $\eqobs$ (proved an equivalence with
congruent fold in the same file), not over an abstract signature; the fully abstract
reading is exactly Lemma~\ref{lem:conv}'s pen-and-paper step. Two mechanization
details folded out of the display: the Lean statement threads an explicit length
parameter for the strong induction (universally quantified, hence eliminable), and the
oracle restriction to enabled prefixes is load-bearing: quantifying $\pi$ over all
lists is unsatisfiable, since several swap-discharge conjuncts are provably false at
junk prefixes.
\end{remark}

\begin{lemma}[Canonical adequacy]\label{lem:adeq}
VC~\ref{vc:merge} and Lemma~\ref{lem:conv} imply that every reachable version state is
$\eqobs\ \sigmacan$ of its delivered event set.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Induction over the version DAG. Initial: the empty fold. Update node: appends one delivered
operation to a canonical fold (definitional). Merge node with LCA $l$ and branches $a,b$: by
hypothesis $a \eqobs \sigmacan(E_a)$ and $b \eqobs \sigmacan(E_b)$, and $l \eqobs
\sigmacan(E_l)$. First rewrite $a,b$ to literal canonical folds using $\eqobs$-congruence of
$\merge$ (so VC~\ref{vc:merge}, stated for branch folds, applies); then VC~\ref{vc:merge}
gives $\merge\,l\,a\,b \eqobs \fold\,l\,\pi$ for a $\loE{E}$-respecting $\pi$ of the branch
events $E_a \cup E_b$. Now $E_l$ causally precedes those branch events (they were generated on
states derived from $l$), so a $\loE{E}$-respecting enumeration of $E_l \cup E_a \cup E_b$
factors as (an $\loE{E}$-enumeration of $E_l$) followed by $\pi$; hence
$\fold\,l\,\pi = \fold\,(\sigmacan(E_l))\,\pi \eqobs \sigmacan(E_l \cup E_a \cup E_b)$ by
Lemma~\ref{lem:conv} (the engine is start-state generic). Backward closure keeps every
enumeration admissible.
\end{proof}

\begin{proof}[Proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:meta}]
Immediate from Lemma~\ref{lem:adeq}: each version state $\eqobs \sigmacan(E_v)$, which is
Definition~\ref{def:ralin}. (Unconditioned template: \lean{ra\_linearizable3\_of\_joinC} plus
the closure-indexed adequacy bridge, already kernel-clean with nine instances; the
conditioned metatheorem generalizes it by carrying $\Inv/\app$ through the two lemmas.)
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Strong eventual consistency]\label{cor:sec}
Two versions with the same delivered set $E$ have $\eqobs$ states: both $\eqobs \sigmacan(E)$
by Theorem~\ref{thm:meta}, hence $\eqobs$ each other.
\end{corollary}

\begin{remark}[The flat instances]
The nine flat production MRDTs (counter, OR-set, MVR, \dots) take $\Inv=\app=\mathsf{true}$:
VC~\ref{vc:comm} is their unconditioned commutation, VC~\ref{vc:inv} is $J=\mathsf{true}$,
and VC~\ref{vc:merge} is the ordinary Join Lemma --- already discharged
(\lean{ra\_linearizable3\_of\_joinC}). They inherit RA-linearizability from the same
Theorem~\ref{thm:meta}. The instances of \S\ref{sec:route} and Part~II
exercise the conditioned mechanisms proper.
\end{remark}

\section{The factored discharge route: honest reachability and
per-configuration joins}
\label{sec:route}

The conditioned instances in production do not each re-prove
VCs~\ref{vc:comm} and~\ref{vc:inv} from scratch. What they share is an
induction: thread a per-configuration honesty contract through
reachability, and invoke a Join Lemma at each merge. This section
presents that induction, extracted once, together with the instances
that ride it: the mergeable queue (the flagship, whose conflict graph
admits no $\rcrel$ at all), the bounded counter (conditioning for
safety), and the FWW reservation register (payload arbitration).
Part~II's embedded-chain RGA is discharged by exactly this route.

\paragraph{The factorization: one metatheorem, three join species.} The
common structure across the conditioned instances is a theorem rather
than a convention. The developments all ran the same induction ---
thread a per-configuration honesty contract through reachability, invoke
a Join Lemma at each merge --- and that induction is
extracted once (\lean{Metatheory/HonestReach.lean}), as the next
definition and theorem; the queue's and the bounded counter's bespoke
inductions are one-line corollaries of the extracted form.

\begin{definition}[honest reachability]
\label{def:honestreach}
Fix a per-configuration contract $H$ and the hypothesis $\Inv(\Sinit)$
(displayed here and in every consumer below; flat data types discharge
it trivially). $\mathsf{HonestReach}\ \M{D}\ H$ is the least predicate
on configurations containing the initial configuration
(Definition~\ref{def:step3}) and closed under: if $C$ is honestly
reachable, $H(C)$ holds, and $C \xrightarrow{\ell} C'$ is a
$\mathsf{Step3}$ transition, then $C'$ is honestly reachable. The
contract is checked at the \emph{pre-step} configuration.
\hfill\lean{HonestReach}
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[the factored metatheorem]
\label{thm:honestreach}
Let $H$ be a contract such that every $H$-configuration admits the Join
at its core: for all $C'$, $H(C')$ implies
$\mathsf{JoinLemma3At}\ \M{D}\ C'$ (Definition~\ref{def:join}). Then,
with $\Inv(\Sinit)$, every $H$-honestly reachable configuration is
RA-linearizable, per version. Axioms: \lean{propext}, \lean{Quot.sound}.
Plain reachability is the degenerate contract $H = \top$
(\lean{honestReach\_of\_reachable}), recovering the unconditional
bridge.
\hfill\lean{ra\_linearizable3\_of\_honest\_reach}
\end{theorem}

What does \emph{not} factorize is the join discharge, and that is the
finding: the instances discharge \lean{JoinLemma3At} by
genuinely different mechanisms --- \emph{conditioned commutation}
(operations commute on invariant-satisfying states, the framework's
fully general quotiented route), \emph{flat
commutation} (the bounded counter: the CD route, contract $\top$), and
\emph{direct witness} (the queue: the merge is its own linearization
certificate; Part~II's embedded-chain family discharges the same way).
Conditioning thus splits as
\[
  \text{generic honest-reachability metatheorem}
  \;\times\;
  \text{per-datatype join discharge},
\]
and the framework's job at the boundary is to \emph{receive} a join, not
to produce one. A witness-classed generalization
(\lean{JoinLemma3AtW}, \lean{Metatheory/WitnessClass.lean}, from the
Shesha investigation of Part~III) extends the hook to datatypes whose
canonical states are unique only per witness-classed enumerations; and
the instance layer is payload-generic where it should be (the embed
instance of Part~II takes its element type as a defaulted parameter, so
the fused Peritext is a pure instantiation). One further regularity is
worth recording: the production honesty contracts all have the shape
``$P$ holds of each event at the fold of
its causal past'' --- anchor liveness for the sequence family, slack for
the counter, head-ness for the queue --- with $P$ the
signature's client-checkable $\app$ (or its accuracy analogue). That
shape is extracted once (\lean{Metatheory/GenHonest.lean}):

\begin{definition}[the generic honesty shape]
\label{def:genhonest}
For a predicate $P$ on (event, state) pairs,
\[
\begin{aligned}
  \mathsf{GenHonest}\ \M{D}\ P\ C \;:\Longleftrightarrow\;
  \forall e \in E(C).\;\; \forall \pi.\;\;
  &\pi \text{ enumerates } \{e' \in E(C) : e' \vis e\}\\
  &\;\Longrightarrow\; P\bigl(e,\, \fold(\Sinit, \pi)\bigr) ,
\end{aligned}
\]
$P$ of every event at the fold of \emph{every} enumeration of its causal
past. $\mathsf{AppHonest}\ \M{D} \coloneqq
\mathsf{GenHonest}\ \M{D}\ \app$ is the canonical instance. The
companion side condition $\mathsf{CausalPastEnumerable}$ (every event's
causal past admits an enumeration) holds in reachable configurations,
whose event sets are finite, but is kept as an explicit hypothesis where
consumed: the repository carries no generic finiteness result for
reachable event sets.
\hfill\lean{GenHonest}, \lean{AppHonest}
\end{definition}

\begin{remark}[two honesty names: $\forall$ versus $\exists$]
\label{rem:honestnames}
The mechanization carries two near-collision names, and both are load-bearing.
\lean{AppHonest} (\lean{Metatheory/GenHonest.lean}) is the $\forall$-form above:
$\app$ at the fold of \emph{every} enumeration of the causal past.
\lean{HonestApp} (\lean{Metatheory/GenericSafety.lean},
Definition~\ref{def:honestapp} below) is the $\exists$-form: $\app$ at
\emph{some causal} fold of the causal past, the one the issuing replica
materialized. The $\forall$-form feeds convergence contracts, whose guards are
fold-order-insensitive; the $\exists$-form feeds the safety metatheorem, because for
order-sensitive datatypes the $\forall$-form is unsatisfiable once a past holds two
surviving candidates (different enumerations materialize different heads).
\end{remark}

\begin{theorem}[the composite generic-honesty metatheorem]
\label{thm:genhonest}
For any $P$: if every $(\mathsf{GenHonest}\ \M{D}\ P)$-configuration
admits the Join at its core, then, with $\Inv(\Sinit)$, every
configuration honestly reachable under the contract
$\mathsf{GenHonest}\ \M{D}\ P$ is RA-linearizable, per version.
\hfill\lean{ra\_linearizable3\_of\_genHonest\_reach}
\end{theorem}

The counter's and the queue's contracts are re-derived as instantiations
(\lean{BCHonest\_iff\_genHonest}, \lean{qHonest\_of\_genHonest}). A generic \emph{safety}
metatheorem completes the factorization; it is the subject of a
paragraph below, and the counter's counting argument indeed turned out
to mark where the per-datatype content sits.

\paragraph{The flagship direct-join instance: the mergeable queue ---
when no $\rcrel$ can exist.} The queue of the certified-MRDT line of work
(Soundarapandian et al., PLDI'22, \emph{Certified Mergeable Replicated Data
Types}) is the catalogue's first data type whose conflict graph is a
\emph{clique} rather than a star: two concurrent enqueues genuinely do not
commute (queue order is arrival order), so VC~1's iff forces an $\rcrel$-edge
between them, and any orientation of a self-conflicting class composes with
itself into a length-2 path --- every candidate assignment violates
\lean{no\_rc\_chain} (Open Question~\ref{oq:rcchain}). The flat engine is
structurally unavailable, and the instance goes through the framework's
per-configuration join hook instead (\lean{JoinLemma3At},
\lean{goodConfig3\_merge\_at}): prove the Join Lemma directly, at every
reachable configuration.

The implementation is the functional queue with Peepul's merge, verbatim:
the state is a list of $(\mathit{tag}, \mathit{value})$ pairs, head first;
$\mathsf{enq}\,v$ appends a fresh element tagged with the operation's own
timestamp; $\mathsf{deq}\,t$ removes the element tagged $t$, wherever it
sits; and the three-way merge keeps the LCA's elements that survive in both
branches (in LCA order), then each branch's new arrivals (in branch order).
The client-facing API is unchanged --- $\mathsf{dequeue}()$ at a replica
reads its local head --- but the \emph{operation} records which element that
call removed: $\app$ for $\mathsf{deq}\,t$ is ``$t$ is the head of the
issuing replica's state''
($\mathsf{qApplicable}(\deq\,t, \st) :\Leftrightarrow \exists v.\;
\mathsf{head?}(\st) = (t, v)$; enqueues are unconditional). As with the
sequence family, the generation discipline is the datatype's natural
client behaviour, captured rather than imposed.

\begin{definition}[the queue's honesty contract]
\label{def:qhonest}
$\mathsf{QHonest}(C)$: every dequeue names a tag its issuer had observed as an
enqueue,
\[
  \forall e \in E(C).\;\; \mathsf{op}(e) = \deq\,t \;\Longrightarrow\;
  \exists a \in E(C).\;\; a \vis e \;\wedge\; \mathsf{time}(a) = t
  \;\wedge\; \exists v.\;\; \mathsf{op}(a) = \enq\,v .
\]
The same clause read on the replica-keyed core is \lean{QHonestCore}; the ternary
form transfers to it (\lean{qHonest\_core}).
\hfill\lean{QHonest}
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[the queue's join, at every honest configuration]
\label{thm:qjoin}
At every core configuration satisfying $\mathsf{QHonest}$, the ternary Join Lemma
holds: $\mathsf{JoinLemma3At}\ Q\ C$.
\hfill\lean{q\_join\_at}
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[the queue capstone]
\label{thm:queue}
Every configuration honestly reachable under $\mathsf{QHonest}$ (the
instance $\mathsf{QReach} \coloneqq \mathsf{HonestReach}\ Q\
\mathsf{QHonest}$ of Definition~\ref{def:honestreach}, with
$\Inv(\Sinit)$ trivial) is RA-linearizable, per version.
\hfill\lean{queue\_ra\_linearizable3}
\end{theorem}

The proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:qjoin} exhibits Peepul's merge as its own
certificate. The Join Lemma
asks for a delivery-respecting enumeration of $E_1 \cup E_2$ whose fold is
$\mathsf{mergeL}(\sigma_0, \sigma_1, \sigma_2)$; the witness is the
concatenation $\rho_0 \cdot (\rho_1 \setminus E_0) \cdot (\rho_2 \setminus
E_0)$ of the given enumerations --- exactly the merge's shape, no
reordering. Three facts make the fold of that concatenation compute the
merge, all consequences of the closure premise (branch event sets are
backward-closed under $\vis$ among non-commuting pairs), timestamp
uniqueness, and the honesty contract (discharged by the $\app$ head-check via
\lean{qHonest\_of\_applicable}: applicability at \emph{some} fold of the
issuer's causal past suffices, the $\exists$-form of
Remark~\ref{rem:honestnames}): a fold from the empty queue is ``the
enqueues, in order, minus the dequeued tags'' (the fold formula, which
needs honesty --- a dequeue preceding its enqueue would consume nothing); a
delta's tags are fresh with respect to the LCA (uniqueness); and a
cross-branch dequeue is impossible --- a $\mathsf{deq}\,t$ in one branch
pulls its enqueue into that branch by closure, so if the enqueue of $t$ is
the \emph{other} branch's delta, it would lie in both branches, hence in
the LCA, contradicting delta-ness. An LCA element then survives the union
iff it survives in both branches, which is precisely the merge's first
clause.

The payoff is the specification. In the PLDI'22 development the queue was
the hardest case exactly because its specification had to be written in a
bespoke relational language over event partial orders, with an explicit
clause recovering \emph{which element is the head}; the merge was then
verified against that ad-hoc spec. Here the specification is the same
generic statement as every other instance in
Table~\ref{tab:catalog} --- every version is the fold of a
delivery-respecting linearization of its event set --- and head-ness,
FIFO order, and once-per-element consumption are \emph{read off} the fold
rather than specified. (One semantic caveat, present equally in Peepul:
two replicas that concurrently dequeue the same head both consume that one
element --- the merged state removes it once. RA-linearizability makes this
visible instead of hiding it: both $\mathsf{deq}\,t$ events fold over the
single $\mathsf{enq}\,t$.) At ${\sim}870$ lines
(\lean{MRDT\_Instances/MergeableQueue/}), the instance carries positional
structure at a fraction of a sequence datatype's cost.

\paragraph{Conditioning for safety: the bounded counter.} Conditioning
first entered the framework to deliver \emph{convergence} for
state-dependent datatypes. The bounded counter (per-replica escrow:
grow-only tallies $(\mathsf{incs}, \mathsf{decs})$ per replica, per-slot
group merge; mirror of \lean{Sal/CRDTs/Bounded\_Counter}) shows the same
mechanisms delivering \emph{safety} for a datatype whose convergence is
flat: all its operations commute, the eight VCs discharge along the
counter route, and the catalogue capstone
(\lean{BC\_ra\_linearizable3\_eq}) is an identity instantiation. What the
flat theory cannot even state is the property the datatype is named
after. Its CRDT development is explicit: ``the bound is enforced
operationally by well-behaved clients'' --- a client refuses to issue
$\mathsf{Dec}$ without slack --- and that discipline lives outside the
formal development. Conditioned, it moves inside: $\Inv$ is the
per-replica escrow invariant
($0 \le \mathsf{decs}\,r \le \mathsf{incs}\,r$), $\app$ is
the client's check (a $\mathsf{Dec}$ needs slack in the \emph{issuing
replica's own} slots --- positive, and checkable against the issuing
replica's state), and the contract on executions is the counter's
honest-delivery contract:

\begin{definition}[the counter's honesty contract]
\label{def:bchonest}
$\mathsf{BCHonest}(C)$: every decrement was applicable at the fold of its causal
past,
\[
  \forall e \in E(C).\;\; \mathsf{op}(e) = \mathsf{dec} \;\Longrightarrow\;
  \forall \pi.\;\; \pi \text{ enumerates } \{e' \in E(C) : e' \vis e\}
  \;\Longrightarrow\; \app\bigl(e, \fold(\Sinit, \pi)\bigr),
\]
with $\app(\mathsf{dec} \text{ by } r,\, \st)$ the issuer's own-slot slack check
$\mathsf{decs}_r(\st) + 1 \le \mathsf{incs}_r(\st)$. This is exactly the generic shape
of Definition~\ref{def:genhonest} at $P \coloneqq \app$: the $\mathsf{dec}$-guard is
immaterial because $\app$ is $\top$ on increments
(\lean{BCHonest\_iff\_genHonest}).
\hfill\lean{BCHonest}
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[the bound, per version]
\label{thm:bcinv}
In every configuration reachable in the plain LTS (from the initial configuration,
$\Inv(\Sinit)$ trivial) whose history satisfies $\mathsf{BCHonest}$, every
registered version satisfies the escrow invariant: $\mathsf{ver}(v) = (s, E)$
implies $0 \le \mathsf{decs}_r(s) \le \mathsf{incs}_r(s)$ for every replica $r$
(heads, LCAs, everything the store registered). Hence, for every finite replica list
$rs$, the readable value is non-negative:
$0 \le \sum_{r \in rs}\bigl(\mathsf{incs}_r(s) - \mathsf{decs}_r(s)\bigr)$
(\lean{bc\_value\_nonneg}).
\hfill\lean{bc\_version\_inv}
\end{theorem}

What the CRDT development could only promise operationally is here a consequence of
the formal client contract. The original proof is a
counting argument riding the flat machinery: every version's state is a fold
of its event set (canonicity), fold slots \emph{are} per-replica event
counts (the group merge is inclusion--exclusion on counts, via the LCA
lemma), and the vis-maximal decrement's honesty at its own causal past ---
which lies inside the version by causal closure --- bounds the decrement
count by the increment count. At ${\sim}600$ lines, it is also a
calibration point for which conditioning machinery is generic: the same
invariant-at-generation shape, none of the positional structure.

\paragraph{The generic safety metatheorem: causal witnesses, and an
escrow route.} The safety half of conditioning also factorizes, but the
obvious formulation is \emph{false}, and the refutation comes from the
flagship safety instance itself. One would like: if updates preserve
$\Inv$ on applicable operations and the history is honest, then $\Inv$
holds at every version --- proved by inducting along the version's
canonical enumeration. But canonicity constrains the enumeration only by
$\mathsf{lo}$, and for the bounded counter --- all of whose operations
commute --- $\mathsf{lo}$ is \emph{empty}: $[\mathsf{dec}, \mathsf{inc}]$
is a perfectly canonical enumeration of an honest version, and its
intermediate state violates the escrow invariant. Only \emph{endpoints}
of canonical folds are guaranteed; commuting causal predecessors may
float behind the event that needs them. The repair is to restrict the
witness class, not the induction:

\begin{definition}[causal folds; causal canonical witnesses]
\label{def:causalcanon}
$\sigma$ is a \emph{causal fold} of $E$ in $C$ (\lean{CausalFold}) when some
enumeration $\rho$ of $E$ respecting $\vis$ folds to it:
$\fold(\Sinit, \rho) = \sigma$. Prefixes of such an enumeration are exactly the
vis-backward-closed subsets of $E$. $C$ is \emph{causally canonical} when every
registered version carries a witness linearizing both orders:
$\mathsf{ver}(v) = (s, E)$ implies some enumeration $\rho$ of $E$ respects $\vis$
\emph{and} respects $\loE{E}$ and folds to $s$, strictly refining clause 1 of
Definition~\ref{def:good}.
\hfill\lean{CausalCanonical}
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[$\exists$-form honesty]
\label{def:honestapp}
For a guard $A$ on (event, state) pairs, $\mathsf{HonestAppOn}\ \M{D}\ A\ C$: every
event satisfies $A$ at \emph{some} causal fold of its causal past,
\[
  \forall e \in E(C).\;\; \exists \sigma.\;\;
  \sigma \text{ a causal fold of } \{e' \in E(C) : e' \vis e\} \text{ in } C
  \;\wedge\; A(e, \sigma) .
\]
$\mathsf{HonestApp}\ \M{D}$ is the instance $A \coloneqq \app$: what the issuer's own
materialized state witnesses. Being an $\exists$-statement it carries its own
enumeration witness, so no enumerability side condition is owed; the contrast with the
$\forall$-form \lean{AppHonest} is Remark~\ref{rem:honestnames}.
\hfill\lean{HonestApp}
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[the per-datatype safety obligation]
\label{def:safetystep}
$\mathsf{SafetyStepOn}\ \M{D}\ I\ A$, over an explicit conditioning pair $(I, A)$:
for every configuration $C$, event sets $E, S$, event $e$, and states
$\sigma_S, \sigma_P$ with
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=2.2em]
\item[(i)] $E \subseteq E(C)$; \quad (ii) $E$ vis-closed ($a \vis b$, $b \in E$
  imply $a \in E$); \quad (iii) $e \in E$;
\item[(iv)] $S \subseteq E$; \quad (v) $e \notin S$; \quad (vi) $S$ vis-closed;
\item[(vii)] $S$ future-free for $e$: $x \in S$ implies $\neg\, e \vis x$; \quad
  (viii) $\mathrm{past}(e) \subseteq S$: $x \vis e$ implies $x \in S$;
\item[(ix)] $\sigma_S$ a causal fold of $S$; \quad (x) $\sigma_P$ a causal fold of
  $\mathrm{past}(e) = \{e' \in E(C) : e' \vis e\}$,
\end{itemize}
it holds that $I(\sigma_S) \wedge A(e, \sigma_P) \Rightarrow
I(\dodo(\sigma_S, e))$: applicability transfers from the causal past to any
admissible prefix, fused with $I$-preservation so unconditionally-safe operations owe
no guard. $\mathsf{SafetyStep}\ \M{D}$ is the instance $(I, A) \coloneqq
(\Inv, \app)$.
\hfill\lean{SafetyStep}
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[generic safety, causal-witness form]
\label{thm:gensafety}
If $\Inv(\Sinit)$, $\mathsf{SafetyStep}\ \M{D}$, and $C$ satisfies the
good-configuration invariant (Definition~\ref{def:good}), is causally canonical, and
satisfies $\mathsf{HonestApp}\ \M{D}$, then every registered version state satisfies
$\Inv$. Axioms: \lean{propext}, \lean{Quot.sound}.
\hfill\lean{version\_inv\_of\_causal\_canonical}
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[the causal witness is free for all-comm datatypes]
\label{thm:ccfree}
If all operation pairs commute and $\rcrel \equiv \Either$, then every configuration
satisfying Definition~\ref{def:good} is causally canonical: a pointwise permutation
argument, no reachability induction.
\hfill\lean{causalCanonical\_of\_all\_comm\_rc\_either}
\end{theorem}

The bounded counter discharges \lean{SafetyStep}
in three lines --- extras in a prefix are other replicas' events and
cannot touch the issuer's slots (\lean{bc\_safetyStep}) --- and
Theorem~\ref{thm:bcinv} is
re-derived as a corollary, retiring the bespoke vis-maximal counting
apparatus, which is thereby revealed as compensation for inducting over
non-causal witnesses. A second, independent route generalizes that
retired argument itself, with no causal witness and no
$\Inv$-preservation at all:

\begin{definition}[measured datatypes]
\label{def:measured}
$\M{D}$ is \emph{measured} over an index type $\kappa$ when it carries observations
$\mathsf{obs}_k : \Sigma \to \mathbb{Z}$ and per-operation weights
$\mu_k : \M{E} \to \mathbb{N}$ with $\mathsf{obs}_k(\Sinit) = 0$ and the affine update
law $\mathsf{obs}_k(\dodo(\st, e)) = \mathsf{obs}_k(\st) + \mu_k(e)$. Folds of
measured observations compute set measures, so enumeration-independence is free.
\hfill\lean{Measured}
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[the escrow metatheorem]
\label{thm:escrow}
Let $\M{D}$ be measured, $k_c$ (consuming) and $k_f$ (funding) indices, and $A$ a
guard, with
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=2.2em]
\item[(i)] $\mu_{k_c}(e) \le 1$ for every event $e$;
\item[(ii)] the guard supplies slack: $\mu_{k_c}(e) = 1$ and $A(e, \sigma)$ imply
  $\mathsf{obs}_{k_c}(\sigma) + 1 \le \mathsf{obs}_{k_f}(\sigma)$;
\item[(iii)] $C$ satisfies the good-configuration invariant
  (Definition~\ref{def:good});
\item[(iv)] consumption is serial: distinct events $a, b \in E(C)$ with
  $\mu_{k_c}(a) = \mu_{k_c}(b) = 1$ are $\vis$-comparable (the counter obtains this
  from same-replica totality, per slot);
\item[(v)] $\mathsf{GenHonest}\ \M{D}\ A\ C$ (the $\forall$-form: measured guards are
  fold-order-insensitive).
\end{itemize}
Then every registered version state $s$ satisfies
$0 \le \mathsf{obs}_{k_c}(s) \le \mathsf{obs}_{k_f}(s)$.
\hfill\lean{escrow\_version\_inv}
\end{theorem}

Theorem~\ref{thm:bcinv} is derived a second time this way. The queue,
by contrast, \emph{refuses} \lean{SafetyStep} --- ``$t$ is the head'' is
not stable under a concurrent honest dequeue of the same head, and
correctly so: the double-dequeue execution satisfies any sound
obligation, and the queue's stable residue is event-level
(\lean{QHonest}), consumed by convergence rather than by a state
invariant. The full analysis, including two further refuted
formulations, is the pen-and-paper memo
\lean{Development/GENERIC\_SAFETY\_PENPAPER.md} --- written, per this
project's standing lesson, before any Lean. (A natural follow-up is a
\emph{conditioned step relation} --- apply guarded by $\app$ at the head
state --- under which honesty becomes a theorem of the LTS rather than a
contract; deferred.)

\paragraph{A small fourth instance: the FWW reservation register --- payload
arbitration, and what honesty cannot buy.} A register claimable once (a
seat, a username): sequentially the first claim wins; concurrently, the
winner is the claim that is first in \emph{Lamport} time. This is the
positive complement to the metadata-free kill-test
(Proposition~\ref{prop:lwwmeta} below): arbitration whose key is not in the
state is impossible, and arbitration whose key \emph{is} in the state is a
semilattice triviality --- the state is the minimum claim
$(\mathsf{ts}, \mathsf{rep}, v)$ under the lexicographic order (or unset),
update and merge are $\min$, and the entire eight-VC discharge is
$\min$-algebra (${\sim}300$ lines, the smallest conditioned instance).

\begin{theorem}[the FWW winner, per version]
\label{thm:fww}
In every configuration reachable in the plain LTS ($\Inv(\Sinit)$ trivial), every
registered version $\mathsf{ver}(v) = (s, E)$ satisfies
\[
  \bigl(\forall e \in E.\;\; s \le \mathrm{claim}(e)\bigr)
  \;\wedge\; \bigl(s = \top \Leftrightarrow E = \emptyset\bigr)
  \;\wedge\; \bigl(\forall m.\;\; s = m \Rightarrow \exists e \in E.\;\;
  \mathrm{claim}(e) = m\bigr):
\]
the register holds exactly the minimum-timestamp claim of its event set (a lower
bound, attained), unset exactly on the empty set. No honesty hypothesis: semilattice
folds are enumeration-independent.
\hfill\lean{fww\_version\_min}
\end{theorem}

\begin{proposition}[metadata-free arbitration is impossible]
\label{prop:lwwmeta}
Let $\mathrm{canon}(\mathit{init}, L)$ be the LWW specification value of
a finite write history $L$ from initial value $\mathit{init}$ (the value
of the timestamp-maximal write, $\mathit{init}$ if $L$ is empty). There
is no function $m : \mathrm{value}^3 \to \mathrm{value}$ with
\[
\begin{aligned}
  &m\bigl(\mathrm{canon}(\mathit{init}, L),\;
  \mathrm{canon}(\mathit{init}, L \mathbin{+\!\!+} \Delta_a),\;
  \mathrm{canon}(\mathit{init}, L \mathbin{+\!\!+} \Delta_b)\bigr) \\
  &\qquad=\; \mathrm{canon}(\mathit{init}, L \mathbin{+\!\!+} \Delta_a
  \mathbin{+\!\!+} \Delta_b)
  \qquad \text{for all } \mathit{init}, L, \Delta_a, \Delta_b :
\end{aligned}
\]
two executions present the identical value triple $(10, 20, 30)$ with opposite stamp
orders, forcing $m(10,20,30) = 30$ and $m(10,20,30) = 20$. The timestamp is forced
into the state by the \emph{merge}, not by replay order.
\hfill\lean{lww\_merge\_needs\_timestamps}
\end{proposition}

Since timestamps respect causality, a causally later claim never displaces
an installed one; the $\min$ rule arbitrates only among \emph{concurrent}
claimants, and it must, on pain of divergence. The instructive part is the
generation discipline: $\app$ for a claim is ``the register is unset in my
causal past'', and \emph{deliberately no theorem consumes it} --- two
honest concurrent claimants both see unset, both claim, and each locally
wins until a merge disabuses one. ``Unset'' is the minimal example of an
applicability check that is \emph{not stable under concurrent honest
extension} (contrast the escrow counter's own-slot slack, which is ---
precisely the boundary the safety metatheorem's \lean{SafetyStep} draws).
A merge-based register is a reservation, confirmed only at causal
stability; it is never a mutex.

\paragraph{The join hook is also the composition boundary.} The factored
interface earns one more dividend: data types compose \emph{at the
per-configuration join}, and only there. Finished capstones never
compose, because reachability does not project (a second-component apply
is a version-duplicating stutter for the first component's projection);
per-configuration certificates do, which retroactively forces the
interface of this section. The binary heterogeneous product
(\lean{Metatheory/Product.lean}) makes this a theorem pair: cross-component
pairs commute definitionally, so $\loE{E}$ has no mixed edges and the
glued join witness is a plain concatenation of the component witnesses,
with no interleaving combinatorics.

\begin{theorem}[the product join gluing]
\label{thm:prodjoin}
Let $\M{D}_1 \otimes \M{D}_2$ be the binary heterogeneous product
(\lean{prodSig}): state $\Sigma_1 \times \Sigma_2$, operations the sum
$O_1 \oplus O_2$, with $\dodo$, $\mergeL$, $\Inv$, and $\app$
componentwise, and $\rcrel$ per component on same-side pairs and
$\Either$ on mixed pairs (forced: cross pairs commute, so any mixed
$\mathsf{Fst\_then\_snd}$ would violate Flat VC~\ref{fvc:rcnc}). For any
product configuration $C$: if $\mathsf{JoinLemma3At}\ \M{D}_1$ holds at
the first core projection of $C$ and $\mathsf{JoinLemma3At}\ \M{D}_2$ at
the second, then $\mathsf{JoinLemma3At}\ (\M{D}_1 \otimes \M{D}_2)\ C$.
\hfill\lean{joinLemma3At\_prod}
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[the composite product metatheorem]
\label{thm:prodmeta}
Let $H_1, H_2$ be component contracts and
$H_\otimes(C) \coloneqq H_1(\pi_1\, C) \wedge H_2(\pi_2\, C)$ the product
contract over the core projections (\lean{prodContract}). If every
$H_1$-configuration admits $\M{D}_1$'s join and every $H_2$-configuration
admits $\M{D}_2$'s, then, with the product's $\Inv(\Sinit)$ (both
components'), every $H_\otimes$-honestly reachable product configuration
is RA-linearizable, per version. Axioms: \lean{propext},
\lean{Quot.sound}.
\hfill\lean{prod\_ra\_linearizable3\_of\_honest\_reach}
\end{theorem}

The component certificates are consumed at the \emph{projections} of
product-reachable cores, which is exactly why they must be
configuration-level, not reachability-indexed. A queue $\otimes$ counter
capstone with zero bespoke proof (\lean{MRDT\_Instances/ProductDemo/})
demonstrates consumability; the safety and $\eqobs$-quotient kits, the
boundary findings, and what remains open are collected in Open
Question~\ref{oq:compose}.

\section{Virtual LCAs: merging without a common-ancestor version}
\label{sec:vlca}

The transition system of \S\ref{sec:setting} gates Merge on the
existence of an LCA: a version $v_\ell$ that reaches both heads and
dominates every common ancestor. In a \emph{criss-cross} configuration
the common ancestors of two heads have two or more maximal elements, no
version satisfies the predicate, and Merge is disabled. This is not an
exotic corner. The shape's routine genesis is two replicas each merging
the \emph{same} pair of diverged heads: both create a version with the
same event set, neither reaches the other, and every later pair of
descendants has both rivals as maximal common ancestors
(Figure~\ref{fig:crisscross}). The JS runtime built on this model makes
the gate explicit, and its randomized testing shows how routine the
shape is under honest peer-to-peer syncing: 592 gated sync attempts in
220 randomized trials. The gap is exactly where git reaches for its
\emph{recursive} merge strategy: merge the maximal common ancestors
into a virtual base, then merge the heads against that. This section
develops that extension inside the metatheory and reports what it
costs: nothing, for join-lemma datatypes.

\begin{figure}[t]
\centering
\begin{tikzpicture}[>=Stealth, node distance=0.5cm and 0.9cm,
  ver/.style={draw, rounded corners, inner sep=3pt, font=\small},
  lca/.style={draw, rounded corners, inner sep=3pt, font=\small,
    fill=blue!8},
  lbl/.style={font=\scriptsize, text=black!60}]
\node[ver] (v0) at (0,0) {$v_0:\ \varnothing$};
\node[lca] (v1) at (-2.0,-1.3) {$v_1:\ \{x\}$};
\node[lca] (v2) at ( 2.0,-1.3) {$v_2:\ \{y\}$};
\node[ver] (v3) at (-2.0,-2.6) {$v_3:\ \{x,y\}$};
\node[ver] (v4) at ( 2.0,-2.6) {$v_4:\ \{x,y\}$};
\node[ver] (v5) at (-2.0,-3.9) {$v_5:\ \{x,y,\del_x\}$};
\node[ver] (v6) at ( 2.0,-3.9) {$v_6:\ \{x,y,\del_y\}$};
\draw[->] (v0) -- (v1); \draw[->] (v0) -- (v2);
\draw[->] (v1) -- (v3); \draw[->] (v2) -- (v3);
\draw[->] (v1) -- (v4); \draw[->] (v2) -- (v4);
\draw[->] (v3) -- (v5); \draw[->] (v4) -- (v6);
\node[lbl, right=0.15cm of v4, align=left]
  {rival merges of the\\ same head pair};
\node[lbl, below=0.12cm of v5] {head $p$};
\node[lbl, below=0.12cm of v6] {head $q$};
\end{tikzpicture}
\caption{The criss-cross store used for the machine pins (the store of
\lean{VirtualLCA\_Spot.lean}, drawn top-down; edges parent to child).
Replicas $p$ and $q$ concurrently add $x$ and $y$; each then merges the
same diverged pair $(v_1, v_2)$, creating the rivals $v_3, v_4$ with
equal event sets; each deletes one element. The common ancestors of the
heads $v_5, v_6$ are $\{v_0, v_1, v_2\}$ with \emph{two} maximal
elements $v_1, v_2$: no version is an LCA, and the gated Merge cannot
fire. Note $E(v_1) \cup E(v_2) = \{x, y\} = E(v_5) \cap E(v_6)$: the
antichain's union is exactly the meet, which is
Proposition~\ref{prop:cover}.}
\label{fig:crisscross}
\end{figure}

\paragraph{The rule.} Write $\mathrm{MCA}(v_1, v_2)$ for the maximal
common ancestors of two versions (maximal in the reachability order,
which is a partial order because a version's identifier is its
allocation rank). $\mathsf{VirtualLCA}(v_1, v_2)$:
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=2em]
\item $M := \mathrm{MCA}(v_1, v_2)$. It is nonempty (the pinned root is
  a common ancestor), and when the heads are incomparable every member
  is a strict ancestor of both.
\item If $M = \{m\}$: return $m$. This is the existing rule, and
  Lemma~\ref{lem:lca} applies unchanged.
\item Otherwise sort $M$ ascending by rank (deterministic: version ids
  are allocation ranks) as $m_1 < \cdots < m_k$ and fold: start with
  $m_1$; at each step, merge the accumulator with the next member
  $m_i$ through the recursively resolved LCA of the sub-pair,
  materializing a \emph{scratch node} whose state is that ternary merge
  and whose event set is the union so far. Return the final
  accumulator.
\end{itemize}
The head merge then runs the ordinary ternary merge with the returned
state in the LCA slot. The scratch nodes exist so the nested MCA
queries are well posed; nothing references them afterwards, so they are
never ancestors of real heads and cannot perturb later queries.
Termination is unconditional: measuring a call on $(a,b)$ by the number
of allocated versions in the pair's joint ancestry, every version in a
sub-pair's support is a common ancestor of the original heads while
$v_1$ itself is not, so the measure strictly drops at each nesting
level, and within a level the fold index drops. In the mechanization
the extension lands as a conservative \emph{new} step relation
\lean{Step3V}, with a base case embedding every old step and
\lean{mergeVirtual} the one new case; widening \lean{Step3} in place
was tried and rejected, because it breaks every exhaustive case
analysis over the old relation in instance files. Only the final
virtual node is allocated; the store invariant extends across the new
step. The next three definitions pin the rule formally.

\begin{definition}[common ancestors and MCAs, set-support form]
\label{def:mca}
The recursion pairs a scratch node, identified with its finite real support $S$,
against a version $w$; $S = \{v_1\}$ recovers the head pair. Over the parent
relation,
\[
  \mathrm{CommonAnc}(S, w) \;=\;
  \{\, x \;:\; (\exists u \in S.\; \mathsf{Reaches}(x, u)) \,\wedge\,
  \mathsf{Reaches}(x, w) \,\},
\]
and $m$ is a \emph{maximal common ancestor} ($\mathsf{IsMCA}$) of $(S, w)$ when
$m \in \mathrm{CommonAnc}(S, w)$ and every $x \in \mathrm{CommonAnc}(S, w)$ with
$\mathsf{Reaches}(m, x)$ equals $m$. The antichain is materialized as a finite set
(\lean{mcaFinset}; ranks are bounded by $w$, so the bound is intrinsic), and LCAs are
exactly the singleton case (\lean{isMCA\_singleton\_of\_isLCA} and its converse).
\hfill\lean{CommonAnc}, \lean{IsMCA}
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[the virtual-LCA state]
\label{def:vlca}
Write $\sigma_v$ for the state registered at $v$, defaulting to $\Sinit$
(\lean{stateD}; the canonicity theorems carry the allocation hypotheses that make
the default unreachable). Sort $\mathrm{MCA}(S, w)$ ascending by rank as
$m_1 < \cdots < m_k$; then the recursion of the rule above is
(\lean{vlcaAux}, \lean{vfoldAux}):
\begin{align*}
  \mathrm{vlca}(S, w) &=
  \begin{cases}
    \Sinit & k = 0\\
    \mathrm{vfold}(\{m_1\},\, \sigma_{m_1},\, [m_2, \dots, m_k]) & k \ge 1
  \end{cases}\\
  \mathrm{vfold}(A, \mathit{acc}, [\,]) &= \mathit{acc}\\
  \mathrm{vfold}(A, \mathit{acc}, m :: ms) &=
  \mathrm{vfold}\bigl(A \cup \{m\},\;
  \mergeL(\mathrm{vlca}(A, m),\, \mathit{acc},\, \sigma_m),\; ms\bigr),
\end{align*}
and $\mathsf{VirtualLCA}(v_1, v_2) \coloneqq \mathrm{vlca}(\{v_1\}, v_2)$.
The empty-antichain base is not a junk case: under the store invariant
its event set, the empty union, is exactly the then-empty meet.
Noncomputable but total, by the rank measure of the termination argument above.
\hfill\lean{virtualLCAState}
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[the widened transition system]
\label{def:step3v}
$\mathsf{Step3V}$ embeds every $\mathsf{Step3}$ rule
(Definition~\ref{def:step3}) through a base constructor and adds one rule,
\textsc{MergeVirtual}$(r_1, r_2)$:
\[
\frac{\begin{array}{c}
  \mathsf{head}(r_1) = v_1 \quad \mathsf{head}(r_2) = v_2 \quad
  \mathsf{ver}(v_i) = (\sigma_i, E_i) \\
  \mathsf{ver}(v_m)\ \text{unallocated} \quad v_1 < v_m \quad v_2 < v_m
\end{array}}{\begin{array}{c}
  \mathsf{ver}(v_m) \coloneqq
  \bigl(\mergeL(\mathsf{VirtualLCA}(v_1, v_2),\, \sigma_1,\, \sigma_2),\;
  E_1 \cup E_2\bigr), \\
  \mathsf{parents}(v_m) \coloneqq [v_1, v_2],
  \quad \mathsf{head}(r_1) \coloneqq v_m
\end{array}}
\]
with visibility unchanged: this is \textsc{Merge} of Definition~\ref{def:step3} with the
$\mathsf{IsLCA}$ gate and its $\mathsf{ver}(v_\ell)$ read replaced by the recursive
antichain merge in the LCA slot; with a singleton antichain the virtual state
\emph{is} the registered LCA state, so the rule strictly generalizes
\textsc{Merge}. The widened LTS is \lean{labeledTS3V}.
\hfill\lean{Step3V}
\end{definition}

\paragraph{The covering proposition.} The Merge rule's semantic
requirement on the LCA slot is Lemma~\ref{lem:lca}: its event set must
be the head intersection. The virtual construction meets it exactly, on
the strength of invariants the store already carries.

\begin{proposition}[covering]
\label{prop:cover}
Let $\mathsf{ver}, \mathsf{parents}$ be a raw store with (i) the store
invariant of Definition~\ref{def:storeinv}, (ii) rank-decreasing parents
($p \in \mathsf{parents}(v)$ implies $p < v$), (iii) every $u \in S$
allocated, and (iv) $w$ allocated. Then, pointwise in the event $e$,
\[
  \bigl(\exists m.\;\; \mathsf{IsMCA}(S, w, m) \wedge e \in E(m)\bigr)
  \;\Longleftrightarrow\;
  \bigl(\exists u \in S.\;\; e \in E(u)\bigr) \wedge e \in E(w) ;
\]
by extensionality,
\[
  \bigcup_{m \in \mathrm{MCA}(S, w)} E(m)
  \;=\;
  \Bigl(\bigcup_{u \in S} E(u)\Bigr) \cap E(w).
\]
Neither finiteness nor nonemptiness of $S$ is assumed, and the degenerate
cases come for free: an empty MCA set forces an empty intersection (the
$\supseteq$ direction \emph{constructs} an MCA from a shared event's
origin), and a singleton MCA set recovers Lemma~\ref{lem:lca}.
\hfill\lean{mca\_events\_cover}
\end{proposition}

With $S = \{v_1\}$, $w = v_2$ this covers the head pair; with $S$ the
support of a scratch node it covers every sub-pair of the recursion;
with a singleton MCA it degenerates to Lemma~\ref{lem:lca}. The subset
direction is event-set monotonicity along reachability, twice. The
superset direction is where the store invariant earns its keep: an
event in both sides has, by the \emph{origin} invariant of
\S\ref{sec:lca} (each event appears first at a unique generator
version), a single birth version that reaches every version whose event
set contains it; that generator is a common ancestor, every common
ancestor reaches a maximal one, and monotonicity carries the event up
into it. Since the fold unions event sets, the returned virtual node
has event set $\bigcup_i E(m_i) = E(v_1) \cap E(v_2)$: exactly what the
Merge rule requires. An earlier forecast (recorded in the GC analysis,
\S\ref{sec:gc}) had guessed the union approximates the intersection
from below; under the carried invariants the approximation is
\emph{exact}, even though each antichain member individually sits
strictly below the meet.

One delimitation is worth stating, because it marks the model boundary
the proposition rests on. The origin invariant is load-bearing: in a
model that can apply the \emph{same} event at two incomparable versions
(op-based redelivery, with no store-wide timestamp freshness), the
event has two incomparable birth sites, neither a common ancestor, and
the union strictly undershoots the intersection. Countermodel: apply
$e$ independently at the root on both branches; then
$E(v_1) \cap E(v_2) = \{e\}$ while the sole MCA is the root with empty
event set. The transition system excludes this by store-wide timestamp
freshness (an op is born at exactly one version), so the covering
invariant is not a new obligation: it is \lean{StoreInv.origin} plus
monotonicity, already carried and maintained.

\paragraph{Canonicity: one fold induction, and the VC surface does not
move.} The adequacy induction of \S\ref{sec:adequacy} consumed two
facts about the LCA slot: its event set is the intersection (now
Proposition~\ref{prop:cover}) and its registered state is canonical for
that event set. The virtual construction re-supplies the second from
the one per-datatype hypothesis the framework already centers on, the
per-configuration Join Lemma:

\begin{claim}[virtual join]
\label{claim:vjoin}
Let $C$ satisfy (i) the store invariant on its ranked store
(Definition~\ref{def:storeinv}), (ii) the good-configuration invariant
(Definition~\ref{def:good}; in particular every allocated version's
state is canonical for its event set), and (iii) the per-configuration
Join Lemma $\mathsf{JoinLemma3At}$ at its core
(Definition~\ref{def:join}). Then, for any registered pair
$\mathsf{ver}(v_1) = (s_1, E_1)$, $\mathsf{ver}(v_2) = (s_2, E_2)$,
\[
  \mathrm{Can}_C\bigl(E_1 \cap E_2,\;\; \mathsf{VirtualLCA}(v_1, v_2)\bigr),
\]
and along the fold every scratch node's state is canonical for its union
event set.
\hfill\lean{virtualLCAState\_canonical}
\end{claim}

The proof is an induction over the ascending-rank fold: the antichain
members are allocated, hence canonical by hypothesis; at each step the
sub-pair's inner LCA slot is canonical by the inner recursion plus
Proposition~\ref{prop:cover}, and the Join Lemma yields canonicity at
the union. Backward closure is preserved by union and intersection, so
all intermediate sets stay in the hypothesis class; and canonicity is a
property of a (configuration, event set, state) triple, not of
allocated versions, so scratch states need no allocation to carry it.
The same induction instantiates at the witness-classed join
(\lean{virtualLCAState\_canonicalF}), and the merge case of the
honest-reachability metatheorem gains the sibling case:

\begin{theorem}[the widened metatheorem]
\label{thm:reachV}
Let $\mathsf{HonestReachV}$ be honest reachability
(Definition~\ref{def:honestreach}) with $\mathsf{Step3}$ replaced by
$\mathsf{Step3V}$ (Definition~\ref{def:step3v}). If every
$H$-configuration admits the Join at its core, then, with
$\Inv(\Sinit)$, every $H$-honestly reachable configuration of the
widened LTS is RA-linearizable, per version: the \emph{same} join
obligation. The induction gains one case
(\lean{goodConfig3\_mergeVirtual\_at}), whose extra store-invariant
hypothesis reachability itself supplies
(\lean{storeInv\_of\_honest\_reachV}).
\hfill\lean{ra\_linearizable3\_of\_honest\_reachV}
\end{theorem}

The headline is what did
\emph{not} change: \lean{Framework/VC\_Set.lean} is untouched. For any
datatype that discharges its join at every configuration, the criss-cross
gate lifts with \textbf{no new per-datatype verification condition};
the per-datatype VC surface does not move.

Two statement-hygiene facts, both machine-witnessed. First,
determinism: the fold order is fixed (ascending rank), and for
join-lemma datatypes order-insensitivity of the \emph{returned} state
is a theorem, since canonical states of a fixed event set are unique.
Git's own recursive strategy folds in commit-date order and is known to
be order-sensitive on conflicting merges; rank fold plus canonicity is
what removes that defect here. Second, the insensitivity claim must be
scoped to the result: on a directed three-member antichain the
\emph{intermediate} scratch states of the ascending and descending
folds genuinely differ (each is canonical for its own, different,
union), and only the final states coincide. Any mirror lemma stating
canonicity of intermediates across fold orders is false.

\paragraph{The single-MCA shortcut, machine-refuted.} The tempting
implementation shortcut (pick \emph{some} maximal common ancestor and
use it as the LCA; no recursion, no scratch nodes) is not a
simplification but a soundness bug, and the store of
Figure~\ref{fig:crisscross} pins it. Resolving the head pair
$(v_5, v_6)$ through the virtual base folds $v_1$ and $v_2$ over the
root into the state with both adds visible; the head merge then sees
each delete on its own side against a base that \emph{contains} the
deleted element, and both deletions survive: the merged state is empty,
the meet's fold. Picking the single MCA $v_1$ instead puts a base
without $y$ in the LCA slot, so $y$ on $v_6$'s side reads as a fresh
add and the deleted $y$ \emph{resurrects}; picking $v_2$ dually
resurrects $x$.

\begin{proposition}[every single-MCA pick resurrects a deleted element]
\label{prop:singlemca}
On the store of Figure~\ref{fig:crisscross}
(\lean{Metatheory/VirtualLCA\_Spot.lean}, a two-element OR-pair
datatype; $\mathrm{query}_y$ is the membership read of $y$, $\sigma_v$
the registered states, and the pair resolved is the heads
$(v_5, v_6)$):
\[
  \mathrm{query}_y\bigl(\mergeL(\sigma_{v_1}, \sigma_{v_5},
  \sigma_{v_6})\bigr) = \mathsf{true}
  \;\;\wedge\;\;
  \mathrm{query}_y\bigl(\mergeL(\mathsf{VirtualLCA}(v_5, v_6),
  \sigma_{v_5}, \sigma_{v_6})\bigr) = \mathsf{false} ,
\]
and dually the pick $v_2$ resurrects $x$
(\lean{t1f\_pick\_v\_resurrects\_x}); neither pick's merged state equals
the virtual resolution's (\lean{t1f\_picks\_ne}). The harness twin is
the \code{T1F} fail companion of \code{virtual\_lca\_check.py}.
\hfill\lean{t1f\_pick\_u\_resurrects\_y}
\end{proposition}

Every
single-MCA pick is wrong on this store; the recursion is not
dispensable. Nor is its depth boundable in practice: in a dense
randomized probe (200 trials, 6 replicas, sync-heavy), criss-crosses
arose in 176 of 200 trials with resolution nesting up to depth 7,
because rivalry propagates upward (rival joins of rival joins); a
depth-limited implementation would simply re-erect the gate.

\paragraph{The quotient layers, and the rehoming stress test.} The
claim above serves the raw layer. The framework's quotient layers
(the $\eqobs$-indexed contracts of \S\ref{sec:meta}) each thread their
own merge case, and their bill was probed on the hardest instance
available: the rehoming RGA, the one production datatype whose proof
route runs through an equivalence quotient with a
reachability-anchored honesty stack (its only role here; it is the
generality stress test, nothing more). Two findings. First, the
dividing line at antichain unions is exactly the per-event versus
prefix-state line: honesty components that speak about an event's
\emph{own} causal past (born accuracy, dependency-prefix correctness)
restrict to every scratch union for free, and the probe confirms them
at every union of 590 randomized trials; components that speak about
\emph{applicability along an enumeration} (\lean{noopFeasible},
applicable delivery) are \emph{false} at antichain unions, in three
named shapes (an insert whose anchor a concurrent kill has already
removed fired 390 times across the probes; stale delete and insert
paths fired 68 and 80 times), while every one of those states remained
canonical for its union. So union-level obligations must be
generation-discipline shaped, never applicability shaped. Second, the
bill collapsed on execution: exactly \emph{one} fold induction was owed
beyond the raw layer (at the H layer, whose merge case consumes the
registered slot's layer canonicity); the Eq-quotient layers contain no
step destructs and re-thread mechanically
(\lean{rga\_ra\_linearizable3\_eq\_V}, and the flat production
catalogue over \lean{Step3V} via \lean{FlatGeneric\_Bridge\_V}); and
the NF layer is precisely the refuted \lean{noopFeasible} route with no
production consumer, so its mirror was \emph{deliberately not built}.
The refuted predicate marks a dead proof route, not a defect of the
construction.

\paragraph{The queue, as a corollary.} The mergeable queue of
\S\ref{sec:route} is the instructive consumer: its join is already
per-configuration (\lean{q\_join\_at}), which is exactly the hook the
virtual fold induction consumes, so lifting the criss-cross gate costs
the queue nothing beyond restating reachability over the widened LTS.
The capstone \lean{queue\_ra\_linearizable3\_V} (RA-linearizability at
every honestly reachable configuration with virtual merges enabled) is
ten lines, all of them plumbing. This is the factored architecture of
\S\ref{sec:route} paying out: the framework extension multiplied
across instances at the join boundary, with zero new per-datatype
content.

\section{Commit GC: the keep set and the GC-safety theorem}
\label{sec:gc}

The version store only ever grows, and merges arbitrarily far in the
future reach back into it for their LCA state, so a runtime cannot
reclaim ``everything below the heads.'' The GC question is which store
entries may be dropped without changing any future behavior; the answer
is a keep set computable from the current heads, and a forward
simulation showing pruning outside it is invisible.

\paragraph{The keep set.} Fix a configuration $C_0$ (the prune point).
A version is a \emph{head} (\lean{IsHead}) when some replica's head map
points to it; write $H$ for the current heads.

\begin{definition}[the keep set]
\label{def:keepset}
Over allocated versions (heads and $v$ allocated, with the displayed
event sets),
\[
  \mathrm{Keep}(C_0) \;=\; \bigl\{\, v \;:\; \exists\, h_1, h_2 \in H
  \ (h_1 = h_2 \text{ allowed}),\ \ E(h_1) \cap E(h_2) \subseteq E(v)
  \,\bigr\},
\]
\[
  \mathrm{Keep}'(C_0) \;=\; \mathrm{Keep}(C_0) \cup \{0\},
\]
and $\mathrm{Dropped}(C_0) = \{\, v \text{ allocated} : v \notin
\mathrm{Keep}'(C_0) \,\}$ is what pruning removes.
\hfill\lean{keepSet}, \lean{keepSet'}, \lean{droppedSet}
\end{definition}

This is the \emph{upward event-set closure of the pairwise head intersections},
with the root $0$ pinned separately (the model fixes
$\mathrm{ver}\,0 = (\Sinit, \varnothing)$ forever, and fresh replicas
are created there). Taking $h_1 = h_2$ shows every head is kept, and
with it everything event-set-above any single head. Pruning drops the
store \emph{payload} only: entries outside the keep set become
unallocated, while the replica cores and the DAG skeleton
($\mathrm{parents}$, a map of ids, not states) are untouched. Retaining
the skeleton is forced, not a convenience: restricting the parent
relation to kept versions changes the LCA predicate itself, since
removing common ancestors makes the domination clause easier to
satisfy, so new LCA triples can appear whose event sets violate
Lemma~\ref{lem:lca}, and the pruned structure would fail its own
invariant. The reclaimable memory is the states and event sets, which
is where the memory is; the skeleton stays.

\paragraph{The theorem.} Safety is a forward simulation, built from
three lemmas, the first two packaged as a run invariant:

\begin{definition}[the GC run invariant]
\label{def:gcinv}
$\mathrm{HeadDom}(C_0, e)$: some allocated prune-time head's event set
is contained in $e$. $\mathrm{Virgin}(C_0, v)$: every
prune-time-allocated ancestor of $v$ (along the current parent
relation) is the root. $\mathrm{GCInv}(C_0, C)$:
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=2.2em]
\item[(i)] \emph{permanence}: allocation is permanent and entries
  immutable, so $v$ allocated in $C_0$ implies
  $\mathsf{ver}_C(v) = \mathsf{ver}_{C_0}(v)$;
\item[(ii)] \emph{monotonicity} (disjunctive): every current head $v$
  with $\mathsf{ver}_C(v) = (s, e)$ satisfies $\mathrm{HeadDom}(C_0, e)
  \vee \mathrm{Virgin}(C_0, v)$;
\item[(iii)] the store invariant of Definition~\ref{def:storeinv} at
  $C$.
\end{itemize}
\hfill\lean{GCInv}, \lean{HeadDom}, \lean{Virgin}
\end{definition}

\emph{Monotonicity} is clause (ii): along any run from
$C_0$, every current head either event-set-dominates some prune-time
head, or its only prune-time ancestor is the pinned root. The
disjunction is forced by replica creation: a fresh replica's head is
the root, and its lineage grows from empty until it first merges with a
dominating lineage, at which point it becomes dominating itself.
\emph{Intersection containment}: if a future merge fires with heads
$v_1, v_2$ and allocated LCA $v_\ell$, then $v_\ell$ was not dropped;
when both sides dominate prune-time heads $h_1, h_2$, Lemma~\ref{lem:lca}
gives $E_0(h_1) \cap E_0(h_2) \subseteq E(v_1) \cap E(v_2) =
E(v_\ell)$, which is membership in the keep set, and when either side
is virgin the LCA can only be the pinned root
(\lean{lca\_not\_dropped}). \emph{Prune
commutation}: every step of the unpruned run is matched, with the same
label, on the pruned store; apply reads only the issuing head and a
fresh slot (never dropped: dropped slots were allocated at prune time
and allocation is permanent), and merge reads the two heads and the
LCA, which the previous lemma keeps.

\begin{theorem}[GC safety]
\label{thm:gcsafety}
Let $C_0$ satisfy the store invariant (supplied by plain reachability,
Theorem~\ref{thm:storeinv}), and let
$C_0 \xrightarrow{\ell_1 \cdots \ell_n} C$ be any labelled
$\mathsf{Step3}$ run. Then the pruned prune point admits the same run
against the correspondingly pruned endpoint,
\[
  C_0 \setminus \mathrm{Dropped}(C_0)
  \;\xrightarrow{\ell_1 \cdots \ell_n}\;
  C \setminus \mathrm{Dropped}(C_0)
  \qquad\text{(\lean{pruneKeep}, \lean{dropVer}),}
\]
with the same label sequence (in particular every query, whose value
rides on the label), and every replica read at every point is unchanged:
$N_{C \setminus \mathrm{Dropped}(C_0)} = N_C$. Prune-definedness is
convention (iv) of \S\ref{sec:setting}.
\hfill\lean{gc\_safety}
\end{theorem}

One scope note, recorded because it is a genuine asymmetry: the
converse simulation is deliberately not mechanized. A pruned store can
exhibit steps the unpruned one cannot (a freed slot can be
re-allocated; store-wide timestamp freshness quantifies over fewer
entries), and the asymmetry is unobservable only via a reachability
invariant (every allocated version sits below some current head) that
is not part of the configuration structure. The direction GC-safety
needs (nothing is lost) is the mechanized one.

\paragraph{The head-sync countermodel.} Head-sync is the discipline
that future merge arguments are current heads; the Merge rule enforces
it syntactically. The keep set is calibrated to exactly that
discipline, and the calibration is tight: a merge from an old interior
version genuinely needs a version outside the keep set. The
countermodel, worked concretely (replicas $A, B$; events
$e_1, e_2, e_3$ on $A$, $e_4$ on $B$):

\begin{center}\small
\begin{tabular}{@{}clllc@{}}
\toprule
version & created by & events & parents & kept? \\
\midrule
0 & root & $\varnothing$ & $[\,]$ & pinned \\
1 & $A$ applies $e_1$ at $0$ & $\{e_1\}$ & $[0]$ & \textbf{dropped} \\
2 & $A$ applies $e_2$ at $1$ & $\{e_1,e_2\}$ & $[1]$ & kept \\
3 & $B$ merges $A$ (LCA $0$) & $\{e_1,e_2\}$ & $[0,2]$ & kept \\
4 & $A$ applies $e_3$ at $2$ (head) & $\{e_1,e_2,e_3\}$ & $[2]$ & kept \\
5 & $B$ applies $e_4$ at $3$ (head) & $\{e_1,e_2,e_4\}$ & $[3]$ & kept \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

\noindent The pairwise head intersection is $E(4) \cap E(5) =
\{e_1,e_2\}$; version $1$, with $E(1) = \{e_1\}$, contains no pairwise
intersection and is dropped, strictly (the store genuinely returns
nothing at $1$ after pruning). The future head merge is safe: the LCA
of $(4,5)$ is version $2$, kept. But version $1$ satisfies the LCA
predicate for the \emph{interior} pair $(1,5)$, so any widened Merge
that accepted a non-head argument (a rewound replica, a checkpoint
restore, a replica missing from the head map) would demand the dropped
entry. Every out-of-band way of resurrecting an old version breaks the
keep set; head-sync is a hypothesis of the metatheorem, not pessimism.
The countermodel is machine-checked:

\begin{proposition}[head-sync is load-bearing]
\label{prop:headsync}
In the store $C_{\mathrm{ex}}$ of the table above
(\lean{GC\_Safety.lean}, over the framework-resident conditioned
grow-only set),
\[
  \mathsf{IsLCA}(1, 5, 1)
  \;\wedge\; \neg\, \mathsf{IsHead}(C_{\mathrm{ex}}, 1)
  \;\wedge\; 1 \in \mathrm{Dropped}(C_{\mathrm{ex}})
  \;\wedge\; \mathsf{ver}_{\mathrm{pruned}}(1) = \mathsf{none} :
\]
the dropped interior version is the LCA of the interior pair $(1, 5)$,
so a non-head-sync merge demands a pruned entry.
\hfill\lean{gc\_needs\_head\_sync}
\end{proposition}

\paragraph{The revision under virtual merges.} \S\ref{sec:vlca} widens
what a merge reads: where no exact LCA exists, the resolution reads the
maximal common ancestors, whose event sets sit strictly \emph{below}
the pairwise meet, while the keep set above retains versions
\emph{above} some pairwise meet. Under the widened rule the original
keep set prunes away exactly what the recursion needs. The revision:

\begin{definition}[the MCA closure, the revised keep set, and the widened invariant]
\label{def:mcasclosure}
$\mathrm{mcasClosure}(C)$ is the least set of versions containing every
allocated head and closed under pairwise MCAs of its members
(inductively: rules \textsc{head} and \textsc{mca}, the latter over
$\mathsf{IsMCA}(\{a\}, b, m)$ for members $a, b$; finite, since ranks
only decrease). The revised keep set is the upward event-set closure of
its members, plus the pinned root:
\[
  \mathrm{Keep}_V(C_0) \;=\; \bigl\{\, v \text{ allocated} \;:\;
  \exists w \in \mathrm{mcasClosure}(C_0) \text{ allocated},\;\;
  E(w) \subseteq E(v) \,\bigr\} \;\cup\; \{0\}.
\]
The run invariant $\mathrm{GCInv}_V$ extends
Definition~\ref{def:gcinv} with: the \emph{prune-time} store invariant;
parent-list immutability at prune-time versions; the \emph{head gate}
(a current head allocated at prune time is a prune-time head or the
root); and the \emph{gateway} invariant: for every $v$ allocated after
the prune point and every prune-time-allocated $w$ with
$\mathsf{Reaches}(w, v)$, either $w = 0$ or some prune-time head $h$ has
$\mathsf{Reaches}(w, h)$ and $\mathsf{Reaches}(h, v)$.
\hfill\lean{mcasClosure}, \lean{keepSetV}, \lean{GCInvV}
\end{definition}

$\mathrm{Keep}_V$ contains the old keep set,
and covers every recursion read: sub-pair MCAs are contained in
real-pair MCAs, iterated. One might hope the one-layer seed (the
pairwise MCAs of the current heads, without the fixpoint) suffices; it
is one closure layer short, machine-witnessed: on a directed nested
criss-cross, a depth-2 resolution reads the MCAs of the MCAs, pruning
by the one-layer rule kills them (the sync raises a pruned-store
error), and pruning by the closure rule keeps them while still pruning
a genuinely dead interior commit, with the post-GC sync equal to the
no-GC control. Future-proofing (the analogue of intersection
containment) needs one new reachability fact, the \emph{gateway}
lemma: after the prune point, every ancestry path from a prune-time
version to a post-prune version passes through a prune-time head or
the pinned root, so a prune-time version that a future virtual
resolution reads is an MCA of prune-time heads (layer one of the
closure), and deeper reads stay in the closure.

\begin{theorem}[GC safety, widened LTS]
\label{thm:gcsafetyV}
Let $C_0$ satisfy the store invariant, and let
$C_0 \xrightarrow{\ell_1 \cdots \ell_n} C$ be any labelled
$\mathsf{Step3V}$ run (gated \emph{and} virtual merges,
Definition~\ref{def:step3v}). Then, pruning to $\mathrm{Keep}_V(C_0)$,
\[
  C_0 \setminus \mathrm{Dropped}_V(C_0)
  \;\xrightarrow{\ell_1 \cdots \ell_n}\;
  C \setminus \mathrm{Dropped}_V(C_0)
\]
as a $\mathsf{Step3V}$ run with the same label sequence, and every
replica read at every point is unchanged. The headline shape of
Theorem~\ref{thm:gcsafety} is kept verbatim; the gateway forced no
statement change.
\hfill\lean{gc\_safetyV}
\end{theorem}

\paragraph{Bounded state: dropping the skeleton by a clock swap.} The
theorem above reclaims the payload but keeps the parent map, so the
pruned store still costs $O(\text{commits ever minted})$: it retains
history's shape, not its states. The skeleton was forced only for the
parent-relation representation, where restricting $\mathrm{parents}$
mints new LCA triples that falsify $\mathrm{lca\_events}$
(the argument of the keep-set paragraph). A change of representation
removes it. The commit graph is a delta-compression of event sets, so a
version's ancestry role is already carried by its event set used as a
\emph{clock}: ancestry is $\subseteq$, and the LCA of a head pair is the
kept version at the \emph{clock meet} $E(v_1) \cap E(v_2)$, which is
exactly what $\mathrm{lca\_events}$ says the structural LCA carries. The
bounded store keeps only the kept payloads and sets $\mathrm{parents}
\equiv [\,]$; with no edges, structural reachability collapses to
equality and $\mathrm{lca\_events}$ holds trivially, so there is no
history to store and the state is $O(|\mathrm{Keep}'|)$
(\code{GC\_BoundedState.lean}).

\begin{definition}[the bounded store and the clock lookup]
\label{def:clockstore}
$\mathrm{clockPrune}(C)$ prunes the payload to $\mathrm{Keep}'(C)$
\emph{and} sets $\mathrm{parents} \equiv [\,]$: its $\mathsf{ver}$
support is contained in $\mathrm{Keep}'(C)$
(\lean{clockPrune\_support}) and its skeleton is the constant empty
function (\lean{clockPrune\_no\_skeleton}).
$\mathrm{ClockLCA}(cc, v_1, v_2, u, s_u)$: the heads are allocated with
event sets $e_1, e_2$ and $\mathsf{ver}_{cc}(u) = (s_u,\, e_1 \cap
e_2)$; the lookup reads event sets only, never parents.
$\mathrm{ClockCoherent}(cc)$, the \emph{lineage guard}: any two
allocated versions with the same event-set clock carry the same state
(the payload is a function of the clock).
\hfill\lean{clockPrune}, \lean{ClockLCA}, \lean{ClockCoherent}
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[the clock lookup is complete; under the guard, sound]
\label{thm:clocklca}
\emph{Completeness} (guard-free): if $C$ satisfies the store invariant
and a head pair $v_1, v_2$ (both allocated, at heads $r_1, r_2$) has a
structural LCA $v_T$ with $\mathsf{ver}(v_T) = (s_T, e_T)$, then
$\mathrm{ClockLCA}(\mathrm{clockPrune}(C), v_1, v_2, v_T, s_T)$: the
swap never disables a merge the skeleton enabled (the LCA is kept,
\lean{lca\_not\_dropped}). \emph{Soundness} (guarded): if
$\mathrm{ClockCoherent}(cc)$ and both
$\mathrm{ClockLCA}(cc, v_1, v_2, u, s_u)$ and
$\mathrm{ClockLCA}(cc, v_1, v_2, u', s_{u'})$, then $s_u = s_{u'}$.
Combined: under the guard, every clock LCA of a head pair in the
bounded store carries exactly the structural LCA's state
(\lean{clockLCA\_recovers}).
\hfill\lean{clockLCA\_complete}, \lean{clockLCA\_sound}
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[bounded-state GC safety]
\label{thm:gcbounded}
Let $C_0$ satisfy the store invariant and
$C_0 \xrightarrow{\ell_1 \cdots \ell_n} C$ be any $\mathsf{Step3}$ run.
On the bounded LTS $\mathsf{Step3C}$ (every rule of
Definition~\ref{def:step3} with $\mathrm{parents}$ frozen at $[\,]$, and
\textsc{Merge} reading its LCA state by $\mathrm{ClockLCA}$ instead of
$\mathsf{IsLCA}$ plus $\mathsf{ver}(v_\ell)$):
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=2.2em]
\item[(i)] $\mathrm{clockPrune}(C_0) \xrightarrow{\ell_1 \cdots \ell_n}
  C \setminus_{\flat} \mathrm{Dropped}(C_0)$, the whole trace, label for
  label (the flat drop $\setminus_{\flat}$ also empties the skeleton);
\item[(ii)] every replica read at every point is unchanged;
\item[(iii)] the bounded store's payload support is contained in
  $\mathrm{Keep}'(C_0)$; and
\item[(iv)] its parent map is constantly empty,
\end{itemize}
so the state costs $O(|\mathrm{Keep}'|)$, not $O(\text{commits
ever})$.
\hfill\lean{gc\_safety\_bounded}
\end{theorem}

The swap has exactly one hazard, and it is the lineage collision that
\S\ref{sec:stability} isolates. Resolving the LCA by clock alone is sound
while the payload is a function of the event set, and unsound once
datatype compaction breaks that: a same-clock version from an unrelated
lineage, used as a merge's $L$, resurrects a dropped instance. The
structural $\mathrm{IsLCA}$ sidesteps it by resolving within shared
ancestry; a bare clock lookup cannot, so it is guarded by
$\mathrm{ClockCoherent}$ (Definition~\ref{def:clockstore}), which is
precisely the $R_S$-compatibility of \S\ref{sec:stability}; soundness
under the guard is Theorem~\ref{thm:clocklca}. The boundary is
machine-checked both ways: a PASS SPOT where a future head-pair merge
finds its LCA at the clock meet and reads correctly, and a FAIL SPOT:

\begin{proposition}[the lineage collision]
\label{prop:lineage}
On the compacted store $C_{\mathrm{coll}}$
(\lean{GC\_BoundedState.lean}, two lineages compacted to the same
clock),
\[
\begin{aligned}
  &\mathrm{ClockLCA}(C_{\mathrm{coll}}, 3, 4, 1, \{10\})
  \;\wedge\; \mathrm{ClockLCA}(C_{\mathrm{coll}}, 3, 4, 2, \emptyset) \\
  &\qquad\wedge\; \{10\} \neq \emptyset
  \;\wedge\; \neg\, \mathrm{ClockCoherent}(C_{\mathrm{coll}}) :
\end{aligned}
\]
the head pair $(3,4)$'s clock lookup returns two same-clock candidates
with divergent states, so clock identification alone resurrects the
dropped instance (picking version $1$) or loses it (picking $2$), and
the guard is provably false on this store; conversely, had the guard
held, Theorem~\ref{thm:clocklca} would have forced the two lookups
equal (\lean{spot\_guard\_would\_fix}).
\hfill\lean{spot\_lineage\_resurrection}
\end{proposition}

One case
is deliberately not covered: a criss-cross antichain of MCAs has no
single clock meet, so the bounded form of a \emph{virtual} merge needs
the retained lineage among the MCA closure, and is left owed.

\paragraph{One frontier, three consumers.} The meet of the current
heads, $\bigcap_{h \in H} E(h)$, is the \emph{stable cut}: the events
every replica has already incorporated, below which no future merge can
ever again place a concurrent event. Three parts of this document
consume that one frontier. The garbage collector reads it from
\emph{above}: the keep set retains the upward closure of the pairwise
meets, and what pruning reclaims is exactly the versions no future LCA
lookup can name. The stability VC of \S\ref{sec:stability} reads it
from \emph{below}: facts certified at the cut stay true under all
future merges, which is what licenses compaction. And the storage layer
of Part~II (\S\ref{sec:storage}) consumes it as the epoch boundary for
coordinate re-coding, where the in-flight-anchor argument needs
visibility of all current heads. A runtime that tracks the frontier
once serves all three consumers from the same bookkeeping, at pairwise
rather than global granularity where GC needs it.

\section{The stability VC: verified GC callbacks}
\label{sec:stability}

Commit GC (\S\ref{sec:gc}) reclaims \emph{versions}; it never touches
the states themselves. But states also accumulate redundancy: an OR-set
holding two add instances of the same element needs only one once both
are known everywhere, and the embedded-chain RGA's coordinates carry
dead history that a re-coding epoch could shed (\S\ref{sec:storage}).
The runtime therefore wants to call back \emph{into the datatype} so
the concrete state can compact. The question is what the datatype must
prove for such a \code{compact} to be sound, and what exactly the
runtime must promise it. Both halves have sharp answers, and both were
shaped by countermodels.

\paragraph{The naive gate is unsound: the discriminating remove.} The
obvious runtime promise (``the cut $S$ is contained in every current
head'') does not suffice, by a near miss that shapes everything after
it. Adds of element $a$ at stamps $t_1 < t_2$, both below every head.
Replica $A$ compacts, dropping the older instance $t_1$. But a remove,
minted long ago by a replica that had observed \emph{only} $t_2$ (the
remove is concurrent with the add $t_1$), is still at or below some
head and arrives later. In the full execution the remove kills only
$t_2$ and the element survives through $t_1$; in the compacted
execution $t_1$ is gone and the element is absent. Reads diverge
(Figure~\ref{fig:discrem}). The countermodel fires mechanically in the
validation harness under the naive gate, and once (seed 2026, trial
218) it was found by the randomized twin runs rather than injected. It
is also machine-checked in Lean, on the concrete merge states:

\begin{proposition}[the discriminating remove]
\label{prop:discrem}
Let $l_V$ hold both adds of $a$ live (the shared LCA payload of
Figure~\ref{fig:discrem}), $a_{\mathrm{naive}}$ the naively compacted
head (older instance $t_1$ dropped), and $b_{\mathrm{rem}}$ the branch
where the in-flight remove killed only $t_2$. Then
\[
  a \in \mathrm{read}\bigl(\mergeL(l_V,\, l_V,\, b_{\mathrm{rem}})\bigr)
  \;\;\wedge\;\;
  a \notin \mathrm{read}\bigl(\mergeL(l_V,\, a_{\mathrm{naive}},\,
  b_{\mathrm{rem}})\bigr) :
\]
the control and the naively compacted merge diverge on $a$
(\lean{MRDT\_Instances/ORSet/ORSet\_Stability.lean}).
\hfill\lean{countermodel\_diverges}
\end{proposition}

\begin{figure}[t]
\centering
\begin{tikzpicture}[>=Stealth,
  ver/.style={draw, rounded corners, inner sep=3pt, font=\small},
  bad/.style={draw, rounded corners, inner sep=3pt, font=\small,
    fill=orange!12},
  lbl/.style={font=\scriptsize, text=black!60}]
\node[ver] (r)  at (0,0)     {$v_0$};
\node[ver] (a1) at (-2.6,-1.2) {$v_1:\ \add_a^{t_1}$};
\node[ver] (a2) at ( 2.6,-1.2) {$v_2:\ \add_a^{t_2}$};
\node[ver] (m)  at (-1.2,-2.4) {$v_3:\ \{t_1, t_2\}$};
\node[bad] (rm) at ( 3.4,-2.4) {$v_4:\ \rem\,\{t_2\}$};
\node[bad] (cp) at (-1.2,-3.6) {$v_5:\ \mathrm{compact}$, drop $t_1$};
\node[ver] (fin) at ( 1.2,-4.8) {$v_6:\ \text{merge}$};
\draw[->] (r) -- (a1); \draw[->] (r) -- (a2);
\draw[->] (a1) -- (m); \draw[->] (a2) -- (m);
\draw[->] (a2) -- (rm);
\draw[->] (m) -- (cp);
\draw[->] (cp) -- (fin); \draw[->] (rm) -- (fin);
\node[lbl, right=0.15cm of rm, align=left]
  {minted seeing only $t_2$:\\ concurrent with $\add^{t_1}$};
\node[lbl, below=0.12cm of fin, align=center]
  {full: $a$ survives through $t_1$\\
   compacted: $a$ absent; reads diverge};
\end{tikzpicture}
\caption{The discriminating remove (top-down; edges parent to child).
Both adds of $a$ sit below every head of the compacting replica, yet an
in-flight remove that observed only $t_2$ can still arrive. Dropping
the older instance $t_1$ under the naive ``below every head'' gate
changes the read: the remove kills $t_2$ alone, and only the full state
still holds $a$. The repair is SettledAt: compaction must wait until
every event concurrent with the cut is already absorbed.}
\label{fig:discrem}
\end{figure}

\paragraph{The interface: SettledAt.} The repair is a version-level
condition.

\begin{definition}[SettledAt]
\label{def:settled}
Events $e, a$ are \emph{concurrent} in $C$ (\lean{ConcOp}) when
$e \neq a$ and neither is $\vis$ the other. A cut $S$ is settled
against an event set $E$ (\lean{SettledAtOn}) when
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=2.2em]
\item[(i)] $S \subseteq E$;
\item[(ii)] $S$ is causally downward closed ($a \vis b$, $b \in S$
  imply $a \in S$); and
\item[(iii)] every $e \in E(C)$ concurrent with some member of $S$
