\noindent The lockstep row is the identification: \emph{the
embedded-chain RGA is observationally the published tombstoned RGA
with the tombstone set compressed into coordinate entropy},
machine-checked on 120 executions and now a kernel-checked theorem
(the compaction theorem, \S\ref{sec:mechanization}): on every honest
event set, any enumeration of the tombstoned RGA's visible ids in its
own visible order \emph{equals} the embed read, element for element. The L1 row is the separation
from the verified rehoming RGA, and it is
exactly the one-line difference of \S\ref{sec:design}: that design's
climb re-sorts a rehomed node by its own timestamp (the
$[b,a,c] \to [c,b]$ anomaly), i.e.\ its sort key is the birth chain
\emph{truncated at each rehoming}; this design keeps the chain whole.

\section{The sided generalization: two-sidedness as a parameter}
\label{sec:sided}

The one blemish in the battery is L19: two replicas that each type a
\emph{backward} run at the same place (repeatedly ``insert before the
current head'') interleave, because every insert-before anchors at the
same left neighbor and both runs land in one sibling group. The fan is
not a traversal artifact: no read order of the same birth tree un-fans
a fan, so any fix must change where insert-before \emph{anchors}. The
Fugue family's answer is a second side: insert-before anchors at the
\emph{right} neighbor as an L-entry, so a backward run is a chain,
exactly as a forward run already is.

\paragraph{The L19 example, concretely.} It is the battery's own trace
(Figure~\ref{fig:sided-l19}). The LCA document is $\langle 1 \rangle$
(one insert, stamp $1$, anchored at the start marker $\diamond$). Then,
concurrently, replica A types a backward run at the head, three
inserts each anchored ``before the current first element'':
\[
  \ins(10 \text{ at head}),\quad \ins(30 \text{ at head}),\quad
  \ins(50 \text{ at head}),
\]
so A locally reads $\langle 50, 30, 10, 1 \rangle$; replica B does the
same with stamps $21, 41, 61$ and locally reads
$\langle 61, 41, 21, 1 \rangle$. (The stamps interleave numerically,
$10 < 21 < 30 < 41 < 50 < 61$, because the two replicas' Lamport
clocks advance together.) In the one-sided datatype, ``insert at the
head'' can only anchor at the left neighbor, which is $\diamond$ for
every one of the six inserts: all six become R-children of $\diamond$,
one sibling group, and the sibling tiebreak (newest first) merges the
two runs by stamp:
\[
  \langle\, 61, 50, 41, 30, 21, 10, 1 \,\rangle
\]
after the merge, the two sentences shuffled together. No traversal of
that tree can do better; the information ``these three form one run''
is simply not in a sibling fan whose stamps interleave.

\paragraph{The sided datatype, and why it prevents the interleaving.}
The generalization does not build a second datatype. An insert names an
anchor \emph{and a side}; chains become sequences of
$(\mathrm{side}, \mathrm{timestamp\ delta})$ entries; and the display
rule generalizes from the prefix rule (``a prefix precedes its
extensions'') to the in-order rule (``L-extensions, then the node, then
R-extensions''). One marker trick makes this ordinary lexicographic
comparison again: a node's sort key is its coordinate followed by a
virtual terminator, and the sided symbol alphabet is ordered
\[
  (R,1) < (R,2) < \cdots < \mathsf{marker} < \cdots < (L,2) < (L,1),
\]
with the L band \emph{mirrored} (among L-siblings the newer sits
adjacent to the node). Marker above the R band puts a node before its
R-extensions; marker below the L band puts L-extensions before the
node. The three displays that carry this section:

\begin{definition}[the sided chain and its coordinate]
\label{def:schain}
A sided chain is a list of entries $(\mathrm{side}, \delta)$,
$\mathrm{side} \in \{R, L\}$, deltas positive (\lean{SEntry},
\lean{SChain}, \lean{PosSChain}). Its stored coordinate concatenates
one symbol block per entry (\lean{sBlock}, \lean{sidedCoordOf}):
\[
  \mathrm{block}(R, \delta) = \Gamma.\mathrm{enc}(\delta)
  \text{ in the R band},
  \qquad
  \mathrm{block}(L, \delta) =
  \overline{\Gamma.\mathrm{enc}(\delta)}
  \text{ in the L band},
\]
the R band mapping $\code{0} \mapsto 1$, $\code{1} \mapsto 2$, the L
band $\code{0} \mapsto 4$, $\code{1} \mapsto 5$, and
$\overline{\,\cdot\,}$ the bitwise complement (\lean{compl}: the
order-reversing twin at identical lengths, prefix-freedom
preserved); equivalently, one band-symbol map per side, L
pre-composing the complement (\lean{sBlock\_eq}, \lean{sideSym}).
The sort key appends the marker, $\mathrm{sKey}(c) = c \cdot
\langle 3 \rangle$, and the bands straddle it: $\{1,2\} < 3 <
\{4,5\}$.
\hfill\lean{sidedCoordOf}, \lean{sKey}
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[the in-order rule]
\label{def:schainbefore}
Entry divergence at a shared anchor (\lean{sEntryBefore}): among
R-siblings the newer first ($(R,d)$ before $(R,e)$ iff $e < d$);
among L-siblings the older first ($(L,d)$ before $(L,e)$ iff
$d < e$, the mirror); every L entry before every R entry. The
display order on sided chains is the least relation closed under
three rules:
\[
  ch \cdot \langle (L,d) \rangle \cdot rest \;\prec\; ch,
  \qquad
  ch \;\prec\; ch \cdot \langle (R,d) \rangle \cdot rest,
  \qquad
  q \cdot \langle e_1 \rangle \cdot c_1 \;\prec\;
  q \cdot \langle e_2 \rangle \cdot c_2
  \ \ (e_1 \text{ before } e_2) :
\]
L-extensions precede the node, the node precedes its R-extensions,
divergences by the entry order. Distinct chains are always
comparable (\lean{schainBefore\_total}).
\hfill\lean{schainBefore}
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[the sided marker theorem]
\label{thm:sidedmarker}
For distinct positive sided chains $c_1, c_2$:
\[
  \mathrm{keyLt}\bigl(\mathrm{sKey}(\mathrm{sidedCoordOf}_\Gamma(c_2)),\
  \mathrm{sKey}(\mathrm{sidedCoordOf}_\Gamma(c_1))\bigr) =
  \mathsf{true}
  \;\iff\; \mathrm{schainBefore}\ c_1\ c_2 :
\]
two-sidedness is plain lexicographic comparison over the marker
alphabet; no bespoke three-way prefix clause survives into the read.
Unique decodability holds alongside (\lean{sidedCoordOf\_inj}: the
side is recoverable from the first symbol's band, the delta by
per-band prefix-freedom).
\hfill\lean{sdisplay\_iff\_schainBefore}
\end{theorem}

On the example, the Fugue anchoring rule (the generation-policy
paragraph below) sends each ``insert at head'' to the \emph{successor}
as an L-entry, so each run is an L-chain
(Figure~\ref{fig:sided-l19}, right). Writing chains as
entry sequences with deltas relative to the parent's stamp
($10 = 1 + 9$, $30 = 10 + 20$, \dots):
\[
\begin{array}{lcl}
  \chain(1)  = (R,1) &\qquad&
  \chain(10) = (R,1)\,(L,9) \\
  \chain(30) = (R,1)\,(L,9)\,(L,20) &&
  \chain(21) = (R,1)\,(L,20) \\
  \chain(50) = (R,1)\,(L,9)\,(L,20)\,(L,20) &&
  \chain(41) = (R,1)\,(L,20)\,(L,20)
\end{array}
\]
and the marker-lex comparisons (keys descending in display order) work
out as follows, writing $M$ for the marker:
\begin{itemize}
\item \emph{L-extensions precede the node}: $\mathrm{key}(10) =
  (R,1)(L,9)\,M$ vs $\mathrm{key}(1) = (R,1)\,M$ first differ at
  position 2, $(L,9)$ vs $M$, and the L band sits above the marker: so
  $10$ displays before $1$. Likewise $30$ before $10$, $50$ before
  $30$: each backward keystroke lands immediately left of the previous
  one, which is the typed intent.
\item \emph{The two runs are decided once, not per pair}:
  $\mathrm{key}(10)$ vs $\mathrm{key}(21)$ first differ at position 2,
  $(L,9)$ vs $(L,20)$, and the L band is mirrored, so $(L,20) <
  (L,9)$: A's run displays before B's. Now \emph{every} node of A's
  chain carries the prefix $(R,1)(L,9)$ and every node of B's carries
  $(R,1)(L,20)$, so \emph{every} cross-pair first-differs at that same
  position 2 with that same verdict. The blocks cannot interleave:
  \[
    \langle\, 50, 30, 10,\ 61, 41, 21,\ 1 \,\rangle .
  \]
  This is the datatype-level subtree convexity
  (Theorem~\ref{thm:sconvex}): \emph{any} policy that keeps a run
  inside one subtree gets non-interleaving from the datatype, for
  free.
\end{itemize}

\begin{theorem}[sided subtree convexity]
\label{thm:sconvex}
For every prefix $p$ and sided chains $c_x, c_y, c_z$: if $c_x$ and
$c_y$ extend $p$, $\mathrm{schainBefore}\ c_x\ c_z$, and
$\mathrm{schainBefore}\ c_z\ c_y$, then $c_z$ extends $p$. With the
empty extension allowed, the node itself is a member: a subtree's
display interval is L-descendants, the node, R-descendants, with
nothing foreign between. A pure chain lemma: no state, no honesty,
no property of the code.
\hfill\lean{schain\_subtree\_convex}
\end{theorem}

The current datatype is the all-R fragment of this order,
\emph{exactly}: with L uninhabited the in-order rule degenerates to the
prefix rule, and the existing key of \S\ref{sec:encoding} (the
$\mathrm{sym}$-mapped bits plus terminator) is literally this
construction with the L band empty. That is a theorem, not a slogan,
twice: once on chains, once on folds.

\begin{theorem}[the all-R fragment theorem]
\label{thm:fragment}
For distinct positive one-sided chains $c_1, c_2$, with
$\mathrm{liftR}$ tagging every entry $R$:
\[
  \mathrm{schainBefore}\ (\mathrm{liftR}\ c_1)\ (\mathrm{liftR}\ c_2)
  \;\iff\; \mathrm{chainBefore}\ c_1\ c_2,
\]
via the key identity
$\mathrm{sKey}(\mathrm{sidedCoordOf}_\Gamma(\mathrm{liftR}\ c)) =
\mathrm{key}(\mathrm{coordOf}_\Gamma(c))$ (\lean{sKey\_liftR}): with
L uninhabited, the in-order rule is the prefix rule, symbol for
symbol.
\hfill\lean{schainBefore\_liftR}
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[the fragment at the instance]
\label{thm:sfoldlift}
For every operation list $\rho$ of the one-sided instance,
\[
  \mathrm{sFold}_\Gamma(\mathrm{map}\ \mathrm{liftOp}\ \rho)
  = \mathrm{map}\ \mathrm{liftRec}\ (\mathrm{eFold}_\Gamma\ \rho),
\]
record for record.
Unconditional: no honesty, no sortedness hypotheses, a pure
simulation; the reads agree as a corollary
(\lean{sided\_allR\_read\_eq}). Chained with the compaction theorem
(\S\ref{sec:mechanization}), the all-R sided instance reads as the
published tombstoned RGA.
\hfill\lean{sFold\_liftOp}
\end{theorem}

\begin{figure}[h]
\centering
\begin{tikzpicture}

\caption{\sloppy L19 under the two anchoring rules, the battery's own trace.
LCA document $\langle 1\rangle$; replica A (blue) types backward
$10, 30, 50$ before the head while replica B (orange) concurrently
types backward $21, 41, 61$. One-sided (left): every insert-before
anchors at the same left neighbor $\diamond$, both runs land in one
R-sibling group, and the timestamp-sorted fan interleaves them. Sided
under the Fugue policy (right): insert-before anchors at the
\emph{successor} as an L-entry, each run is an L-chain hanging off
$1$, and the in-order read keeps the blocks contiguous: L-extensions
precede the node ($50, 30, 10$ before their parents' line ending at

\paragraph{How the encoding realizes it, for zero extra bits.} The
one-sided block of \S\ref{sec:encoding} spends a constant leading
\code{1} per level whose only job is keeping coordinates nonempty;
repurpose it as the side bit. The L band needs an
order-\emph{reversing} prefix-free code, and the bit complement
$\overline{C}$ is exactly that at identical lengths (flipping every
bit flips every first-difference verdict and preserves both lengths
and prefix relations), so the block becomes
\[
  \mathrm{block}(\mathrm{side}, \delta) \;=\; \mathrm{sideBit}
  \,\|\, \bigl(C(\delta) \text{ if } R,\ \overline{C(\delta)}
  \text{ if } L\bigr),
\]
prefix-free per band, with no framing change and no new bits. On the
example, with the binary delta code ($C(1) = \code{0}$, $C(9) =
\code{1110001}$, $C(20) = \code{111100100}$, so $\overline{C(9)} =
\code{0001110}$, $\overline{C(20)} = \code{000011011}$), the stored
coordinates are the concatenated blocks:
\begin{center}\small
\begin{tabular}{@{}lll@{}}
\toprule
node & chain & stored coordinate (side bits marked) \\
\midrule
$1$  & $(R,1)$ &
  $\underline{\code{1}}\code{0}$ \\
$10$ & $(R,1)(L,9)$ &
  $\underline{\code{1}}\code{0}\;\underline{\code{0}}\code{0001110}$ \\
$21$ & $(R,1)(L,20)$ &
  $\underline{\code{1}}\code{0}\;\underline{\code{0}}\code{000011011}$ \\
$30$ & $(R,1)(L,9)(L,20)$ &
  $\underline{\code{1}}\code{0}\;\underline{\code{0}}\code{0001110}\;
   \underline{\code{0}}\code{000011011}$ \\
$50$ & $(R,1)(L,9)(L,20)(L,20)$ &
  $\underline{\code{1}}\code{0}\;\underline{\code{0}}\code{0001110}\;
   \underline{\code{0}}\code{000011011}\;
   \underline{\code{0}}\code{000011011}$ \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
To compare two coordinates, parse: read a side bit; if \code{1},
decode one $C$ codeword as an R-band symbol $(R,\delta)$; if \code{0},
decode one $\overline{C}$ codeword (decode $C$ on the flipped bits) as
an L-band symbol $(L,\delta)$; repeat, then terminate with the marker.
Prefix-freedom per band delimits every block, so no length fields or
framing are needed, exactly as one-sided. The complement is what makes
the mirror free: within the L band, plain descending comparison of the
\emph{stored} bits is already the mirrored delta order ($
\overline{C(20)} = \code{000011011} < \overline{C(9)} = \code{0001110}
$ bitwise, matching $(L,20) < (L,9)$), so the carrier stores entropy
payload only and the marker-lex of the previous paragraph is read off
the bits. Faithfulness of this parse (unique decodability plus order
embedding of the sided alphabet) is machine-checked by the
chain-model/code-model lockstep below.

Two honest cost notes. Runs absorb the side bit like every other
run-constant (paid once at the head). On \emph{this} trace the sided
coordinates are longer than the one-sided fan's (the runs' deltas are
$20$ because the two replicas' Lamport clocks interleave); the
compression claim is about the common case, continuation-stamped
backward typing ($\delta = 1$ per keystroke, an \code{0}$\|
\overline{C(1)} = \code{01}$ block per level), where today's design
pays a growing-$\delta$ fan. Two-sidedness buys the semantics always
and the compression exactly when backward runs are locally stamped.

\paragraph{Side selection is a generation policy, not a datatype
choice.} If insert merely ``takes a side,'' convergence and
RA-linearizability hold for any sides whatsoever: those proofs never
consult the order's intent. What depends on \emph{which} side is
picked is the ordering intent. Fugue's non-interleaving needs the rule
``right child of the left neighbor when it has no right children, else
left child of the successor,'' and that rule lives at generation time,
where the framework already has a slot: honesty, the same layer that
polices the mint. The architecture is therefore one sided datatype,
verified once, policy-free (canonicity, fold exactness, the Join,
RA-linearizability), with intent theorems per generation policy: under
always-R, read-equivalence to the published RGA (the fragment theorem
composed with the compaction theorem); under the Fugue rule,
non-interleaving (a subtree is an in-order interval). RGA and Fugue
become two disciplines over one kernel rather than two datatypes.

\paragraph{Validation.} \code{whiteboard/litmus/embed\_sided.py}: the
chain semantics and the bit carrier as lockstep twin models, both run
under both policies. Every design prediction survived the first full
run:

\begin{center}\small
\begin{tabular}{@{}ll@{}}
\toprule
check & verdict \\
\midrule
full gauntlet under the Fugue policy (L1--L25, credential & clean,
  \textbf{including L19} \\
\quad family, subordination CEs, stale forks) &
  (out $= \langle 50,30,10,61,41,21,1\rangle$) \\
same gauntlet under always-R & clean except L19: the \\
 & one-sided fan, reproduced \\
chain semantics $\sim$ bit carrier, both policies & lockstep-EXACT
  (unique \\
 & decodability + order embedding) \\
all-R fragment $\sim$ the one-sided embed model & lockstep-EXACT, every
  scenario \\
randomized version-DAG PBT, all four models & 120 + 300 clean \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

\noindent The Lean mechanization of both layers (the sided chain-lex
kernel and the sided conditioned instance) has landed kernel-clean;
its capstone is the policy/datatype split as a quantifier.

\begin{theorem}[the sided capstone]
\label{thm:sidedcapstone}
Call a configuration $C$ of the sided instance \emph{honest}
(\lean{SHonest}) when (a) every delete's target has a visible prior
insert, and (b) one global chain assignment exists: some
$\mathrm{chainOf} : \mathbb{N} \to \mathrm{SChain}$ such that every
insert event $o$ of $C$ has $\mathrm{chainOf}(t_o)$ positive, stored
coordinate $\mathrm{sidedCoordOf}_\Gamma(\mathrm{chainOf}(t_o))$,
and deltas telescoping to the id: $\sum \mathrm{chainOf}(t_o) =
t_o$. Let $\mathrm{SReach}$ be honest reachability at this contract
(Definition~\ref{def:honestreach}). Then every
$\mathrm{SReach}$-reachable configuration is RA-linearizable, per
version, for every ordered prefix code and for \emph{every side
assignment}: sides are payload to convergence. The discharge is the
direct-join route of \S\ref{sec:route} (\lean{s\_join\_at}); the
generation discipline supplies the contract
(\lean{sHonest\_of\_applicable}) and fold canonicity mirrors the
one-sided \lean{e\_fold\_canon} as \lean{s\_fold\_canon}.
\hfill\lean{sided\_embed\_ra\_linearizable3}
\end{theorem}

\noindent \S\ref{sec:mechanization} records the surrounding
mechanization inventory.

\section{Maximal non-interleaving, closed: the traversal theory and
Theorem 9}
\label{sec:theorem9}

The sided kernel of \S\ref{sec:sided} hosts the Fugue family, and the
Weidner--Kleppmann paper (TPDS'25) supplies that family's ordering
specification. This section states the specification natively, presents
the theory that discharges it, and records the closure: all three
conditions of the paper's Definition~4 are kernel-checked for the
FugueMax variant, which is, to our knowledge, the first fully
mechanized maximal non-interleaving theorem for a sequence datatype.
Along the way three findings recur on one theme: \textbf{the dead are
load-bearing}, in stating the specification, in achieving it, and in
proving it.

\paragraph{The specification, natively.} Fix an execution. The
\emph{left origin} of an element is the element directly preceding its
insertion position at the time of insertion (or a start sentinel); its
\emph{right origin} is the element directly following the left origin
\emph{in the list including tombstones} at that moment (or an end
sentinel). The paper's Definition~4 (\emph{maximal non-interleaving})
demands, over all reachable list states:
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=2em]
\item[(1)] \emph{forward}: if $A$ is the left origin of $B$ and $B$
  displays earliest among elements with left origin $A$, then $A$ and
  $B$ are consecutive;
\item[(2)] \emph{backward, with exceptions}: if $B$ is the right origin
  of $A$ and $A$ displays latest among elements with right origin $B$,
  then $A$ and $B$ are consecutive, unless the exception holds: $A$ and
  $B$ have different left origins and some $C$ in the list state sits
  strictly between $\mathrm{lo}(A)$ and $B$ without being a descendant
  of $\mathrm{lo}(A)$ in the left-origin tree;
\item[(3)] \emph{arbitration}: same left and right origins order by
  lower identifier first.
\end{itemize}
Both origin definitions and both ``earliest/latest among'' quantifiers
range over the list \emph{including tombstones}: that is the first of
the three places the dead carry the statement. The conditions are
formalized over reachable configurations of the FugueMax kernel variant
(the recorded origins are the generation policy's, the display order
the kernel's), with the conclusion ``consecutive'' read over the
\emph{visible} document, which is the property an editor's user sees.

\emph{The knowledge model} (the generation-layer fold of
\S\ref{sec:design}, declared here once). A global configuration is a
map $G$ from replica to its list of \emph{generation records}; a
record carries $(\mathrm{ts}, \mathrm{rep}, \mathrm{op},
\mathrm{lo}, \mathrm{ro}, \mathrm{chain})$: the Lamport id, the
issuing replica, the op, the recorded left origin ($0$ = start), the
recorded right origin (the tombstone-visible successor at mint time;
none = end), and the minted birth chain. For the plain Fugue policy
the record list is \lean{Know} with reachability \lean{FugueReach}
(local positional inserts with globally fresh, locally dominant
Lamport ids; positional deletes; pairwise whole-knowledge sync); for
the FugueMax variant, \lean{KnowM} and \lean{MaxReach}, same shape
over the tagged records. The readers, per knowledge state $K$:
$\mathrm{lo}(x)$ and $\mathrm{ro}(x)$ read the minted record
(\lean{mLoOf}, \lean{mRoOf}); $x \prec y$ is ``displays before,
tombstones included,'' the display comparator on the strong-list
keys (\lean{mBefore}); $\mathrm{live}(x)$ is membership in the
visible document (\lean{mLive}); $\mathrm{minted}(K)$ the minted
ids, tombstones included (\lean{mMintedIds}); left-origin ancestry
is the iterated-$\mathrm{lo}$ relation (\lean{mLoDesc}). Plain-Fugue
twins carry g-names (\lean{gLoOf}, \lean{gBefore}, \dots).

\begin{definition}[adapted Definition 4, the quantified form]
\label{def:wkdef4}
Over a knowledge state $K$ (FugueMax readers shown; the plain-Fugue
twins are verbatim over g-readers):
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=2em]
\item[(1)] \emph{forward} (\lean{ForwardNIM}): for all minted $A,
  B$, both live, with $\mathrm{lo}(B) = A$ and $B$ display-earliest
  among minted elements with left origin $A$ (every minted $D \ne B$
  with $\mathrm{lo}(D) = A$ has $B \prec D$): $A$ and $B$ are
  \emph{consecutive}, i.e.\ $A \prec B$ with no \emph{live} $c$
  strictly between (\lean{mConsecutive}).
\item[(2)] \emph{backward, with the exception}
  (\lean{BackwardNIExcM}): for all minted live $A, B$ with
  $\mathrm{ro}(A) = B$ and $A$ display-latest among minted elements
  with right origin $B$: if the exception fails, $A$ and $B$ are
  consecutive. The exception (\lean{BackwardExceptionM}):
  $\mathrm{lo}(A) \ne \mathrm{lo}(B)$, and some \emph{minted} $C$
  (tombstones included) has $\mathrm{lo}(A) \prec C \prec B$ and is
  not a left-origin descendant of $\mathrm{lo}(A)$.
\item[(3)] \emph{arbitration} (\lean{SameOriginLowFirstM}): minted
  live $A, B$ with equal left origins, equal right origins, and
  $A < B$ display $A \prec B$.
\end{itemize}
$\mathrm{MaxNonInterleaving}(K)$ is the conjunction of the three;
the quantified statement
$\mathrm{FugueMaxMaximallyNonInterleaving}(\Gamma)$ demands it at
every replica of every \lean{MaxReach}-reachable $G$. The premise
quantifiers and both origins range over minted elements, tombstones
included; the conclusion is live (the visible document). The
plain-Fugue twin of the exception (\lean{BackwardException})
carries one extra conjunct, a \emph{live} witness: that reading is
refuted below and is retained as the negative control.
\hfill\lean{MaxNonInterleavingM},
\lean{FugueMaxMaximallyNonInterleaving}
\end{definition}

\paragraph{The traversal theory.} The paper's proofs run through a
tree traversal (its Lemma~7: the list order of a forward
non-interleaving algorithm is a depth-first traversal of the
left-origin tree; its Lemma~8 computes origins by walking the
algorithm's own tree). On the embedded-chain family the algorithm's
tree is implicit in the chains, and the marker theorems of
\S\ref{sec:sided} already state that the display order \emph{is} the
in-order rule on chains. What the discharges consume is therefore not
an emission function but an \emph{interval} form of traversal theory
(\code{Sided\_Traversal.lean}): subtree convexity (a chain prefix's
extensions display as one contiguous block around it), the side lemmas
(an extension displays after the node iff its first added entry is
R-headed, before iff L-headed), prefix strip-and-lift, and transitivity
of the chain order. A recursive emission function was designed and
deliberately not built: every consumer needs only the interval lemmas,
and the emission would add a fueled recursion with no downstream
client; the executable emission lives in the Python twin
(\code{traversal\_check.py}), checked against the descending-key sort
on both policies.

The bridge from recorded origins to chain geometry is one shape
invariant, the chain form of the paper's walk-up rule.
\emph{loShape}: every minted element $x$ satisfies
\[
  \chain(x) \;=\; \chain(\mathrm{lo}(x)) \cdot \langle \text{R
  entry}\rangle \cdot \ell s, \qquad \ell s \text{ all-L}.
\]
R mints satisfy it with $\ell s$ empty; for L mints the content is that
the tombstone-visible successor of an anchor with an R-child is itself
an all-L descendant of that R-child, proved by an argmax argument (an R
entry inside the rest would exhibit a minted element displaying between
anchor and successor, beating the successor's maximality; the invariant
and its key lemma are \lean{loShape} and \lean{succ\_R\_desc\_allL},
\code{SidedRGA\_NonInterleaving.lean}). loShape is a
\emph{generation-discipline} fact, not a kernel fact: a forged state
that stores an L entry under the wrong parent displays fine (the total
order does not care) but breaks the walk-up rule, and condition (1)
then genuinely fails; the honesty clause excluding it is precisely that
side and parent come from the policy at mint time, never from op
payload.

\paragraph{The forward discharge.} With loShape in hand, the paper's
``first node the traversal visits among $A$'s right descendants'' is
replaced by a \emph{descent}: if a minted $c$ is an R-headed extension
of $A$, then some minted $D$ with $\mathrm{lo}(D) = A$ displays at or
before $c$ (strong induction on chain length, comparing the two prefix
decompositions of $\chain(c)$). Condition (1) follows: any live $c$
strictly between $A$ and its earliest left-origin child $B$ lands in
$A$'s subtree by convexity, is R-headed by the side lemma, and the
descent produces a $D$ with $\mathrm{lo}(D) = A$ displaying before $B$,
contradicting $B$'s minimality. The one new invariant clause, the
all-L successor shape for L mints, transports through insert, delete,
and sync like the main bundle. Condition (3) was already the positive
twin of the Fugue refutation
(\lean{fuguemax\_same\_origin\_low\_first}: at every replica of every
\lean{MaxReach}-reachable $G$, same-origin pairs display lower id
first).

\begin{theorem}[forward non-interleaving, FugueMax]
\label{thm:fwdmax}
For every ordered prefix code $\Gamma$: every replica of every
\lean{MaxReach}-reachable configuration satisfies condition (1) of
Definition~\ref{def:wkdef4}. No hypotheses beyond reachability;
every invariant consumed is internal. Kernel axioms only.
\hfill\lean{fuguemax\_forward\_ni}
\end{theorem}

Everything in the forward proof never consults the sibling order, and
the same argument discharges the \emph{plain Fugue} policy's forward
condition as stated: the link layer was rebuilt as an external
invariant over an untouched \lean{FugueReach}, with roughly 85
percent of the FugueMax machinery transferring verbatim.

\begin{theorem}[forward non-interleaving, plain Fugue]
\label{thm:fwdfugue}
For every ordered prefix code $\Gamma$: every replica of every
\lean{FugueReach}-reachable configuration satisfies condition (1)
in its g-reader form (\lean{ForwardNI}). Kernel axioms only.
\hfill\lean{fugue\_forward\_ni}
(\code{SidedRGA\_Fugue\_ForwardNI.lean})
\end{theorem}

Plain Fugue's conditions (2) and (3) remain false, as the paper
itself proves, and the separation is machine-checked at reachable
configurations:

\begin{proposition}[the plain-Fugue refutations]
\label{prop:fuguerefuted}
At the unary code:
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=2em]
\item \emph{Condition (3) fails}: on the reachable two-concurrent-%
front-inserts configuration (\lean{GTie}), ids $1$ and $2$ share
both origins and display $[2, 1]$: the kernel's newest-first sibling
order is the exact reverse of the paper's (by its Theorem~10, not
optional) lowest-id-first arbitration. Hence
$\neg\,\mathrm{FugueMaximallyNonInterleaving}(\mathrm{unary})$.
\hfill\lean{fugue\_not\_maximally\_noninterleaving}
\item \emph{Condition (2) fails}, on the paper's own Figure-7
execution (\lean{KFig}): the pair $(A, B) = (7, 4)$ satisfies the
backward premises, the live $6$ sits strictly between, and every
candidate exception witness is refuted pointwise, so
$\neg\,\mathrm{BackwardNIExc}(\mathrm{unary}, K_{\mathrm{Fig}})$
(\lean{fugue\_backward\_gap}); lifted to the full statement at the
reachable configuration as
\lean{fugue\_not\_maximally\_noninterleaving\_backward}. This is
the paper's own Fugue-versus-FugueMax gap, so the separation does
not hinge on the tiebreak clause.
\end{itemize}
Both close by \lean{native\_decide} on the real fold, under the SPOT
convention (\S\ref{sec:design}).
\end{proposition}

\paragraph{The backward condition: the live-witness refutation.} The
Lean statement of the exception, as first adapted, required the witness
$C$ to be \emph{live}. That reading is false, and the countermodel is
the paper's own Figure-7 execution followed by one delete. In that
execution (elements here named by their ids) the display is
$[3, 6, 7, 4, 5]$ with $\mathrm{ro}(6) = 5$ and
$\mathrm{lo}(6) = 3 \ne \mathrm{lo}(5)$; delete $4$, so the visible
document is $[3, 6, 7, 5]$. The premise pair $(A, B) = (6, 5)$ holds:
$6$ is the only minted element with right origin $5$, hence trivially
display-latest among them. The pair is not consecutive: $7$ sits
between. The exception's first half holds. But no \emph{live} witness
exists: the only elements strictly between $\mathrm{lo}(6) = 3$ and $5$
are $6$ and $7$ (both descendants of $3$ in the left-origin tree) and
the \emph{dead} $4$, which is exactly the $C$ the paper's own proof
produces for this execution. The paper's Lemma~5 quantifies $C$ over
the list state including tombstones, so the corrected definition drops
the one liveness conjunct and nothing else. Second load-bearing role of
the dead: without them the specification itself is not a theorem of
FugueMax (the refutation fired on the directed families and on 3 of
4500 randomized delete-enabled states; the earlier insert-only sweeps
could never see it). Two remarks. The corrected reading is strictly
paper-faithful and strictly weaker as a guarantee: a pair excused by a
dead witness is a pair the visible document may interleave; that is
forced, not chosen. And the \emph{conclusion} keeps its live reading
(no live element strictly between): the visible-document property is
the one proved. Per this project's standing rule, the goal was
corrected in the open, forced by a countermodel and matching the
paper's own quantification, never silently renamed.

\paragraph{Replacing causal minimality.} The paper's condition-(2)
proof exhibits its exception witness by choosing an in-between element
``not causally later than any other,'' then consumes that minimality
three times. A reachability-invariant formulation cannot replay this:
the invariant carries per-record facts about the current knowledge, not
a well-founded causal order to minimize over. The replacement is
geometric. Three mint-time clauses on R-mint records (a supplementary
invariant bundle, transported exactly like the existing ones): a
root-anchored R mint records no successor; a recorded successor
displays after the anchor; and the two \emph{RSucc} clauses pin, for an
R mint whose anchor is itself an L or R mint, a stable witness (the
anchor's parent, a later L-sibling, or the anchor's own recorded
successor) whose key bounds the recorded successor's (the \lean{RBk}
bundle, \code{SidedRGA\_Backward.lean}). With those, a
deterministic, state-definable witness function replaces the causal
choice: it inspects the in-between element's chain, walks at most two
steps (four routes, exercised as $\alpha$ 253 times, $\beta_1$ 22,
$\beta_0$-direct 2, $\beta_0$-ladder 2 across 4500 validation states,
the $\beta_0$ routes reachable only by directed construction), and
returns a minted element strictly between $\mathrm{lo}(A)$ and $B$ that
the left-origin walk can never reach. The methodological point: a
\emph{universal} fact about the minter's knowledge does not transport
to later states, but an \emph{existential} witness does, because minted
records, chains, and keys are stable under knowledge growth. Third
load-bearing role of the dead: the constructed witness may itself be a
tombstone (it is, in the Figure-7 family), and the intermediate lemmas
are deliberately stated over minted, not live, elements so the ladder
case can call them.

\paragraph{The capstone.} The backward discharge
(\code{SidedRGA\_Backward.lean}, \lean{fuguemax\_backward\_ni})
closes the arc.

\begin{theorem}[Theorem 9, mechanized]
\label{thm:t9}
For every ordered prefix code $\Gamma$:
$\mathrm{FugueMaxMaximallyNonInterleaving}(\Gamma)$, that is, all
three conditions of Definition~\ref{def:wkdef4} hold at every
replica of every \lean{MaxReach}-reachable FugueMax configuration.
No hypotheses beyond reachability and the code; kernel-clean on
$\{\lean{propext}, \lean{Classical.choice}, \lean{Quot.sound}\}$.
Assembled from conditions (1) and (3)
(Theorem~\ref{thm:fwdmax}, \lean{fuguemax\_same\_origin\_low\_first})
and the backward condition (\lean{fuguemax\_backward\_ni}) by the
reduction theorem \lean{fuguemax\_theorem9\_of\_backward}.
\hfill\lean{fuguemax\_maximally\_noninterleaving}
\end{theorem}

\noindent The reduction theorem and the forward file recompiled with
zero edits after the definition correction, as the design phase
predicted. SPOTs pin the witness values on all four delete-bearing
directed variants, PASS and FAIL shaped.

\paragraph{The convergence capstone, and the TagsOK finding.} Ordering
intent is only half the variant's bill. The FugueMax realization writes
records over a \emph{different block format} (R entries carry the
mint-time successor's key as an order-reversing tag before the
complemented delta code), so RA-linearizability for the tagged datatype
was owed separately and is paid: same canonical sorted-list state,
same mergeable-queue route, fold canonicity mirrored as
\lean{f\_fold\_canon}. The honesty contract needs one clause the sided
contract did not:

\begin{definition}[wellformed tags]
\label{def:tagsok}
A right-origin tag $t$ is \emph{wellformed} when it is the end tag
$\langle 0 \rangle$, or a real key: $t = (h \cdot c) \cdot \langle 3
\rangle$ with head $h \notin \{0, 3\}$, $h \le 5$, and every symbol
of $c$ marker-free and bounded ($\ne 3$, $\le 5$). The obligation
applies to R entries (L entries carry no tag);
$\mathrm{TagsOK}(ch)$ demands it of every entry of the chain.
\hfill\lean{TagOK}, \lean{TagsOK}
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[FugueMax convergence]
\label{thm:fmralin}
Call a configuration $C$ of the tagged instance \emph{honest}
(\lean{FMHonest}) when (a) every delete's target has a visible prior
insert, and (b) one global chain assignment exists: some
$\mathrm{chainOf}$ with, for every insert event $o$ of $C$, the chain
positive, its tags wellformed
($\mathrm{TagsOK}(\mathrm{chainOf}(t_o))$, the new clause), the
stored coordinate equal to the chain's tagged coordinate
(\lean{fmCoordOf}), and deltas telescoping to the id. With
$\mathrm{FMReach}$ honest reachability at this contract
(Definition~\ref{def:honestreach}): every
$\mathrm{FMReach}$-reachable configuration is RA-linearizable, per
version, for every ordered prefix code. The right-origin tags ops
carry are payload to this theorem, exactly as sides were to
Theorem~\ref{thm:sidedcapstone}.
\hfill\lean{fuguemax\_ra\_linearizable3}
(\code{SidedRGA\_FugueMax\_RA\_Lin.lean})
\end{theorem}

\noindent The finding worth a name: \textbf{the right-origin tag is
convergence-load-bearing}, not merely intent-load-bearing. Unique
decodability of the variant coordinate format
(\lean{fmCoordOf\_inj}), which key injectivity and hence fold
canonicity run through, is only unambiguous on wellformed tags: the
generation layer supplies $\mathrm{TagsOK}$ for free, but a
hand-forged tag would break convergence itself, not just the display
intent. Convergence is otherwise insensitive to what the coordinates
contain, exactly as the policy/datatype split predicts: coordinates
are injectively minted, immutable, and consumed only through key
comparisons.

\subsection{Where the family sits in the published table}
\label{sec:table1}

The Weidner--Kleppmann paper's Table~1 is the nearest published designs
$\times$ anomalies matrix: roughly ten list CRDTs classified by hand
against the interleaving conditions. The placement this repository
contributes is not a new row but a change in how rows are occupied:
\textbf{one kernel datatype, verified once, whose generation policy
selects the row, each placement by theorem}.

\begin{center}\small
\begin{tabular}{@{}lll@{}}
\toprule
policy / variant & row occupied & by which theorem \\
\midrule
always-R & RGA & fragment theorem + compaction theorem: \\
 & & read-equal to the published RGA, \\
 & & anomalies \emph{included} (L19 inherited by theorem) \\
Fugue policy & Fugue & forward NI as stated
  (\lean{fugue\_forward\_ni}); \\
 & & \emph{not} maximally non-interleaving: the paper's \\
 & & own separation, machine-checked on its \\
 & & Figure-7 execution \\
FugueMax variant & FugueMax & Theorem~9 mechanized \\
 & & (\lean{fuguemax\_maximally\_noninterleaving}); \\
 & & convergence separately
  (\lean{fuguemax\_ra\_linearizable3}) \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

\noindent Two readings of the table. First, the one-sided
embedded-chain RGA occupies the RGA row \emph{by theorem}: the
compaction theorem (\S\ref{sec:mechanization}) makes it the published
RGA's reads verbatim, so every property Table~1 records for RGA,
favorable or not, transfers; the design does not get to keep the good
rows and disown L19. Second, the convergence column is
policy-independent (sides and tags are payload to the merge; the sided
capstone quantifies over every side assignment) while the interleaving
column is per-policy, which is the policy/datatype split of
\S\ref{sec:sided} rendered as table structure. What no policy can do is
leave the family's \emph{delete-insensitive} rows altogether: display
keys are immutable and deletion is pure removal for every policy, so a
policy selects among the retention-faithful orders and nothing else.
The row a splice-based design would occupy is unreachable by policy,
and the next section proves that unreachability is not an accident of
this kernel but an information-theoretic fact.

\section{The chain price: what delete must remember}
\label{sec:chainprice}

The conservation law of \S\ref{sec:design} (the sort key must retain
dead ancestors' timestamps) was stated as an empirical regularity of
the design sweep: every design that forgets them has a named
countermodel. This section upgrades it to a characterization. The
question that forced it (KC, 2026-07-18): the embed RGA works, but its
proof rests on keeping a deleted node's memory alive inside live
descendants' coordinates; could a \emph{sibling-edge} implementation,
which splices a dead node out and lifts its children a level up, be
shown equivalent to the embed RGA with no such retention? The answer is
no, by a four-event fooling pair
(\lean{Refutations/SiblingSplice\_Fooling.lean}), and the counterexample
names exactly the information the splice destroys.

\paragraph{The mechanism.} In the embed order, a lifted child displays
at its dead ancestor's rank: the subtree keyed by the ancestor's stamp
holds its \emph{slot}, and the child sits in that slot. Splice-and-lift
erases the ancestor and re-ranks the child among its new siblings by
the child's \emph{own} stamp. Whenever a concurrent sibling's stamp
lies strictly between the dead ancestor's and the child's, the two
rankings disagree, and no trace of the disagreement survives in the
spliced state.

\paragraph{The fooling pair, walked.} Timestamps only; slot keys
shown. Both worlds share the LCA and the concurrent branch $B$; only
branch $A$ differs, and its two variants land on \emph{equal} spliced
states.

\begin{center}\small
\begin{tabular}{@{}lll@{}}
\toprule
 & world 1 & world 2 \\
\midrule
shared LCA & $\ins\ a$ (stamp 1) at the root & same \\
shared branch $B$ & $\ins\ c$ (stamp 2) at the root, & same \\
 & concurrent with all of $A$ & \\
branch $A$ & $\ins\ b$ (5) \emph{under} $a$; $\del\ a$ (6) &
  $\del\ a$ (4); $\ins\ b$ (5) at the root \\
spliced $A$-state & the one-node forest $[\,b(5)\,]$ &
  $[\,b(5)\,]$: \textbf{equal}, machine-checked \\
$b$'s slot key (embed) & $1$ (dead $a$'s slot) & $5$ (its own) \\
owed read after merge & $[\,c,\, b\,]$ ($1 < 2$) &
  $[\,b,\, c\,]$ ($5 > 2$) \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

\noindent The knob is the stamp crossing $a < c < b$ with $b$ and $c$
on \emph{different} branches, and it rides on concurrency: $B$ saw only
the LCA, so its stamp $2$ is Lamport-honest, and every stamp on $A$
exceeds everything its issuer observed ($1 < 5 < 6$; $1 < 4 < 5$).
Sequentially the crossing is impossible (a later insert has a larger
stamp than everything visible, the dead anchor included), which is why
no sequential test can see this and why the pair had to be built on a
version DAG. The impossibility is then three lines: a ternary merge
function on the splice representation receives \emph{identical}
arguments in the two worlds (the LCA and branch $B$ verbatim, the
$A$-states by the machine-checked equality) and owes \emph{different}
answers, so there is none.

\begin{proposition}[the fooling pair: no merge function over spliced
states]
\label{prop:foolpair}
Let $w_L$ and $w_B$ be the spliced LCA and branch-$B$ states,
$w_{1A}$, $w_{2A}$ the two worlds' branch-$A$ states, and $\rho_1$,
$\rho_2$ the two worlds' embed histories (unary code; payloads $101 /
105 / 102$ for $a / b / c$; $\mathrm{read}$ is the element projection
of the fold). The supporting pins, expected values hand-derived:
\[
  w_{1A} = w_{2A}, \qquad
  \mathrm{read}(\mathrm{eFold}\ \rho_1) = [102, 105], \qquad
  \mathrm{read}(\mathrm{eFold}\ \rho_2) = [105, 102]
\]
(\lean{states\_equal\_A}, \lean{embed\_read\_w1},
\lean{embed\_read\_w2}; the FAIL pin \lean{embed\_reads\_differ} is
the inequality of the two reads). The impossibility, quantified over
\emph{every} merge function on the splice representation: for all
$f : \mathrm{St} \to \mathrm{St} \to \mathrm{St} \to
\mathrm{List}\ \mathbb{N}$,
\[
  \neg\bigl(\, f\ w_L\ w_{1A}\ w_B = \mathrm{read}(\mathrm{eFold}\
  \rho_1) \;\wedge\; f\ w_L\ w_{2A}\ w_B =
  \mathrm{read}(\mathrm{eFold}\ \rho_2) \,\bigr).
\]
The proof is the three lines above; the pins close under the SPOT
convention (fold~(iii), \S\ref{sec:design}).
\hfill\lean{sibling\_splice\_no\_merge\_function}
\end{proposition}

\begin{finding}[the retention characterization]
The fooling pair (Proposition~\ref{prop:foolpair}) is a \emph{lower}
bound: any state representation that forgets a lifted child's dead
slot key admits the pair, so no merge over it can reproduce the
embed's reads. The capstone is the matching \emph{upper} bound:
keeping the whole dead chain suffices for everything
(\lean{embed\_ra\_linearizable3}, the compaction theorem, the
sequential spec, delete-order preservation;
\S\ref{sec:mechanization}). Between them: \emph{the chain of dead slot
keys is exactly the information content of delete}. Deletion's
semantic content in this family is ``the subtree keeps its slot,'' and
the slot chain is both necessary and sufficient to honor it. What
remains negotiable is only \emph{where} the memory lives (coordinate
prefixes here, carried spine paths in the validated sibling-edge
design, tombstone records in the published RGA: three encodings of the
same bits) and \emph{how many bits} it costs (\S\ref{sec:encoding},
\S\ref{sec:storage}); whether it lives is not. This is the pairing of
two mechanized bounds, not a single Lean name.
\end{finding}

\paragraph{The two-by-two.} Retention crossed with display rank sorts
the four named designs of this document's arc:

\begin{center}\small
\setlength{\tabcolsep}{4pt}%
\begin{tabular}{@{}llll@{}}
\toprule
design & retains the dead chain? & lifted child ranks by &
  verdict \\
\midrule
embed RGA & yes: coordinate prefix & dead ancestor's slot key &
  capstone + seq.\ spec \\
RGA$_\dagger$ (tombstoned) & yes: tombstone records &
  dead ancestor's slot key & read-equal to embed \\
 & & & (compaction theorem) \\
rehoming (flat TF) & truncated at each rehoming &
  own stamp, after the climb & RA-lin; seq.\ spec \\
 & & & \emph{refuted} (Part~III) \\
Shesha (splice) & no & own stamp, after the splice &
  no merge function \\
 & & & (the fooling pair) \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

\noindent Reading down the rows: full retention with slot-key display
gives the two read-equal designs (tombstone-free and tombstoned differ
only in encoding); truncating the chain at each rehoming preserves
convergence but breaks the sequential specification; discarding it
altogether leaves nothing for any merge function to work with. The
verdicts degrade exactly as the retained information does.

\paragraph{The delete-sensitivity separation.} \S\ref{sec:table1}
showed the generation policy selecting rows of the interleaving table.
Here is what no policy can select.

\begin{theorem}[delete never moves survivors]
\label{thm:delfree}
For every ordered prefix code $\Gamma$, every state $s$, and every
delete operation: $\mathrm{eUpdate}\ \Gamma\ s\ (t, r, \del\ x)$ is a
\emph{sublist} of $s$ (the survivors, in unchanged order). No
hypotheses: no honesty, no reachability, no policy. The proof is one
line (\code{List.filter\_sublist}); the theorem carries the separation
because it is unconditional.
\hfill\lean{eUpdate\_del\_sublist}
\end{theorem}

\noindent For every policy over the retaining kernel, display keys are
immutable and deletion is record removal, so a delete never moves
survivors (Theorem~\ref{thm:delfree} is policy-free): the whole family
is \emph{delete-insensitive}. Shesha's
row is delete-\emph{sensitive} (the splice moves survivors), and the
fooling pair shows the sensitivity is not a stylistic difference but an
information-theoretic one: the splice state cannot support the
retention family's reads at all. Policies span the interleaving axis;
nothing spans the retention axis. The two degrees of freedom are
orthogonal, and only one of them is free.

\paragraph{No coding escapes.} Could a cleverer coordinate code rescue
the splice? No, in both directions. Downward: the impossibility
theorem quantifies over \emph{every} merge function on the splice
states, and an encoding is a function of the state, so any coded splice
inherits the fooling pair verbatim (the two worlds' encodings are equal
bit for bit, being encodings of equal states). Upward: the positive
theorems are parametric in the code (\S\ref{sec:mechanization}: one
proof, three instantiated codes), so the entire coding freedom provably
lives in what retention \emph{costs}, never in whether it happens.
Coding moves bits; it cannot manufacture information the state has
discarded. That is the chain price: the storage layer of the next
section spends considerable effort making the price small, and none
trying to evade it.

\section{The storage layer: re-coding, compaction, and the run table}
\label{sec:storage}

The datatype's costs were settled upstairs (\S\ref{sec:encoding}: pay
the entropy of the anchor gaps at mint time) and its obligations
downstairs (\S\ref{sec:chainprice}: the dead chain must be retained).
This section is the layer between: what a running system does about
accumulated weight. The arc has three rungs, and stating them up front
keeps the bookkeeping honest. \emph{Chains carry the semantics}: birth
constants, never edited, the objects of every correctness theorem.
\emph{Runs carry the representation}: an invertible re-encoding of the
current state, valid with no hypotheses at all. \emph{Stability is
needed only for forgetting}: epoch maps that genuinely discard dead
history rewrite geometry, and rewriting is safe only at a settled cut
(\S\ref{sec:stability}). Every measurement below reproduces from the
cited harnesses, and each table's accounting model is stated with it.

\subsection{Re-coding at a settled cut: the theorem cluster}
\label{sec:recoding}

\paragraph{The epoch problem.} Coordinates are birth constants and
never shrink: a live record whose birth chain passed through nine
deleted ancestors carries all nine dead codewords as a prefix forever.
Tombstone-freedom removed the dead \emph{records}; their
\emph{codewords} survive inside every descendant, so long editing
accumulates coordinate weight proportional to total history, not to the
live document. A GC epoch should re-code: assign live records fresh,
short coordinates inducing the same display order, and let new mints
extend the fresh coordinates.

\paragraph{Lazy translation, and why it is the only nontrivial
formulation.} Replicas cannot switch codes in lockstep: a message
minted against old coordinates may be in flight while the receiver has
already compacted. The model is therefore \emph{lazy translation}: the
re-map is a pure function on coordinates, applied once to the state at
rest and to each incoming record or op minted in the old code (the op's
carried anchor prefix is translated; its delta codeword is not
touched). Formally, fix a set $S$ of \emph{stable} coordinates,
downward closed under the ancestor relation. Downward closure is not a
design choice but a fact about cuts: a coordinate's proper
codeword-prefixes are exactly its ancestors' coordinates, ancestors
causally precede their descendants, and any causally downward-closed
notion of stable therefore contains the ancestors of everything it
contains. Given a compaction $\rho$ on $S$, the induced re-map factors
every coordinate at the cut:
\[
  \hat\rho(c) \;=\; \rho(\mathrm{stab}\ c) \,\|\, \mathrm{rest}\ c,
\]
where $\mathrm{stab}\ c$ is the longest stable prefix (it is exactly
one chain prefix, again by downward closure) and the unstable tail is
kept verbatim. The theorems consume two hypotheses, both relativized to
the coordinates \emph{at hand} (convention~(v), \S\ref{sec:design}),
never to all bit strings; injectivity is free. The Lean bundle
(\code{EmbedRGA\_Recoding.lean}, payload- and code-generic,
kernel-clean):

\begin{definition}[the stable-prefix re-map bundle]
\label{def:spm}
A \emph{stable-prefix map} for $\Gamma$ is $F = (f, \mathrm{Rest},
\mathrm{MintAt}, \mathrm{ext}, \mathrm{ord})$: a coordinate map $f$,
the at-rest domain $\mathrm{Rest}$ (the cut state's records), the
declared beyond-cut mints $\mathrm{MintAt}$, and two laws:
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=2em]
\item[(H3)] \emph{extension commuting, guarded by} $\mathrm{MintAt}$:
  $\mathrm{MintAt}\ \pi\ d$ implies $f(\pi \cdot
  \Gamma.\mathrm{enc}(d)) = f(\pi) \cdot \Gamma.\mathrm{enc}(d)$;
\item[(H2)] \emph{order preservation on the domain}: for $c$, $c'$ at
  hand, $\mathrm{keyLt}(\mathrm{key}(f\,c))\,(\mathrm{key}(f\,c')) =
  \mathrm{keyLt}(\mathrm{key}\ c)\,(\mathrm{key}\ c')$, a Boolean
  equation carrying both directions of the display comparator.
\end{itemize}
``At hand'' ($F.\mathrm{Dom}\ c$) is $\mathrm{Rest}\ c \vee \exists
\pi\, d,\ \mathrm{MintAt}\ \pi\ d \wedge c = \pi \cdot
\Gamma.\mathrm{enc}(d)$. There is no injectivity field.
\hfill\lean{StablePrefixMap}, \lean{StablePrefixMap.Dom}
\end{definition}

\begin{lemma}[H1 is derived]
\label{lem:spmh1}
For $c$, $c'$ at hand with $f\,c = f\,c'$: $c = c'$. (H2 at the pair,
plus totality and irreflexivity of $\mathrm{keyLt}$ and injectivity of
$\mathrm{key}$: a strict first-difference order admits no two-sided
tie except equality.)
\hfill\lean{StablePrefixMap.injOn}
\end{lemma}

\noindent Writing $\mathrm{remap}_F$ for the translation of records,
states, and ops by $F.f$ (\lean{eRemapSt}, \lean{eRemapOp}: an op's
carried anchor prefix is translated; its delta codeword and deletes
are untouched):

\begin{theorem}[T1: cut-parametric fold congruence]
\label{thm:recode1}
For a stable-prefix map $F$, cut state $s$, and continuation $\tau$
with $s$ at hand and $\tau$ declared at $F$:
\[
  \mathrm{applySeq}\ (\mathrm{remap}_F\ s)\
  (\tau.\mathrm{map}\ \mathrm{remap}_F)
  \;=\; \mathrm{remap}_F\ (\mathrm{applySeq}\ s\ \tau) :
\]
folding the translated continuation over the translated cut state is
the translation of the untranslated fold.
\hfill\lean{eRecode\_applySeq}
\end{theorem}

\begin{proposition}[the global-collapse negative]
\label{prop:globalcollapse}
One might hope to state T1 over the whole history (map every op ever
issued, re-fold from the initial state); that statement forces H3 at
every historical mint. If a coordinate map $f$ satisfies H3 at
\emph{every} positive mint ($\forall \pi\, d$, $1 \le d$ implies
$f(\pi \cdot \Gamma.\mathrm{enc}(d)) = f(\pi) \cdot
\Gamma.\mathrm{enc}(d)$), then for every positive chain $\mathit{ch}$
\[
  f(\mathrm{coordOf}_\Gamma\ \mathit{ch}) \;=\; f(\varepsilon) \cdot
  \mathrm{coordOf}_\Gamma\ \mathit{ch} :
\]
a constant-prefix prepend, which compacts \emph{nothing}. Re-coding is
inherently an epoch operation on states, not a history rewrite; the
lazy, cut-parametric formulation is the only nontrivial one. A
finding, mechanized; kernel-clean (a theorem over all such maps, not a
SPOT).
\hfill\lean{eRecode\_ext\_global\_collapse}
\end{proposition}

\begin{theorem}[T2: reads identical]
\label{thm:recode2}
Under exactly the hypotheses of T1 ($s$ at hand, $\tau$ declared at
$F$), the query of the translated run equals the untranslated one:
\[
  \mathrm{query}\bigl(\mathrm{applySeq}\ (\mathrm{remap}_F\ s)\
  (\tau.\mathrm{map}\ \mathrm{remap}_F)\bigr)
  \;=\; \mathrm{query}\bigl(\mathrm{applySeq}\ s\ \tau\bigr) :
\]
compression is invisible to every observer.
\hfill\lean{eRecode\_reads\_identical}
\end{theorem}

\noindent The contentful half of T2 hides in T1: the translated side
re-sorts by the \emph{new} keys, so equality of reads is exactly the
statement that the new keys induce the old order, H2 doing its job.
The negative control earns its keep here: an injective, H3-satisfying
but order-breaking map genuinely flips the read (the \code{badMap}
FAIL companion of the file's SPOT), pinning H2 as the load-bearing
hypothesis.

\begin{theorem}[T3: the capstone's witness statement transports]
\label{thm:recode3}
Hypothesis: $\mathrm{EReach}\ \Gamma\ C$ (honest reachability,
\S\ref{sec:mechanization}); no at-hand hypothesis. For every
registered version $C.\mathrm{ver}\ v = (s, E_v)$ there is a witness
enumeration $\pi$ with: $\pi$ a permutation of $E_v$
(\lean{listPermOf}); $\pi$ respecting the delivery order of $C$'s
core; and
\[
  \mathrm{remap}_F\ s = \mathrm{remap}_F\ (\mathrm{eFold}_\Gamma\
  \pi), \qquad
  \mathrm{query}(\mathrm{remap}_F\ s) =
  \mathrm{query}(\mathrm{eFold}_\Gamma\ \pi).
\]
Canonicity, sortedness, and version-level sortedness transport too,
each under its own side conditions: canonicity needs $\mathrm{EWf}$ of
both enumerations, a membership condition, and declared mints
(\lean{eRecode\_fold\_canon}); sortedness needs the at-hand hypothesis
of convention~(v) (\lean{eRecode\_sorted}); version-level sortedness
needs \emph{EHonest in addition to EReach} (honesty at the endpoint,
which honest reachability alone does not supply) plus at-hand
(\lean{eRecode\_version\_sorted}).
\hfill\lean{eRecode\_ra\_transport}
\end{theorem}

\noindent Honest scope note. The transport is at the level of states
and witnesses, not configurations: the re-coded history is \emph{not}
itself an honest configuration of the same datatype (compacted
coordinates deliberately forget dead chain segments, so the
chain-generation honesty clause fails for them). Re-basing honesty
across the epoch, so that the capstone re-invokes natively in the new
epoch, was the deferred obligation of this cluster's first revision
and is now closed: the $(\ast)$ cluster of \S\ref{sec:compactED}
(Definition~\ref{def:caf} through Corollary~\ref{cor:itwoepoch});
what stays open downstream is the multi-replica half (agreement on a
common cut, Open Question~\ref{oq:epochdag}). The cut hypothesis
itself is discharged by \S\ref{sec:stability}'s SettledAt gate:
\lean{eRecode\_settled\_bridge} (in
\code{EmbedRGA\_Stability\_Bridge.lean}, with the stability import,
not in the re-coding file) packages T1${}+{}$T2 at a settled cut
against the named residue \lean{AnchorsFactorBeyond}, and is
superseded by the residue-free honest form
(Theorem~\ref{thm:bridgehonest}).

\paragraph{Measurement 1: the microbenchmark.} Directed, hand-computed
worked example (unary code; history: insert $a$ at the root, $b$ under
$a$, $c$ under $b$, delete $a$ and $b$; then, beyond the cut, insert
$d$ under $c$ and $e$ at the root). Accounting: the numbers are
\emph{coordinate bit lengths of live records}, order metadata alone,
identical convention to the entropy tables of \S\ref{sec:encoding};
characters are excluded.

\begin{center}\small
\begin{tabular}{@{}lcc@{}}
\toprule
live record & original coordinate & re-coded \\
\midrule
$c$ (two dead codewords carried) & 6 bits & 2 bits \\
$d$ (minted beyond the cut, under $c$) & 10 bits & 6 bits \\
$e$ (minted beyond the cut, at the root) & 8 bits & 8 bits \\
\midrule
total & 24 bits & 16 bits \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

\noindent Reads are identical on both sides (hand-checked and pinned as
SPOTs); the dead-chain share of $c$ and $d$'s bits is gone entirely,
and $e$, which never passed through the dead region, is untouched. The
same harness's delete-heavy directed case (a 200-deep chain, binary
code) drops live coordinate weight from 200 bits to 1 bit, the
\textbf{200$\times$} figure quoted in the arc's motivation; 500
randomized histories (random cut, two replicas forked beyond the cut
with concurrent ops and a merge at the end) pass reads-identical and
the T1 record-for-record correspondence at every step, and the
order-breaking negative control flips the read exactly as predicted.

\subsection{The verified concrete compaction, and what real traces say}
\label{sec:compactED}

\paragraph{compactEliasDelta.} The theorem cluster is parametric in the
re-map; the concrete v1 compaction instantiates it
(\code{EmbedRGA\_CompactEliasDelta.lean}): \emph{(a) drop dead ranges}
(a stable chain is carried forward iff a live descendant or a declared
in-flight coordinate passes through it; everything else is dropped and
its sibling rank reclaimed) and \emph{(b) rank-renumber} (in each
sibling group the surviving deltas are renumbered to $1..k$
order-isomorphically and re-encoded with the same ordered prefix code).
Its H2 discharge runs the per-group order isomorphism through the
chain-lex theorem, with renumbered ranks dominated by Lamport-fresh
future deltas; the headline discharges everything at a settled cut of
an honest configuration.

\begin{theorem}[the concrete compaction preserves every read at a
settled cut]
\label{thm:compacted}
Let $C$ be a configuration at the Elias-$\delta$ code with
(i)~$\mathrm{EReach}\ C$ (honest reachability,
\S\ref{sec:mechanization}), (ii)~$\mathrm{GenHonest}$ at
$\mathrm{eApplicable}$ (the generation discipline), and
(iii)~$\mathrm{CausalPastEnumerable}\ C$; let (iv)~$C.\mathrm{ver}\ v
= (s, E_v)$ be a registered version with
(v)~$\mathrm{SettledAtOn}\ C\ E_v\ S$ a settled cut
(\S\ref{sec:stability}). Let $\mathit{tree}$, $\mathit{live}$,
$\mathit{inflight}$ be the compactor's inputs with
(vi)~every $\mathit{tree}$ chain positive ($h_{\mathrm{tpos}}$);
(vii)~$\mathit{tree}$ exactly the settled inserts' chains, in both
directions ($h_{\mathrm{Tree}}$: every settled insert's coordinate is
some $\mathit{tree}$ chain's coordinate; $h_{\mathrm{TreeS}}$: every
$\mathit{tree}$ chain is some settled insert's);
(viii)~$h_{\mathrm{Live}}$: a $\mathit{tree}$ chain whose insert no
delete of $S$ targets carries $\mathit{live} = \mathrm{true}$; and
(ix)~$h_{\mathrm{Inflight}}$: every insert of $E_v$ escaping $S$ is
declared in $\mathit{inflight}$ with its (positive) chain. Then there
is a stable-prefix map $F$ with
\[
  F.f = \mathrm{fStab}\ \bigl(\mathrm{rED}\ (\mathrm{ePruneKeep}\
  \mathit{tree}\ \mathit{live}\ \mathit{inflight})\
  \mathit{inflight}\bigr)\ (\mathrm{ePruneKeep}\ \mathit{tree}\
  \mathit{live}\ \mathit{inflight})
\]
(drop dead ranges, rank-renumber outside \lean{skipAt}-guarded groups,
re-encode) such that every continuation $\tau$ of events existing
beyond the cut ($o \in C.\mathrm{events}$, $o \notin E_v$) satisfies
both the T1 fold congruence and T2 reads-identical of
\S\ref{sec:recoding}.
\hfill\lean{compactEliasDelta\_settled\_reads}
\end{theorem}

\begin{remark}
Display compressions, for the record: the mechanized statement phrases
(vii)--(ix) per operation, over $\mathrm{eIsIns}\ o = \mathrm{true}$
with coordinate matching $\mathrm{coordOf}\ k = \mathrm{eCoord}\ o$;
each per-operation quantifier there additionally ranges over $o \in
C.\mathrm{events}$ (a conjunct the display drops: it is redundant
under hypothesis~(i), which bounds every registered version's events
by $C.\mathrm{events}$ through $\mathrm{GoodConfig3}$, but it is
present in the mechanized statement); and (viii)'s undeleted clause
compares payloads, $\forall \mathit{dl} \in S$, $\mathit{dl} \neq
\del(o.1)$ read at the payload of $\mathit{dl}$. The theorem is the
Elias-$\delta$ instantiation of the code-parametric engine
\lean{compactRanked\_settled\_reads}, whose hypothesis list is
identical.
\end{remark}

\paragraph{The in-flight wrinkle, pinned both ways.} Renumbering a
sibling group is only an order isomorphism against the deltas it can
see. If an op minted in the old code is still in flight with a delta in
that group, dense renumbering can slide a surviving sibling past it:
the FAIL SPOT pins exactly this (an in-flight root insert; the
unguarded renumbering sends a surviving delta $9 \mapsto 2$, and the
delivered op lands on the wrong side: the pinned read flips). The v1
resolution is the guard \lean{skipAt}: sibling groups with a known
in-flight child delta are skipped this epoch. Both pins are
hand-derived, PASS and FAIL shaped.

\begin{proposition}[the in-flight wrinkle, pinned both ways]
\label{prop:inflight}
On the file's directed cut (root sibling group $\{1,2,3,4,9\}$,
survivor $4$, dead-but-kept $9$; an epoch-0 in-flight root insert with
chain $[6]$, payload $110$; hand-derived control read $[106, 110,
104]$):
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=2em]
\item[FAIL] the \emph{unguarded} dense renumbering (the tempting
degenerate, \lean{rED} fed an empty in-flight list) yields
\[
  \mathrm{read}(\text{unguarded}) = [110, 106, 104] \;\neq\;
  [106, 110, 104] = \mathrm{read}(\text{control}) :
\]
$9 \mapsto 2$ slides the compacted survivor under the in-flight $6$
and the late op jumps to the front
(\lean{unguarded\_flips\_order});
\item[PASS] the \emph{guarded} construction skips exactly the root
group (\lean{skip\_guard}: $\mathrm{skipAt} = \mathrm{true}$ there,
$\mathrm{false}$ at the in-flight-free $[9]$ group), keeps the read
pinned at $[106, 110, 104]$, and still compresses: coordinate weight
$19$ against the control's $23$
(\lean{guarded\_skips\_group}).
\end{itemize}
\hfill\lean{unguarded\_flips\_order}, \lean{guarded\_skips\_group}
\end{proposition}

\paragraph{AnchorsFactorBeyond: the residue is a theorem.} The bridge
from SettledAt to the cluster left one named residue: every op minted
with the cut in its causal past must carry an anchor prefix that
factors at the map. This is \emph{not} dischargeable from bare honest
reachability (countermodel: the chain-generation clause constrains the
minted coordinate, not the carried split, so a dishonest split
satisfies it while defeating the factorization). It \emph{is} a theorem
of the generation discipline: under the framework's honest generation
(the same hypotheses the capstone's honesty discharge consumes), every
insert's carried prefix is the stored coordinate of an actual anchor
record in the fold of its causal past, so the factorization sits on a
codeword boundary by construction, and the bridge is restated
residue-free.

\begin{theorem}[the residue is a theorem of the discipline]
\label{thm:anchorsfactor}
Call $(\pi, \delta)$ a \emph{canonical chain-aligned mint beyond the
cut} $S$ (\lean{ChainMintBeyond}) when $1 \le \delta$ and $\pi =
\mathrm{coordOf}_\Gamma(\mathit{ch}_A)$ for a positive chain
$\mathit{ch}_A$, witnessed by an actual insert event $o \in
C.\mathrm{events}$ with $S$ entirely in its causal past ($\forall a
\in S$, $\mathrm{vis}\ a\ o$) and $\mathrm{chainOf}(o.1) =
\mathit{ch}_A \cdot \delta$. Hypotheses: (i)~$\mathrm{EAnchored}\
\Gamma\ C\ \mathrm{chainOf}$ (the anchor certificate the honesty
discharge produces); (ii)~$F.\mathrm{MintAt}$ contains every canonical
chain-aligned mint beyond $S$. Conclusion:
$\mathrm{AnchorsFactorBeyond}\ \Gamma\ F\ C\ S$, i.e.\ every op minted
with the whole cut in its causal past carries an anchor prefix that
factors at the map. The containment (ii) is definitional for the
stage-2 construction; the residue is a theorem of the anchored
geometry, not an extra hypothesis.
\hfill\lean{anchorsFactorBeyond\_of\_anchored}
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[the bridge, residue-free]
\label{thm:bridgehonest}
Hypotheses: a stable-prefix map $F$; $\mathrm{GenHonest}$ at
$\mathrm{eApplicable}$; $\mathrm{CausalPastEnumerable}\ C$;
$C.\mathrm{ver}\ v = (s, E_v)$; $\mathrm{SettledAtOn}\ C\ E_v\ S$;
$s$ at hand for $F$; and the construction-checkable containment: for
\emph{every} anchored assignment $\mathrm{chainOf}$ certified by
$\mathrm{EAnchored}$, the canonical chain-aligned mints beyond $S$ lie
in $F.\mathrm{MintAt}$. Conclusion: every continuation $\tau$ of
events existing beyond the cut folds and reads identically under the
lazy translation (T1 $\wedge$ T2). The $\mathrm{AnchorsFactorBeyond}$
hypothesis of \lean{eRecode\_settled\_bridge} is \emph{gone}; no
$\mathrm{vis}$-side condition survives in the interface.
\hfill\lean{eRecode\_settled\_bridge\_honest}
\end{theorem}

\noindent Scope, honestly
delimited: the single-epoch statement, with cut-side data addressed by
coordinates and chains, never by event ids resolved through prefix sums
(the runtime twin's erratum: renumbered coordinates no longer telescope
to ids, so id-addressed cuts break after epoch one). Concurrent
divergent compactions (an epoch DAG with common-refinement translation)
remain the deferred protocol half.

\paragraph{Spine fusion, mechanized.} Rank renumbering alone leaves the
\emph{spine depth}: typing runs mint $\delta{=}1$ chains, and every level
costs at least one codeword even at ordinal $1$ (Measurement 2 quantifies
this). Fusion removes the dead levels themselves. A \emph{fusible spine}
is a maximal chain of settled dead nodes, each with exactly one child
branch (counting every known coordinate, in-flight prefixes included) and
no in-flight op anchored on it; it collapses to a single level at the
spine head's codeword, so a typing-run-then-delete chain of depth $k$
costs one codeword instead of $k$. Composed after the rank pass this is
the full iteration-two map, and reads survive it
(\code{EmbedRGA\_Fusion.lean}, kernel-clean).

\begin{theorem}[fusion composes with the rank pass, reads intact]
\label{thm:fusereads}
Let $F$ be the rank-pass map. Fusion data: a positive spine $Q$
($h_{Q\mathrm{pos}}$), its surviving head $Q'$ a prefix of $Q$
($h_{Q'}$), and a chain domain $\mathcal{D}$ with (i)~every
$\mathcal{D}$ chain positive ($h_{\mathcal{D}\mathrm{pos}}$) and
(ii)~\emph{through-traffic passes the whole spine} ($h_{\mathrm{thru}}$:
a $\mathcal{D}$ chain extending $Q'$ extends $Q$). Let the two maps be
compatible at a surviving domain $(\mathrm{Rest}', \mathrm{MintAt}')$
($\mathrm{CompatOn}$, Definition~\ref{def:spmcomp}), and let the cut
state $s$ be at hand and the continuation $\tau$ declared at the
\emph{composite} $F.\mathrm{comp}\ (\mathrm{fuseSPM}\ \Gamma\ Q\ Q'\
\mathcal{D}\ \ldots)$. Then the reads of any beyond-cut continuation
survive the two-pass translation $\mathrm{fuseBits} \circ F.f$:
\[
  \mathrm{query}\bigl(\mathrm{applySeq}\
  (\mathrm{remap}\ s)\ (\tau\ \text{translated})\bigr)
  = \mathrm{query}\bigl(\mathrm{applySeq}\ s\ \tau\bigr).
\]
(\lean{fuseSPM} packages the fusion as a stable-prefix bundle over
$\mathrm{fuseBits}$, its bit-level map; the fusion-alone form is
\lean{fuse\_reads\_identical}; both are direct re-invocations of T2.)
\hfill\lean{compactThenFuse\_reads}
\end{theorem}

\noindent Order preservation is a three-class H2 argument:
(1)~within a fused block the common prefix is replaced wholesale,
relative order untouched; (2)~a block against the spine head's siblings
is decided at the head's level, whose codeword fusion keeps; (3)~the
anchor-versus-extension corner is vacuous, since dead spine nodes hold no
records and no key ends at a fused-away level. The mechanization sharpens
class~2 into triviality: because fusion runs \emph{after} the rank pass,
the head codeword it sees is already the ordinal, so the fusion map keeps
the head verbatim and drops only the strict interior, and the class-2
comparison is unchanged by construction rather than by a separate
``block inherits the rank'' argument. The guard is conservative (any
known in-flight branch or anchor blocks its node from every spine);
through-traffic below a blocked node still fuses.

\paragraph{The merge clause, discharged (VC-S4 for the remap species).}
The stability bundle's argumentwise merge congruence
(\S\ref{sec:stability}, VC-S4) is, for the coordinate re-map, the
statement that lazy translation commutes with the ternary merge
(\code{EmbedRGA\_MergeCongr.lean}).

\begin{theorem}[the merge clause]
\label{thm:mergeremap}
For a stable-prefix map $F$ and states $l$, $a$, $b$ with $a$ and $b$
at hand for $F$ (\emph{no} hypothesis on the LCA $l$):
\[
  \mathrm{eMergeL}\ (\mathrm{remap}_F\ l)\ (\mathrm{remap}_F\ a)\
  (\mathrm{remap}_F\ b) \;=\; \mathrm{remap}_F\ (\mathrm{eMergeL}\ l\
  a\ b).
\]
The LCA enters the survival rule solely through its id set, which the
re-map preserves; this is what lets a compacted head merge against a
full LCA payload, the mixed case \S\ref{sec:stability} calls the
normal one. The VC-S4 packaging: the simulation relation
$\mathrm{eRemapRel}$ (compacted $=$ translation of full, full at hand)
is preserved by the ternary merge (\lean{eRemapRel\_merge}), literally
the \code{vc\_merge} conclusion for the remap species;
\lean{embed\_merge\_remap\_congr} restates the congruence at
$(E\ \Gamma\ \alpha).\mathrm{mergeL}$.
\hfill\lean{merge\_remap\_congr}
\end{theorem}

\paragraph{Multi-epoch composition.} A single re-coded configuration is
not a native honest configuration (T3's gap), so a \emph{second}
compaction cannot naively re-invoke the cluster. The data-plane half is
now closed (\code{EmbedRGA\_MultiEpoch.lean}, kernel-clean): the
semantic residue of one epoch is a stable-prefix map, and maps compose.

\begin{definition}[epoch composition at the surviving domain]
\label{def:spmcomp}
For maps $F$ (earlier) and $G$ (later), $\mathrm{CompatOn}\ F\ G\
\mathrm{Rest}'\ \mathrm{MintAt}'$ carries the \emph{composite's own}
at-hand domain, the coordinates surviving to $G$'s epoch, and demands
four boundary clauses: $\mathrm{restF}$ ($\mathrm{Rest}'\ c$ implies
$F.\mathrm{Dom}\ c$), $\mathrm{restG}$ ($\mathrm{Rest}'\ c$ implies
$G.\mathrm{Dom}\ (F.f\ c)$), and $\mathrm{mintF}$/$\mathrm{mintG}$
(the same two clauses for declared mints, the later mint at the
translated prefix $F.f\ \pi$). Under them $F.\mathrm{comp}\ G$ is
again a stable-prefix map: $f = G.f \circ F.f$ over
$(\mathrm{Rest}', \mathrm{MintAt}')$; H3 chains through both maps'
$\mathrm{ext}$ (an epoch-$1$ mint $(\pi, d)$ once remapped is an
epoch-$2$ mint $(F.f\,\pi, d)$, the delta codeword untouched); H2
chains because order-isomorphisms compose; H1 is derived as always.
The no-reclaim special case $\mathrm{Compat}$ (every $F$-at-hand
coordinate stays $G$-at-hand after remapping, every $F$-mint stays a
$G$-mint) collapses the four clauses to two at $F$'s full domain. The
$n$-fold closure $\mathrm{chainSPM}$ composes a whole
$\mathrm{CompatChain}$ of maps into one, carrying the \emph{earliest}
map's domain.
\hfill\lean{CompatOn}, \lean{StablePrefixMap.comp}, \lean{chainSPM}
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[the $n$-epoch headline]
\label{thm:multiepoch}
For a compatibility chain $\mathrm{CompatChain}\ (F :: F_s)$, cut
state $s$ at hand for the \emph{first} map $F$, and continuation
$\tau$ declared at $F$:
\[
\begin{aligned}
  &\mathrm{query}\bigl(\mathrm{applySeq}\
  (\mathrm{remap}_{\mathrm{chainSPM}\,(F :: F_s)}\ s)\
  (\tau\ \text{translated through every epoch's map})\bigr) \\
  &\qquad= \mathrm{query}\bigl(\mathrm{applySeq}\ s\ \tau\bigr) :
\end{aligned}
\]
after arbitrarily many settled-cut compactions, a beyond-all-cuts
continuation, its lagging ops translated through every epoch map,
reads exactly as the uncompacted run. (The fold-congruence twin is
\lean{multiEpoch\_applySeq}; both are direct re-invocations of T1/T2
on the composite.)
\hfill\lean{multiEpoch\_settled\_reads}
\end{theorem}

\noindent The subtlety that makes this non-trivial is \emph{rank
reclaim}, pinned by the first of two companion negatives; the second
records the runtime twin's erratum.

\begin{proposition}[rank reclaim defeats naive composition]
\label{prop:rankreclaim}
Unary code. With the epoch-$1$ map $gc_1$ ($\mathrm{enc}\ 3 \mapsto
\mathrm{enc}\ 1$, $\mathrm{enc}\ 7 \mapsto \mathrm{enc}\ 2$) and the
reclaiming epoch-$2$ map $gc_2$ ($\mathrm{enc}\ 2 \mapsto
\mathrm{enc}\ 1$):
\[
  (gc_2 \circ gc_1)(\mathrm{enc}\ 3) = (gc_2 \circ
  gc_1)(\mathrm{enc}\ 7)
  \quad\text{while}\quad \mathrm{enc}\ 3 \neq \mathrm{enc}\ 7 :
\]
a dead coordinate and a live one collide on the reclaimed rank, H1
fails, and the composite is not a stable-prefix map on the full
first-epoch domain. This is exactly why $\mathrm{comp}$ carries the
surviving domain.
\hfill\lean{naive\_composition\_collides}
\end{proposition}

\begin{proposition}[the id-addressing erratum]
\label{prop:idaddr}
After epoch one a renumbered coordinate no longer telescopes to its
event id: with the unary telescope decode $\mathrm{unaryId}$ (the
count of $1$-bits), $\mathrm{unaryId}(\mathrm{enc}\ 7) = 7$ but
$\mathrm{unaryId}(gc_1(\mathrm{enc}\ 7)) \neq 7$, and an id-addressed
second cut at $\{7\}$ resolves to \emph{no} record of the renumbered
state while the coordinate-addressed cut resolves the record exactly.
Second cuts must be coordinate- or record-addressed.
\hfill\lean{id\_addressing\_breaks}
\end{proposition}

\paragraph{The diamond: incomparable cuts, and the barrier removed.}
Everything above composes cuts that are \emph{comparable} (each a
refinement of the last). The distributed question is the incomparable
case: two replicas compact at settled cuts $S_1$ and $S_2$ that neither
refines, then merge. The result is that they merge with \emph{no}
coordination, and the finding is that this is not a new algebraic
obstruction at all. Since $S_1 \subseteq W$ and $S_2 \subseteq W$ for the
join $W = S_1 \cup S_2$, each leg $S_i \to W$ is a comparable (nested)
step, hence a genuine $\mathrm{StablePrefixMap}$ by the same $\mathrm{comp}$
closure; the only new content is that the two legs and the one-shot
$W$-compaction \emph{agree}, and agreement is determinism of the
$W$-map in its certificate. The join map is produced by a builder that
is a function of a $\mathrm{JoinCert}$ alone (surviving coordinates,
settled-dead, declared mints), so two replicas that computed the same
certificate install the same map:
\[
  c_1 = c_2 \;\Rightarrow\; \mathcal{C}(c_1) = \mathcal{C}(c_2)
  \qquad (\lean{certMap\_deterministic}).
\]
This \code{congrArg} \emph{is} the ``without coordination'' content:
equal certificates give equal maps by definition, not by reconciliation.
Agreement then upgrades to bit-identical states.

\begin{theorem}[the epoch diamond at $s1$]
\label{thm:diamond}
For maps $\mathrm{spm}_{S_1}, \mathrm{spm}_{S_2}, \mathrm{rel}_A,
\mathrm{rel}_B, \mathrm{spm}_W$ and a carried domain $D$, if every
record of the cut state $s$ has its coordinate in $D$, and both relative
composites agree with the one-shot on $D$
($\mathrm{rel}_A(\mathrm{spm}_{S_1}\,c) = \mathrm{spm}_W\,c$ and
$\mathrm{rel}_B(\mathrm{spm}_{S_2}\,c) = \mathrm{spm}_W\,c$ for all $c \in
D$), then the two-leg compactions and the one-shot compaction produce
\emph{bit-identical} states, not merely equal reads:
\[
\begin{aligned}
  &\mathrm{remap}_{\mathrm{rel}_A \circ \mathrm{spm}_{S_1}}\ s
    = \mathrm{remap}_{\mathrm{spm}_W}\ s
    = \mathrm{remap}_{\mathrm{rel}_B \circ \mathrm{spm}_{S_2}}\ s, \\
  &\quad\text{and their reads coincide.}
\end{aligned}
\]
\hfill\lean{diamond\_confluence} (via \lean{diamond\_confluence\_comp}
through $\mathrm{comp}$)
\end{theorem}

\noindent The one genuinely new obstruction is the carried domain. It
must be the join cut's kept id-set transported to epoch-$0$ coordinates
\emph{by record identity},
$\mathrm{transportedDom}\ s\ \mathrm{kept}_W = \{\,c : \exists x \in s,\
\mathrm{kept}_W(x.\mathrm{id}) \wedge x.\mathrm{coord} = c\,\}$
(\lean{transportedDom}, coordinate-nodup on a sorted state by
\lean{transportedDom\_coords\_nodup}, so it never decodes a renumbered
coordinate and the id-addressing erratum does not apply). The naive
membership pullback aliases, the incomparable-cut twin of
Proposition~\ref{prop:rankreclaim}:

\begin{proposition}[naive pullback aliases]
\label{prop:pullbackalias}
Unary code. A dropped coordinate falls through a leg's map verbatim onto
a kept later coordinate: $(\mathrm{rel} \circ gc)(\mathrm{enc}\ 3) =
(\mathrm{rel} \circ gc)(\mathrm{enc}\ 4)$ while $\mathrm{enc}\ 3 \neq
\mathrm{enc}\ 4$. Intersecting the two legs' kept sets would admit the
aliased coordinate; the record-identity transported domain excludes it.
\hfill\lean{naive\_pullback\_aliases}
\end{proposition}

\noindent Two more obligations close the protocol. Dropping a superseded
epoch's map is a fold no-op once that map fixes every live coordinate and
every continuation anchor (the ack-plus-\lean{AllHeardSince} certificate
supplies exactly these hypotheses; the ack-only shortcut is machine-refuted
as its \textsc{fail} companion):
\[
  \mathrm{applySeq}\ (\mathrm{remap}_F\ s)\ (\tau.\mathrm{map}\
  \mathrm{remap}_F) = \mathrm{applySeq}\ s\ \tau
  \qquad (\lean{mapDrop\_sound}),
\]
and continuation freshness survives the join epoch (dense renumbering
never grows a delta, so a fresh delta stays key-distinct from every
renumbered ordinal, \lean{contOK\_root\_key\_fresh},
\lean{rED\_fresh\_dominates\_join}) --- the theorem face of the
$11029/0$ \code{ContOK} observation in the model harness. The model
(\code{whiteboard/epoch\_diamond\_check.py}) validated the whole diamond
at $s1$ across $2400$ trials before any of this was stated.

\begin{remark}[what the runtime realizes, and an honest gap]
\label{rem:glb}
The shipped runtime (\code{runtime/src/epoch.js}, task \#112) turns the
per-replica epoch integer into a cut-indexed DAG and replaces the old
cross-epoch \emph{throw} with the certificate-determined join. Building
it surfaced that the model's ``relative-compact each side to $W$, merge
in epoch $W$'' is unsound under \emph{partial straggler visibility}: a
settled record's group freezes on the side holding a straggler and
renumbers on the side without it, so the two sides disagree on shared
coordinates. The sound protocol lifts both heads \emph{down} to their
common base (the greatest lower bound) via inverse coordinate maps and
merges there; this is read- and fold-equivalent to the $W$-merge
\emph{by} Theorem~\ref{thm:diamond}, and coordination-free (both sides
lift deterministically to the same frame). The merged head therefore
de-compacts to the base frame, recovering compactness only on the next
certified compaction: a compaction frame is minted solely by the
content-addressed \code{compactStable}, never re-derived at a merge from
