divergent local holdings, which is arguably the correct discipline
rather than a compromise. The twin property-based test is clean across
the $2000$-trial validation sweep, of which $600$ are committed to CI
(\code{runtime/test/epoch.test.js}).
\end{remark}

\noindent Freshness across epochs is representation-agnostic
(\lean{rED\_le\_self}, \lean{rED\_fresh\_dominates}: a delta fresh against
original sibling deltas stays fresh against renumbered ordinals without
inspecting their meaning), so the order-iso half composes whether stored
deltas are timestamp differences or epoch-$k$ ordinals. The
\lean{StablePrefixMap} bundle is not text-specific: the rich-text marks
layer instantiates the same interface a second time as its keep-set
relabeling, and composes through the same route
(\S\ref{sec:marks}).

\paragraph{The honesty-rebasing lemma $(\ast)$, closed.} Two residues
above --- the single-epoch honesty re-basing (T3's gap: the re-coded
history is honest only \emph{modulo} the coordinate order-embedding) and
the multi-epoch $\mathrm{CompatChain}$ residue --- are the \emph{same}
obligation. Its causal-stability half (\lean{nested\_cuts\_settled},
from $\mathrm{settledAt\_of\_allHeard}$ and cut-antitonicity of the
frontier, \code{EmbedRGA\_CompatChain.lean}) and its order-iso freshness
half (\lean{rED\_hocc\_fresh}) closed first; what remained was one
lemma, which we called $(\ast)$: exhibit an epoch-$2$ configuration
honest \emph{modulo} the order-embedding $F_1$, so that
anchored-existence re-derives at the epoch boundary. $(\ast)$ is now
discharged (\code{EmbedRGA\_HonestyRebase.lean}, kernel-clean), and the
shape of the discharge is itself the finding: honesty is not
\emph{re-derived} at epoch~2, it is \emph{re-based}. The invariant that
crosses the boundary is not $\mathrm{EHonest}$ (false of a compacted
state, whose coordinates are no longer birth-chain telescopes) but a
history-free \emph{coded-forest} invariant $I$ over the live coordinate
set: honest configurations satisfy $I$ (S1), every chain-aligned
stable-prefix map preserves $I$ (S2), and $I$ alone discharges the
epoch-boundary obligations of the next epoch's map (S4), with no
epoch-2 honesty assumption anywhere.

\begin{definition}[the coded anchored forest invariant $I$]
\label{def:caf}
For a code $\Gamma$ and a list $L$ of live coordinates, $I(L)$
($\mathrm{CodedAnchoredForest}\ \Gamma\ L$) has two fields:
(\emph{nodup}) $L$ is duplicate-free; (\emph{decode}) every $c \in L$
is $\mathrm{coordOf}_\Gamma(\mathit{ch})$ for some \emph{nonempty
positive} birth chain $\mathit{ch}$. History-free: a predicate on the
coordinate set alone, which is the whole point --- a re-coded
configuration has no honest history but still carries $I$. (The
nearest-live-ancestor forest is reconstructed from $I$ by
prefix-freeness alone: \lean{caf\_ancestor\_factor},
\lean{caf\_ancestor\_shorter}, \lean{caf\_chain\_inj}.)
\hfill\lean{CodedAnchoredForest}
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[S1: honest configurations satisfy $I$]
\label{thm:ehonesti}
Hypotheses: $\mathrm{GenHonest}$ at $\mathrm{eApplicable}$;
$\mathrm{CausalPastEnumerable}\ C$; a well-formed enumeration
$\mathrm{EWf}\ \Gamma\ \rho$; every op of $\rho$ in
$C.\mathrm{events}$. Conclusion: $I$ of the live coordinate set of
$\mathrm{eFold}_\Gamma\ \rho$. (The anchor certificate gives each live
insert's coordinate as its chain's; nodup is sortedness; nonemptiness
is the nonzero id.)
\hfill\lean{ehonest\_implies\_I}
\end{theorem}

\noindent Call a map $F$ \emph{chain-aligned} via $\varphi$
(\lean{ChainAligned}) when $F.f(\mathrm{coordOf}_\Gamma\ \mathit{ch})
= \mathrm{coordOf}_\Gamma(\varphi\ \mathit{ch})$ on positive chains
and $\varphi$ preserves positivity and nonemptiness: the
stable/unstable split lands on a codeword boundary and the tail rides
verbatim. Both shipped passes are chain-aligned
(\lean{chainAligned\_compactSPM}, \lean{chainAligned\_fuseSPM}), and
chain-alignment composes (\lean{chainAligned\_comp}).

\begin{theorem}[S2, load-bearing: stable-prefix maps preserve $I$]
\label{thm:spmpreservesI}
Hypotheses: $F$ chain-aligned via $\varphi$; $I(L)$; every $c \in L$
at hand for $F$. Conclusion: $I(L.\mathrm{map}\ F.f)$. Decodability
survives through $\varphi$; distinctness survives because $F$ is
injective on its domain (H1, Lemma~\ref{lem:spmh1}). Honesty rebases:
no re-derivation of $\mathrm{EHonest}$ on the re-coded set.
\hfill\lean{stablePrefixMap\_preserves\_I}
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[S4: the epoch boundary discharged from $I$]
\label{thm:starclosed}
Hypotheses: epoch-1's map $F_1$ chain-aligned via $\varphi_1$; a
domain $\mathcal{D}_1$ of positive chains; and the two
construction-shape clauses, true of the shipped compaction and fusion
maps: $h_{\mathrm{RestShape}}$ (every $F_1.\mathrm{Rest}$ coordinate
is $\mathrm{coordOf}$ of a $\mathcal{D}_1$ chain) and
$h_{\mathrm{MintShape}}$ (every declared mint $(\pi, d)$ has $\pi =
\mathrm{coordOf}(\mathit{ch}_A)$ with $\mathit{ch}_A$ positive,
$\mathit{ch}_A \cdot d \in \mathcal{D}_1$, $1 \le d$). Conclusion:
\[
  \mathrm{CompatOn}\ F_1\ (\mathrm{rebaseSPM}\ \Gamma\ \mathcal{D}_1\
  \varphi_1\ \ldots)\ F_1.\mathrm{Rest}\ F_1.\mathrm{MintAt} :
\]
the epoch-boundary obligations $\mathrm{restG}$/$\mathrm{mintG}$ of
Definition~\ref{def:spmcomp} are met by epoch~2 built over the
\emph{rebased image domain} (\lean{rebaseSPM}, \lean{rebasedDom}: the
$\varphi_1$-images of epoch-1's at-hand chains), a survivor's image at
hand by chain-alignment and a beyond-cut mint's image a declared mint
by the snoc-commute (\lean{chainAligned\_snoc}). No epoch-2 honesty
assumption; this is $(\ast)$, closed. ($I$ is also exactly the
at-hand certificate a next-epoch compaction's domain needs:
\lean{I\_implies\_atHand}, S3.)
\hfill\lean{twoEpoch\_compatOn\_of\_I}
\end{theorem}

\begin{corollary}[$I$ rebases across two epochs]
\label{cor:itwoepoch}
For $F_1$, $F_2$ chain-aligned via $\varphi_1$, $\varphi_2$, $I(L)$,
and the two domain coverages: $I((L.\mathrm{map}\
F_1.f).\mathrm{map}\ F_2.f)$ --- the invariant survives both
compactions, so the second epoch operates on the $I$-carried rebased
domain, not by transport through the first.
\hfill\lean{I\_preserved\_two\_epoch}
\end{corollary}

\noindent What is left downstream is the protocol layer: deriving the
per-continuation hypotheses from a delivery protocol, and everything
multi-replica (agreement on a common cut, concurrent divergent
compaction), Open Questions~\ref{oq:starlemma},~\ref{oq:epochdag}.

\paragraph{Measurement 2: real traces, and where the bits actually
sit.} The full epoch stack (rank renumbering plus \emph{spine fusion}:
collapse each maximal settled dead chain with exactly one child branch
and no in-flight anchor into a single level) was run over the standard
collaborative-editing replay corpus (the josephg traces). Accounting:
per record, the sum over its kept-chain levels of $|D(\delta)|$ bits,
$D$ the flipped Elias-$\delta$ code of \S\ref{sec:encoding}; totals
divided by live characters; character payloads excluded; ``fused'' is
after the renumber-plus-fusion epoch at a settled cut.

\begin{center}\small
\begin{tabular}{@{}lrrr@{}}
\toprule
trace (one-sided) & chain b/ch, raw & chain b/ch, fused & ratio \\
\midrule
friendsforever & 1622.5 & 856.5 & 1.9$\times$ \\
clownschool & 2488.7 & 1471.0 & 1.7$\times$ \\
seph-blog1 & 2974.9 & 917.2 & 3.2$\times$ \\
automerge-paper & 2304.5 & 1279.4 & 1.8$\times$ \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

\noindent Three findings, in causal order. \emph{(i)} Rank renumbering
alone recovers only 1.1--1.8$\times$: typing runs mint $\delta{=}1$
chains, and every level costs at least one bit even at ordinal 1, so
the residual cost is \emph{spine depth}, which is what fusion owns.
\emph{(ii)} Fusion then removes essentially everything removable:
surviving dead levels cost 3 to 76 bits per character in total, and the
fused column equals the code cost of the \emph{live tree shape} alone,
verified by a per-level attribution that accounts for every bit.
\emph{(iii)} The few-bits-per-character hope fails for a reason worth
the number: the live shape is itself expensive. Real traces anchor
typing runs in \emph{live} chains of mean depth 835 to 1468 levels at
about one bit per level, and 97 to 99.8 percent of the post-fusion bits
sit on live ancestor levels that no epoch map may touch (they are the
retained semantics, \S\ref{sec:chainprice}). History independence holds
three ways (staggered cuts, one final cut, and from-scratch compaction
land on bit-identical totals), and the fused display spells the trace's
final content on every sequential trace. Conclusion: the epoch stack
removes the dead-history share; what survives is a
\emph{representation} cost, which is the run table's brief
(\S\ref{sec:runtable}).

\paragraph{Measurement 3: the sided instantiation.} The same stack,
band-aware (renumber within each parent-band group; fusion carries the
head's band and codeword), replayed under the Fugue policy. Same
accounting, plus one side bit per level, which is precisely the point:

\begin{center}\small
\begin{tabular}{@{}lrrr@{}}
\toprule
trace (sided, Fugue) & chain b/ch, raw & chain b/ch, fused & ratio \\
\midrule
friendsforever & 2582.0 & 1735.7 & 1.5$\times$ \\
clownschool & 4058.5 & 2988.1 & 1.4$\times$ \\
seph-blog1 & 5559.1 & 1919.8 & 2.9$\times$ \\
automerge-paper & 4448.0 & 2583.7 & 1.7$\times$ \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

\noindent The ratios sit \emph{systematically below} the one-sided
1.7--3.2$\times$, and the naive expectation that the ratio would be
preserved is falsified by its own arithmetic: adding the same flat
per-level side cost to numerator and denominator pulls the quotient
toward the \emph{level-count} ratio, which is below the payload ratio.
Where the sides pay: the fused sided state costs about twice the fused
one-sided state, and the premium is \emph{entirely the flat side flag};
the sided delta payload exceeds the one-sided payload by only 1.0 to
4.7 percent (on these traces the Fugue tree is payload-equivalent to
RGA's). The flag costs a flat 1.000 bit per level against a true side
entropy of 0.19 to 0.27 bits (L-mints are 3.0 to 4.6 percent of mints
under Fugue on real traces): a \textbf{4--5$\times$ overpay} on the
side channel, load-bearing for lexicographic band separation, and 94 to
98 percent of the side bits sit on live levels no epoch map can touch.
Both residues (live depth, flat flags) point at the same lever: a
different representation of the same immutable chain data.

\subsection{The run table: the representation lever}
\label{sec:runtable}

\paragraph{Runs.} Work over the \emph{kept tree}: the live records plus
their dead ancestors (every other node has been removed outright;
tombstone-freedom is unchanged). Call the edge from node $p$ to child
$c$ \emph{fusible} when (1) $c$ is the unique kept child of $p$, (2)
$\delta(c) = 1$, (3) $c$'s side is R (vacuous one-sided), and (4) $c$
and $p$ have the same liveness. A \emph{run} is a maximal chain of
fusible edges. Fusibility is a per-edge predicate and condition (1)
gives each node at most one fusible out-edge, so the runs are a
canonical partition of the kept tree into chains: a function of the
state, independent of arrival order. A table entry is one run, with
header (liveness flag, parent entry, offset in parent, side, head
delta, length); members after the head implicitly have delta 1 and side
R, and liveness is uniform along a run. A record's address is
\emph{(run id, offset)}. Dead entries carry no records, only the
structure live descendants' coordinates pass through. The design's
starting observation: what typing mints \emph{is} exactly a fusible
chain, so real documents decompose into few long runs.

\begin{definition}[the run table]
\label{def:runtable}
Over the kept tree of a live chain set $L$ (\lean{keptL}: the live
chains plus their nonempty prefixes, deduplicated), the edge into $c$
is \emph{fusible} (\lean{fusible}) when $c$ and its parent are kept,
$c$'s label is $1$, $c$ is the parent's \emph{unique} kept child, and
$c$ and the parent have equal liveness ($c \in L \leftrightarrow
c.\mathrm{dropLast} \in L$); a \emph{head} (\lean{isHead}) is a kept
node whose incoming edge is not fusible. Runs are the fibers of the
climb \lean{headOf}; every member is its head extended by a block of
$1$ labels. An entry (\lean{RTEntry}) is the header
$\langle \mathrm{par} : \mathrm{Option}(\text{parent index} \times
\text{offset}),\ \mathrm{live},\ \delta,\ \mathrm{len} \rangle$; the
table (\lean{tableOf}) maps the canonical head enumeration
(\lean{headsList}, sorted by the short-lex \lean{hLt}: shorter first,
so parents precede children, \lean{keyLt} within a length) through the
entry compiler (\lean{entryOfHead}); the abstraction (\lean{stateOf})
recovers each live entry's members from headers alone.
\lean{Canonical} recognizes the image: positive lengths, backward
parent references, tail attachment (\code{par\_tail}), maximal fusion
(\code{no\_fuse}), dead leaves pruned (\code{leaves\_live}), sorted
head enumeration.
\hfill\lean{RTEntry}, \lean{tableOf}, \lean{stateOf}, \lean{Canonical}
\end{definition}

Two properties before any numbers. \emph{No stability gate}: the run
table is an invertible re-representation of the current state, whatever
that state is, unsettled suffixes included, and in particular the
freshly typed hot end of the document where the depth cost is being
minted; the PBT exploits this by rebuilding the table mid-flight
between merges after every event. \emph{Lossless in the strong sense}:
from the table alone one recovers every kept node's delta (head delta
from the header, 1 elsewhere), side, liveness, and pre-epoch timestamp
(deltas summed along the contracted path), hence every coordinate,
hence the state; the harness gates exactly this, with zero tolerance,
on every record of every trace.

\begin{theorem}[tail attachment]
\label{thm:tailattach}
In the canonical table, every entry attaches at its parent entry's
last member. Kernel form: if $h$ is a head, the edge into
$h.\mathrm{dropLast} \cdot 1$ is not fusible,
\[
  \mathrm{isHead}\ L\ h = \mathrm{true} \;\Longrightarrow\;
  \mathrm{fusible}\ L\ (h.\mathrm{dropLast} \cdot 1) =
  \mathrm{false}.
\]
(If an entry attached at an interior member $m$, then $m$'s run
successor and the entry's head would be two kept children of $m$,
contradicting fusibility of $m$'s outgoing run edge.) No honesty, no
stability, no reachability hypothesis.
\hfill\lean{tail\_attachment}
\end{theorem}

\noindent Three consequences: the
``offset in parent'' header field is derivable (always parent length
minus one; the accounting still counts it and reports it recoverable);
the cross-run comparator only ever diverges at a tail or inside a
shared run, so it consults entry chains and header fields only and
\emph{never materializes a coordinate}; and it is cheap to assert,
which the harness does on every table built.

\paragraph{The comparator.} Within a run, display order is offset
order (each member is the R delta-1 child of its predecessor, hence
displays directly after it). Across runs, compare the two entry chains
from the root; by the lemma every step exits at a tail, and at the
first difference either one chain is a prefix of the other (the deeper
record is inside the shallower's continuation subtree; one-sided the
shallower displays first, sided an L branch displays first) or two
branch entries hang at the same tail and the branch headers decide
(one-sided newest first; sided by the banded order). The
$(\mathrm{ts}, \mathrm{agent})$ concurrency tiebreak is \emph{not}
encoded in either representation's counted bits; the tie oracle rides
outside the representation on both sides and is charged to neither,
preserving parity with the chain measurements. The cost of a comparison
is the \emph{contracted} depth: one entry per run on the path instead
of one level per node.

\paragraph{Mutation, and the forced coalesce.} Splits mutate the table,
never the semantics: stored chain data is immutable, only the partition
changes. Insert at anchor $a$: if $a$ is interior, split its entry
after $a$; attach the new record as a singleton; then \emph{coalesce}
(a fusible singleton that is the tail's unique attachment fuses into
the parent entry), which is how typing extends a run in $O(1)$ table
work. Delete of $x$: split to make $x$ a tail; if $x$ still has kept
children, carve it out as a dead singleton (a liveness split);
childless, it simply vanishes, dropping newly unkept dead tails upward
and re-coalescing the parent with a now-unique fusible child. Coalesce
is the inverse of split \emph{and it is not optional}: without it,
deleting a concurrent interloper leaves the two halves of the old run
split forever and the table drifts from the canonical partition (found
during design, pinned by a directed case and by the PBT's
incremental-equals-canonical-rebuild gate after every event). Delivery
under a vanished anchor (the anchor chain passed through nodes deleted
while childless here) re-materializes the missing levels as dead
entries from the op's carried coordinate: the run-table cost of
tombstone-freedom, paid with information the embed op already carries.
Merges deliver the other side's events through the same two rules;
epochs rebuild the table over the compacted tree (sound because the
table is a function of the state).

\paragraph{The accounting model.} Applied identically to both
representations; every pointer and every header bit counted, no hidden
structure. $D$ is the flipped Elias-$\delta$ code; $W = \lceil \log_2
(N_{\mathrm{entries}} + 1) \rceil$ is the id width.
\begin{align*}
  \text{per record:}&\quad W + |D(\mathrm{offset}+1)| \\
  \text{per entry:}&\quad 1 + W + |D(\mathrm{poff}+1)| +
  |D(\mathrm{head}\ \delta)| + |D(\mathrm{len})|
  \ \ (+\ 1 \text{ side bit, sided}).
\end{align*}
Totals are per live character, reported with the full per-field
breakdown so every number is auditable back to a field. Not charged on
either side: the tie oracle (above). Two fields are counted although
the canonical table makes them recoverable, and the breakdown lets a
reader subtract them: the parent offset (the tail-attachment lemma) and
the per-record run id when records are stored grouped by run (it is
then positional).

\paragraph{Measurement 4: the run table.} Same traces and replay as
measurements 2 and 3. Columns: the raw chain representation
(``chain''), the chain after its best epoch (``fused,'' the previous
subsection's current best), the run table over the \emph{raw
uncompacted} chains with no epoch and no settled cut (``rt''), and the
run table rebuilt after the epoch (``rtc'').

\begin{center}\small
\setlength{\tabcolsep}{5pt}
\begin{tabular}{@{}llrrrrr@{}}
\toprule
trace & family & chain & fused & rt & rtc & fused/rtc \\
\midrule
friendsforever\_flat & 1-sided & 1622.5 & 856.5 & 22.13 & 22.30 & 38.4$\times$ \\
friendsforever\_flat & sided/F & 2582.0 & 1735.7 & 22.54 & 20.98 & 82.7$\times$ \\
clownschool\_flat & 1-sided & 2488.7 & 1471.0 & 22.42 & 22.64 & 65.0$\times$ \\
clownschool\_flat & sided/F & 4058.5 & 2988.1 & 24.11 & 21.20 & 140.9$\times$ \\
seph-blog1 & 1-sided & 2974.9 & 917.2 & 26.00 & 23.16 & 39.6$\times$ \\
seph-blog1 & sided/F & 5559.1 & 1919.8 & 27.21 & 24.99 & 76.8$\times$ \\
automerge-paper & 1-sided & 2304.5 & 1279.4 & 23.76 & 23.92 & 53.5$\times$ \\
automerge-paper & sided/F & 4448.0 & 2583.7 & 25.21 & 24.58 & 105.1$\times$ \\
friendsforever & 1-sided & 1243.2 & 856.5 & 20.92 & 22.30 & 38.4$\times$ \\
friendsforever & sided/F & 2189.2 & 1735.6 & 22.36 & 20.98 & 82.7$\times$ \\
clownschool & 1-sided & 1863.1 & 1471.0 & 20.85 & 22.64 & 65.0$\times$ \\
clownschool & sided/F & 3421.3 & 2988.1 & 22.29 & 21.20 & 140.9$\times$ \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

\noindent All values in bits per live character under the accounting
model above; the \code{\_flat} traces are the concurrent traces
replayed single-author, their unsuffixed twins the concurrent
originals. The verdict: \textbf{20.9 to 27.2 bits per character, 38 to
141 times below the fused chains}, confirming the design prediction
(per-record cost collapses from $\Theta(\mathrm{depth})$ to $O(1)$
amortized plus a log-sized offset) with margin. Where the remaining
bits sit, from the audited breakdown:
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=2em]
\item The per-record \emph{run id} now dominates (10 to 14 of the 21 to
  27 bits, the flat id width): the deep-chain cost did not shrink to
  the offset code, it shrank to \emph{one pointer}. An implementation
  storing records grouped by run makes this field positional and free,
  putting totals at 9 to 14 bits per character; the model charges it
  because the pre-registered accounting does.
\item Offsets cost 5.2 to 12.1 bits per character; all header fields
  together amortize to 0.6 to 6.4.
\item The side channel collapses from one flat bit per live level
  (867.8 to 1494.1 bits per character after fusion) to one bit per
  \emph{entry}: \textbf{0.061 to 0.174 bits per character}, three to
  four orders of magnitude, and below even an entropy-coded per-level
  flag ($H(\mathrm{side})$ times 850 to 1500 levels); the explicit
  L-entry-list alternative is computable from the reported entry counts
  and is dearer at these sizes.
\item The epoch stack becomes \emph{bit-wise marginal} under this
  representation: the composed column beats raw by at most 2.9 bits per
  character (sided) and loses to raw by up to 1.8 (one-sided, where
  longer post-renumber runs make offsets dearer than the headers they
  save). The run table alone, with no settled cut at all, already
  delivers the collapse. What the epoch still buys is \emph{structure},
  not bits: entries drop up to 7$\times$, the id width shrinks, raw
  cursor-jump boundaries vanish under renumbering, and the contracted
  depth drops up to 11.8$\times$.
\item The honest limit: the \emph{bit} cost per record is $O(1)$
  amortized plus the offset code, but the comparator's \emph{time} is
  still $\Theta(\text{contracted depth})$ per cross-run comparison
  (mean 19.6 to 261 runs per path against chain depths of 850 to
  1500). Collapsing comparison time further (an order index over the
  contracted tree) is a separate, orthogonal layer; nothing here needs
  it for the metadata claim.
\end{itemize}
Gates, all passing: display identity on all six traces, both families,
raw and composed (walk order equals the chain display; text equals the
final content); about 198k sampled pairwise comparator verdicts
matching display positions with antisymmetry checked; losslessness
exact on every record; the tail-attachment invariant on every entry of
every table; and a 150-trial PBT (8{,}186 events, three replicas,
concurrent edits and merges) gating walk identity, the all-pairs
comparator, and incremental-equals-canonical after every event, while
exercising 872 merges, 315 mid-run splits, 438 liveness splits, 551
coalesces, and 26 vanished-anchor materializations.

The Lean mechanization of this subsection, owed in the previous
revision, is now in place (\code{RunTable.lean}, state-level and
hypothesis-free except where noted); the load-bearing kernel is
Theorem~\ref{thm:tailattach}. Only the epoch-composition clause
(\lean{runTable\_epoch}: sorted state, at-hand domain, chain
representation; walk and read identity after the epoch) carries a
stability hypothesis. Task~\#73 has since closed the remaining theorem
bill: the four mutation rules are promoted from SPOT-pinned
perturbation lemmas (canonicalization under mutation:
\lean{tableOf\_congr}) to \emph{closed} \lean{tableOf} equations, and
the walk is promoted to a literal, table-direct reading procedure. The
sided run table has its own subsection (\S\ref{sec:sidedruntable});
the one face still open is the wire format (Open
Question~\ref{oq:runtable}).

\begin{theorem}[the representation iso, over labels and liveness]
\label{thm:runiso}
Losslessness: for $[] \notin L$, $\mathrm{stateOf}(\mathrm{tableOf}\
L)$ is duplicate-free and membership-exact,
\[
  (\mathrm{stateOf}(\mathrm{tableOf}\ L)).\mathrm{Nodup}
  \;\wedge\; \forall c,\ c \in \mathrm{stateOf}(\mathrm{tableOf}\ L)
  \leftrightarrow c \in L
\]
(\lean{stateOf\_tableOf}; this membership form is the load-bearing
declaration, its unconditional sibling \lean{stateOf\_tableOf\_eq}
being the flatMap computation the proof runs on). Canonicity: for
$\mathrm{Canonical}\ T$, $\mathrm{tableOf}(\mathrm{stateOf}\ T) = T$,
field for field (\lean{tableOf\_stateOf}); without \code{no\_fuse}
this direction is false (a run split in two re-canonicalizes to one
entry, the drift the coalesce rule exists to prevent). Image: every
$\mathrm{tableOf}\ L$ is canonical (\lean{canonical\_tableOf}), so the
pair is a genuine bijection between live-chain sets and canonical
tables. Stated over labels, sides (vacuously R), and liveness,
\emph{not} timestamps: the exact correction the implementation forced,
rank ordinals forgetting time after the first epoch.
\hfill\lean{stateOf\_tableOf}, \lean{tableOf\_stateOf},
\lean{canonical\_tableOf}
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[the contracted comparator equals the key order]
\label{thm:runcmp}
For every ordered prefix code $\Gamma$: if every chain of $L$ is
positive, and $x$, $y$ are kept nodes with $x \neq y$, then
\[
  \mathrm{cmpTable}\ L\ x\ y \;=\; \mathrm{keyLt}\
  (\mathrm{key}(\mathrm{coordOf}_\Gamma\ y))\
  (\mathrm{key}(\mathrm{coordOf}_\Gamma\ x)),
\]
the display comparator of the coded fold. The chain form
(\lean{cmpTable\_iff\_chainBefore}) is the two-case rule of the design
prose; the label order stands where the $(\mathrm{ts},
\mathrm{agent})$ tie oracle rides, on both sides identically.
\hfill\lean{cmpTable\_eq\_keyLt}
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[the structural walk is the display]
\label{thm:runwalk}
For $[] \notin L$: $\mathrm{walk}\ L$ is duplicate-free, contains
exactly the live chains, and is pairwise in $\mathrm{chainBefore}$
order (via \lean{walkE\_sound}/\lean{walkE\_complete}), which pins it
to \emph{the} display sequence, strict orders having unique sorted
enumerations. Key form: descending in $\mathrm{keyLt}$ on the coded
coordinates for every ordered prefix code
(\lean{walk\_pairwise\_keyLt}, adding the positivity hypothesis). No
stability hypothesis.
\hfill\lean{walk\_display}
\end{theorem}

\paragraph{The mutation rules, as closed equations.} Each rule is now
an equation: \lean{tableOf} of the mutated state equals an explicit
rewrite of pre-mutation data. New heads enter the enumeration by sorted
insertion, removed heads leave by filtration, surviving entries keep
their fields except the announced parent rehoming and length
arithmetic, and parent references are re-indexed into the new
enumeration (itself closed, being the equation's own head list). No
stability or honesty hypothesis appears in any of the four. The engine
is a pair of characterization lemmas that force the right-hand sides
rather than check them: any strictly sorted enumeration of the heads
\emph{is} the canonical head list (\lean{headsList\_eq\_of\_iff}), and a
run's length follows from an explicit offset enumeration of its members
(\lean{lenOf\_eq\_of\_iff}). One equation displayed, the other three
grouped.

\begin{theorem}[D1: the mid-run split, closed]
\label{thm:d1split}
Hypotheses, exactly two: $\mathrm{fusible}\ L\ (a \cdot 1) =
\mathrm{true}$ (the anchor $a$ is run-interior: its run successor is
fused) and $a \cdot d \notin \mathrm{keptL}\ L$ (the delivered record
is fresh); $d \neq 1$ is \emph{derived}
(\code{splitc\_d\_ne\_one}), not assumed. Writing $g :=
\mathrm{headOf}\ L\ a$, $j := |a| - |g|$ (the anchor's offset), $\ell
:= \mathrm{lenOf}\ L\ g$: the new table $\mathrm{tableOf}(L + [a
\cdot d])$ is the old one with
\[
  \begin{array}{l@{\;\;}l}
    g: & \mathrm{len} := j + 1 \quad (\text{the anchor run cut at the
         offset}),\\[2pt]
    a \cdot 1: & \langle\, \mathrm{par} = (g,\, j),\ \delta = 1,\
         \mathrm{len} = \ell - j - 1 \,\rangle \quad (\text{the suffix,
         re-headed}),\\[2pt]
    a \cdot d: & \langle\, \mathrm{par} = (g,\, j),\ \delta = d,\
         \mathrm{len} = 1 \,\rangle \quad (\text{the fresh live
         singleton}),\\[2pt]
    h \text{ attached in } g\text{'s run}: & \mathrm{par} :=
         (a \cdot 1,\ \mathrm{off} - (j{+}1)) \quad (\text{rehomed to
         the suffix}),\\[2pt]
    \text{every other } h: & \text{unchanged},
  \end{array}
\]
the two new heads entering at their sorted positions and the suffix
keeping the run's (uniform) liveness; by tail attachment the rehomed
entries are exactly those at the old tail. (The childless-anchor happy
path stays the O(1) typing equation, grouped below.)
\hfill\lean{tableOf\_insert\_split}
\end{theorem}

\begin{remark}
Display dictionary: in the mechanized equation the new enumeration is
literally $(\mathrm{headsList}\ L + [a \cdot 1, a \cdot d])$
re-sorted, parent references re-indexed by index-of into it, and the
suffix liveness is $a \cdot 1 \in L$; the table above is that term
with $g$, $j$, $\ell$ substituted. Nothing else is elided.
\end{remark}

\begin{theorem}[the remaining closed equations: typing, D2, D4, D5]
\label{thm:mutrules}
Each is an equation with all right-hand data pre-mutation; none
carries a stability or honesty hypothesis.
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=2em]
\item \emph{Typing} (\lean{tableOf\_insert\_extend}). Hypotheses: $a
\in L$, $[] \notin L$, $a$ childless in the kept tree ($\forall d$,
$a \cdot d \notin \mathrm{keptL}\ L$). Appending the delta-1 child
re-canonicalizes to the same table with ONE $\mathrm{len}$ field
bumped, at $\mathrm{headOf}\ L\ a$: O(1) table work per keystroke.
\item \emph{D2, the delete liveness split}
(\lean{tableOf\_delete\_split}). Hypotheses: $x \in L$, both edges of
$x$ fused ($\mathrm{fusible}\ L\ x$, $\mathrm{fusible}\ L\ (x \cdot
1)$), and $L'$ = $L$ minus $x$ (membership-exact). The kept tree is
untouched; the run cuts in three at the liveness break: the live
prefix keeps the old head with length cut to $x$'s offset, $x$
survives as a dead singleton entry, the live suffix re-heads at $x
\cdot 1$, and old-tail attachments rehome to the suffix. The boundary
shapes ($x$ a run head, $x$ the tail) split in two rather than three
and are the same construction with one empty side.
\item \emph{D4, the coalesce} (\lean{tableOf\_coalesce}), the closed
delete-inverse of D1. Hypotheses: $a \neq []$, $d \neq 1$, the
interloper $a \cdot d \in L$ and childless in the kept tree, the
suffix $a \cdot 1$ kept with $a$'s liveness ($a \cdot 1 \in L
\leftrightarrow a \in L$), $a$'s only kept children $a \cdot 1$ and
$a \cdot d$, and $L'$ = $L$ minus $a \cdot d$. Both the interloper's
entry and the suffix entry are removed, the anchor's run absorbs the
suffix ($\mathrm{len}$ the sum of the two), and suffix-tail
attachments rehome into the merged run.
\item \emph{D5, directed materialization}
(\lean{tableOf\_materialize}). Hypotheses: $[] \notin L$, the
vanished anchor's parent $m.\mathrm{dropLast} \in L$ and childless in
the kept tree. Delivering $m \cdot d$ inserts two singleton entries
at their sorted head positions, the dead materialized anchor $m$ and
the live record below it, parent references re-indexed. This is the
directed single-anchor shape; the general vanished-path cascade (a
whole dead spine re-materializing, with cuts at non-unit labels and a
possible mid-run split at the re-attachment point) is deliberately
presented as the composite of this rule, applied once per vanished
level, with D1-shaped splits: a genuine cascade, not forced into one
equation.
\end{itemize}
The necessity of D4 is pinned by
\lean{spot\_d4\_coalesce\_necessity}.
\hfill\lean{tableOf\_insert\_extend}, \lean{tableOf\_delete\_split},
\lean{tableOf\_coalesce}, \lean{tableOf\_materialize}
\end{theorem}

\paragraph{The literal walk.} The structural walk behind
Theorem~\ref{thm:runwalk} still consults the state. Its promoted form,
\lean{tableWalk}, consults nothing but the table: entry headers, parent
references, and address arithmetic (each entry's head recovered by
summing deltas along the contracted parent path, each run emitted as
that head followed by its offset extensions, recursion into the entries
attached at the run's tail, newest label first). \lean{stateOf} is
never materialized, and the recursion fuel (one more than the total of
the length fields) is proved sufficient.

\begin{theorem}[the literal walk is faithful]
\label{thm:tablewalk}
For $[] \notin L$:
\[
  \mathrm{tableWalk}\ (\mathrm{tableOf}\ L) \;=\; \mathrm{walk}\ L :
\]
build the table, read it back table-directly, obtain the display of
the live-chain set verbatim. The simulation at matched fuel is
\lean{walkT\_eq\_walkE}; the canonical-table form is
\lean{tableWalk\_eq\_walk}. No stability hypothesis.
\hfill\lean{tableWalk\_tableOf}
\end{theorem}

\noindent This is the formal face of the reading claim the measurement bullets
priced: a full read is one pass over the entries in walk order,
emission per record with no coordinate materialized. The
$\Theta(\text{contracted depth})$ comparator time of the honest-limit
bullet above is a \emph{random-access} cost; a sequential read never
pays it, because the table itself is the order index. The instance file
pins the generic procedure on the D1 state
(\lean{spot\_d1\_tableWalk\_generic}: the concrete SPOT and the
faithfulness theorem name the same reading procedure).

\subsection{The sided run table: the same lever under Fugue}
\label{sec:sidedruntable}

Measurements 3 and 4 already ran the sided (Fugue) family through the
same accounting and reported the decisive number: the side channel
collapses from one flat bit per live level to one bit per entry, 0.061
to 0.174 bits per character. This subsection is that family's verified
counterpart (\code{SidedRunTable.lean}): the full mirror of the
one-sided development of \S\ref{sec:runtable}, over the sided chain
kernel of \S\ref{sec:sided}, state-level and hypothesis-free
throughout (no stability gate, no honest-delivery hypothesis).

\paragraph{Entries, runs, and where the L data lives.} A sided entry
adds one field to the one-sided header: (parent entry and offset,
liveness, \emph{side}, head delta, length). Fusibility condition (3) of
\S\ref{sec:runtable} (the child's side is R), vacuous one-sided, is now
live.

\begin{theorem}[runs are uniformly R; L nodes are heads]
\label{thm:sidedL}
A fusible sided edge's last step is $(R, 1)$ with the parent's
liveness: $\mathrm{sfusible}\ L\ c = \mathrm{true}$ implies the side
of $c$'s last element is R (\lean{sfusible\_side\_R}), so runs are
uniformly R and member sides cost nothing. And every kept node whose
last element is L-sided fails condition (3)
(\lean{Lentry\_not\_fusible}), hence is a head with its own explicit
entry: \lean{Lentry\_isHead}, with hypotheses $c \in \mathrm{skeptL}\
L$ and $c$'s last side $= L$. No further hypotheses.
\hfill\lean{sfusible\_side\_R}, \lean{Lentry\_isHead}
\end{theorem}

\noindent The entire L content of a state is therefore the side bits
of the entry headers, which is the verified shape of the measured
collapse: the representation stores L data nowhere else.

\paragraph{The canonical order and the iso.} The head enumeration is
sorted by \lean{shLt}, short-lex over an \emph{injective} flattening of
the sided word stream: each head chain flattens to its alternating
(side, delta) word, injectivity makes the order strict and total,
parents strictly precede children (flattened lengths double), and the
tie-break is inherited wholesale from the one-sided head order.
Canonical tables (\lean{SCanonical}) carry the side-aware
\lean{no\_fuse} condition: only an R, delta-1, same-liveness
\emph{lone} attachment counts as a missed fuse, so an L singleton
hanging alone at a tail is canonical.

\begin{theorem}[the sided representation iso]
\label{thm:sidediso}
Losslessness: for $[] \notin L$, $\mathrm{sStateOf}(\mathrm{sTableOf}\
L)$ is duplicate-free and membership-exact for $L$
(\lean{sStateOf\_sTableOf}). Canonicity: for $\mathrm{SCanonical}\ T$,
$\mathrm{sTableOf}(\mathrm{sStateOf}\ T) = T$, field for field, the
side field included (\lean{sTableOf\_sStateOf}). Image: every sided
table of a state is canonical (\lean{scanonical\_sTableOf}). Over
labels, sides, and liveness; deliberately \emph{not} over timestamps,
for the same reason as one-sided: rank ordinals forget time, so a
timestamp-preserving iso is false after the first epoch, and the iso
is stated over exactly what survives one.
\hfill\lean{sStateOf\_sTableOf}, \lean{sTableOf\_sStateOf},
\lean{scanonical\_sTableOf}
\end{theorem}

\paragraph{The comparator: the offset-vs-tail exit-side rule.} The
two-case structure of \S\ref{sec:runtable} survives, licensed by the
sided tail-attachment lemma (\lean{stail\_attachment}), and the
divergence case is still a header verdict: two branch entries at the
same tail compare by the banded entry order (L before R; among L
siblings the older label first, among R the newer first). The new
structural content is in the prefix case. One-sided, an ancestor simply
displays before its continuation subtree. Sided, the comparator
consults the ancestor's offset against its run length: a strictly
interior ancestor precedes its subtree \emph{regardless of the exit
side}, while exactly at the run tail the exit side decides (an R exit
displays after the ancestor, an L exit before). This is the run-table
shadow of the kernel's in-order display rule: runs are uniformly R, so
strictly inside a run the only continuation is the $(R,1)$ step toward
the tail, an R extension, and the ancestor wins; an L edge can hang
only at a tail, where every attachment lives, so sidedness surfaces
exactly there.

\begin{theorem}[the sided comparator equals the sided key order]
\label{thm:sidedcmp}
For every ordered prefix code $\Gamma$: if every chain of $L$ is a
positive sided chain, and $x$, $y$ are kept with $x \neq y$, then
\[
  \mathrm{scmpTable}\ L\ x\ y \;=\; \mathrm{keyLt}\
  (\mathrm{sKey}(\mathrm{sidedCoordOf}_\Gamma\ y))\
  (\mathrm{sKey}(\mathrm{sidedCoordOf}_\Gamma\ x)),
\]
refining the kernel's in-order marker theorem (\S\ref{sec:sided}) to
the storage layer; the chain form is
\lean{scmpTable\_iff\_schainBefore}.
\hfill\lean{scmpTable\_eq\_keyLt}
\end{theorem}

\noindent The cost is unchanged from one-sided: contracted depth,
header fields only, no coordinate materialized.

\paragraph{Congruence and the typing equation.} \lean{sTableOf\_congr}:
the sided table is a function of the live sided-chain set, so merges
land wherever delivery order put them and the table cannot tell.
\lean{sTableOf\_insert\_extend}: appending an $(R,1)$ child to a
childless live node changes exactly one length field, O(1) sided table
work per keystroke, exactly as one-sided.

\paragraph{The in-order walk, and its literal form.} The sided walk of
one run: interior members in offset order, then the L subtrees at the
tail (ascending, older label first), then the tail member itself, then
the R subtrees (descending, newer label first); at the virtual root,
the L-rooted subtrees ascending, then the R-rooted descending.
\begin{theorem}[the sided walk is the in-order display]
\label{thm:sidedwalk}
For $[] \notin L$: $\mathrm{swalk}\ L$ is duplicate-free,
membership-exact for the live sided chains, and pairwise in
$\mathrm{schainBefore}$ order (\lean{swalk\_display}); descending in
$\mathrm{keyLt}$ on the sided coordinates for every ordered prefix
code (\lean{swalk\_pairwise\_keyLt}, adding the positivity
hypothesis). The literal form, \lean{stableWalk}, consults entry
headers, parent references, and the same address arithmetic only, with
$\mathrm{sStateOf}$ never materialized; its faithfulness theorem is
\lean{stableWalk\_sTableOf}, mirroring Theorem~\ref{thm:tablewalk}:
build the sided table, read it back, obtain the in-order display
verbatim.
\hfill\lean{swalk\_display}
\end{theorem}

\paragraph{The all-R lockstep bridge.} On an all-R live set the sided
development projects to the one-sided one in lockstep.

\begin{theorem}[the all-R lockstep bridge]
\label{thm:liftR}
Table half, no hypotheses:
\[
  \mathrm{sTableOf}\ (L.\mathrm{map}\ \mathrm{liftR}) \;=\;
  (\mathrm{tableOf}\ L).\mathrm{map}\ \mathrm{liftEntry} :
\]
the sided table of a lifted all-R state \emph{is} the one-sided table
with the side bit stamped R on every entry (\lean{sTableOf\_liftR}).
Walk half, hypotheses every chain of $L$ positive and $[] \notin L$:
\[
  \mathrm{swalk}\ (L.\mathrm{map}\ \mathrm{liftR}) \;=\;
  (\mathrm{walk}\ L).\mathrm{map}\ \mathrm{liftR},
\]
consuming the kernel's fragment theorem \lean{schainBefore\_liftR}
(\S\ref{sec:sided}).
\hfill\lean{sTableOf\_liftR}, \lean{swalk\_liftR}
\end{theorem}

\noindent This is the storage-layer analog of the
convergence-layer ``sides are payload'' theorem
(\S\ref{sec:mechanization}): the one-sided datatype is the
L-uninhabited fragment of the sided one, so every one-sided storage
result of \S\ref{sec:runtable} transports to the sided table on that
fragment with no re-proof.

Per house discipline the file closes with hand-derived PASS+FAIL
executions: the L child that does not fuse where the R child does; the
band order pinned both in the entry order and in the coordinate keys;
the L-band mint displaying L band, then node, then R band; the band
\emph{direction} with its FAIL companion (sorting the L band
newest-first, like the R band, provably flips the display: the tempting
degenerate order is itself refuted); the table-direct walk on the mint
case; and the all-R lockstep pinned on the one-sided D1 state, table
half and walk half. Every capstone named in this subsection and in
\S\ref{sec:runtable} passes its \code{\#print axioms} audit at
\code{propext}/\code{Classical.choice}/\code{Quot.sound} or below;
\code{native\_decide} is confined to the SPOTs.

\subsection{From projection to shipped bytes, and the cross-system question}
\label{sec:crossbench}

Two external facts close the representation arc; both are measured, and
both are laid out with their full methodology in the performance section
(\S\ref{sec:perf}), so only the headline belongs here. First, the
run-table projection of \S\ref{sec:runtable} is no longer merely
measured-in-model: task~\#104 ships a lossless run-table serializer
(\code{runtime/src/serialize.js}), whose \lean{accountingBits} routine
reproduces the projection's bit totals \emph{bit-for-bit} on every trace,
while the shipped bytes land \emph{below} the projection (the model
charges the recoverable positional fields --- per-record run id and
offset, and the tail-attachment parent offset --- that a real encoder
drops). Over a settled-cut compacted state the serializer reaches $1.1$
to $1.3$ bytes per character on the josephg traces, at production save
size and an order of magnitude below the packed absolute-chain estimate.
Second, placed against production CRDT libraries this closes the save-size
gap to rough parity with the smallest state-only production saves, and it
confirms the design's one categorical storage win: tombstone-freedom
means the save \emph{shrinks} on delete where the history-carrying systems
grow. The full cross-system matrix --- per-character apply latency, merge
and sync, load, all four save representations, sync payload, and the
grows-on-delete reproduction --- is developed in \S\ref{sec:perf}.

\section{The marks layer: document-order rich text, and its GC}
\label{sec:marks}

Rich text is the family's first \emph{effect-coupled} extension: marks
(bold, links, comments) are anchored in the sequence but are not
sequence elements. The repository holds two mark encodings. Part~III's
fused Peritext (\S\ref{sec:specs}) encodes a mark as a pair of
in-sequence boundary inserts; the model of this section is the other
encoding, the one the runtime ships as its third datatype
(\S\ref{sec:runtime}): characters live in the embed order, and marks
are \emph{separate immutable records} that name their boundary anchors
by id. Two campaigns are recorded here. Task~\#55 built the read model
and its theorems (\code{Peritext\_Embed/PeritextEmbed\_MarkIntent.lean}),
replacing a retracted claim; task~\#110 built its garbage collector
(design and validation \code{whiteboard/marks-gc-note.md} with harness
\code{whiteboard/litmus/marks\_gc\_check.py}; mechanization
\code{PeritextEmbed\_MarksGC.lean} and
\code{PeritextEmbed\_MarksGC\_A3.lean}; runtime
\code{runtime/src/compact-peritext.js}), moving the runtime's Peritext
state GC from a justified refusal to a certified fire. Verdicts below
are labeled by provenance, as everywhere in this document:
\emph{proved} is kernel-checked Lean, \emph{measured-in-model} is the
checked-in Python harness, \emph{measured} is the shipped JavaScript
runtime's checked-in tests.

\subsection{The document-order mark read model}
\label{sec:markmodel}

\paragraph{State.} Text is an insert-only shadow: a map from character
id (a Lamport stamp, globally unique, exceeding the id of the insert's
anchor) to its immutable birth coordinate, plus a \emph{grow-only set
of deleted ids}. Text deletes are logical because the resolver below
needs the birth positions of \emph{dead} anchors; that retention is
exactly what the GC half of this section must then contend with. Marks
are a grow-only map from mark id $\mathit{mid}$ (the same Lamport
space) to an immutable record $(\mathit{mid}, \mathit{mtype},
\mathit{op}, \mathit{startId}, \mathit{endId}, \mathit{startSide},
\mathit{endSide})$ with $\mathit{op} \in \{\mathsf{add},
\mathsf{remove}\}$: a \textsf{removeMark} is a first-class negative
record, and per $(\text{character}, \mathit{mtype})$ the covering mark
with the highest $\mathit{mid}$ wins (LWW), a winning remove
suppressing the formatting. A per-mark value (a link url, a colour)
rides outside the LWW key. Reading order is the embed order of
\S\ref{sec:design} (birth-chain lexicographic, newer children first);
the live order is that order filtered by the deleted set.

\paragraph{Boundary resolution.} Each boundary resolves to a live
position. For a live anchor the side is gravity, and ``newer than the
mark'' means character id $> \mathit{mid}$; \emph{growth is end-side
only}, the one design decision carried in from validation: an end with
$\mathit{endSide} = \mathit{after}$ grows right over the
newer-than-mark run (typing at the end of a bold span stays bold),
while a $\mathit{before}$ start does \emph{not} grow left, else it
grabs unrelated newer siblings and diverges from the Peritext paper's
semantics. For a dead anchor the boundary \emph{rehomes}: the nearest
surviving character in reading order, scanning from the anchor's birth
position in the side's direction. The eight cases:

\begin{center}\small
\begin{tabular}{@{}lllp{6.1cm}@{}}
\toprule
boundary & anchor & side & resolution \\
\midrule
start & live & $\mathit{before}$ & the span starts at the anchor \\
start & live & $\mathit{after}$ & starts after the anchor, skipping
  the newer-than-mark run \\
start & dead & $\mathit{before}$ & rehome right, the span starting at
  the survivor; scan exhausted: the mark collapses \\
start & dead & $\mathit{after}$ & rehome left, then as the live case
  (the skip applies to the survivor); exhausted: document
  start \\
end & live & $\mathit{after}$ & ends at the anchor, then \emph{grows}
  right over the newer-than-mark run \\
end & live & $\mathit{before}$ & ends before the anchor, stepping back
  over the newer-than-mark run \\
end & dead & $\mathit{after}$ & rehome left, then as the live case
  (the growth applies to the survivor); exhausted: the mark
  collapses \\
end & dead & $\mathit{before}$ & rehome right, then as the live case
  (the step-back applies to the survivor); exhausted: document
  end \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

\begin{definition}[the mark-record state and the document-order read]
\label{def:markmodel}
The document is $d = (\mathit{shadow}, \mathit{deleted})$
(\lean{DocD}): an insert-only embed state (every insert ever applied,
payload the codepoint) plus the grow-only deleted-id list. The
\emph{birth order} is the shadow's id sequence in embed reading order
(\lean{DocD.birthIds}); the \emph{live order} is the birth order
filtered by $\mathit{deleted}$ (\lean{DocD.liveIds}). A mark is the
immutable record $(\mathit{mid}, \mathit{mtype}, \mathit{op},
\mathit{value}, \mathit{startId}, \mathit{endId}, \mathit{startSide},
\mathit{endSide})$ (\lean{MarkD}; two frozen ancestry snapshots ride
along for the retracted tree control alone and are ignored by the
resolver). The resolver returns option-valued \emph{live indices}:
$\mathrm{startIncl}\ d\ m$ is the first covered index and
$\mathrm{endExcl}\ d\ m$ the exclusive upper index, each computed by
the eight-case table above, in \emph{resolve-then-side} order: a dead
anchor first rehomes by the side's nearest-survivor scan from its
birth position (exhaustion giving the side's collapse or document
sentinel), and the boundary anchor, live or so rehomed, then takes
its side's rule (gravity; the newer-than-mark run --- contiguous live
ids $> \mathit{mid}$ --- skipped at an $\mathit{after}$ start and
grown over at an $\mathit{after}$ end); $\mathrm{none}$ is the
collapsed span. Coverage is the
half-open interval:
\[
\begin{aligned}
  &\mathrm{markCoversPos}\ d\ m\ k \;=\;
  \bigl(f \le k \,\wedge\, k < e\bigr) \\
  &\qquad\text{when}\quad
  \mathrm{startIncl}\ d\ m = \mathrm{some}\ f,\
  \mathrm{endExcl}\ d\ m = \mathrm{some}\ e,
\end{aligned}
\]
and false when either boundary collapses. Per $(\text{position},
\mathit{mtype})$ the covering mark with the largest $\mathit{mid}$
wins (\lean{bestCover}), and the position is formatted iff that
winner is an \textsf{add} (\lean{fmtAt}). The render
$\mathrm{renderMarksDoc}\ d\ \mathit{marks}\ \mathit{mt}$ is the live
order with each character's flag,
$\mathrm{renderFlagWith}\ d\ (\mathrm{markCoversPos}\ d)$; the
retracted control $\mathrm{renderMarksTree}$ is the same render over
$\mathrm{treeCoversPos}$ (climb the frozen path to the nearest live
ancestor and cover the inclusive interval between the two resolved
anchors, sides ignored).
\hfill\lean{DocD}, \lean{MarkD}, \lean{renderMarksDoc}
\end{definition}

\paragraph{The positive theorem, and the retraction it replaces.} An
earlier composed-Peritext read resolved a dead boundary by climbing
\emph{tree} ancestry (frozen path snapshots), and carried a
\lean{mark\_*\_no\_leak} theorem asserting that formatting never leaks
under deletion. That claim was \textbf{false} and is retracted: a tree
ancestor sits \emph{earlier in reading order} than its descendants, so
a dead boundary anchor migrates backward in the document and formats
text that was never in the span. The document-order model is built so
that the replacement is provable: rehoming scans \emph{reading} order,
so a single-replica rehome can only shrink or hold a span, never
migrate it backward.

\begin{theorem}[no backward leak, document order]
\label{thm:noleak}
For every document $d$, mark $m$, type $\mathit{mt}$, and live
indices $f$, $k$: if $\mathrm{startIncl}\ d\ m = \mathrm{some}\ f$
and $k < f$, then
\[
  \mathrm{fmtAt}\ (\mathrm{markCoversPos}\ d)\ [m]\ \mathit{mt}\ k
  \;=\; \mathsf{false} :
\]
no character strictly before the mark's rehomed start carries that
mark's formatting. Stated at the singleton mark list $[m]$, which is
exactly the per-mark claim: $\mathrm{markCoversPos}$ is
list-independent, so in any larger list $m$ still covers nothing
before $f$, and a position there can be formatted only by
\emph{another} mark's own span. Proved on axioms
$\{\mathrm{propext}\}$ alone, below even the document's kernel-clean
baseline.
\hfill\lean{doc\_no\_backward\_leak}
\end{theorem}

\begin{proposition}[the tree-ancestry read leaks backward]
\label{prop:treeleak}
There exist a document $d$, a mark $m$, and a live position $k$ with
\[
  \mathrm{markCoversPos}\ d\ m\ k = \mathsf{false}
  \quad\wedge\quad
  \mathrm{treeCoversPos}\ d\ m\ k = \mathsf{true} :
\]
the retracted claim's own resolver formats a character the
document-order read does not, so no analogue of
Theorem~\ref{thm:noleak} holds for the frozen-path read. The witness
is concrete: delete a bold span's start anchor; document order
rehomes the start forward to the next survivor
(\lean{leak\_doc\_shrinks\_doc}), while the tree read climbs to the
anchor's parent and formats text that was never bold
(\lean{leak\_doc\_shrinks\_tree}), with the $\ne$ companion
\lean{leak\_doc\_shrinks} pinning that the two renders genuinely
differ.
\hfill\lean{tree\_backward\_leak\_refutes\_noleak}
\end{proposition}

\paragraph{The Litt et al.\ pins.} The model is pinned against the
worked examples of the Peritext paper (Litt et al.\ CSCW'22), each a
concrete SPOT with a hand-derived expected render and a $\neq$
companion refuting the tempting degenerate: insertion within a bold
span is formatted (Ex\,1); overlapping bold and italic carry both on
the overlap (Ex\,2); marking a span, deleting the whole span, and
reinserting yields plain text under document order, while the tree
control leaks the formatting back (Ex\,3, the leak made concrete);
concurrent add versus removeMark resolves LWW by $\mathit{mid}$, and
swapping which is newer flips the verdict (Ex\,5); typing at the end
of a bold span is grabbed exactly when the typed text is newer than
the mark (Ex\,7); and the gravity contrast (Ex\,8): on the same
insertion, a bold span ($\mathit{endSide} = \mathit{after}$) expands
and a link ($\mathit{endSide} = \mathit{before}$) does not.
Convergence is watched at the same grain: the render is a function of
the mark \emph{set} (permutation invariance, with a dropped-mark FAIL
pin); character order converges by the embed capstone.

\paragraph{The honesty theorem: the atomicity cost, stated.} The
substrate is tombstone-free and live, so the mark-positioning trilemma
(atomic mark placement, tombstone-freedom, live state: pick two;
\S\ref{sec:specs}) charges this model too, and it pays on the growth
rule.

\begin{proposition}[a delete can re-span: the trilemma horn]
\label{prop:respan}
There is a four-character document and a bold mark --- concretely:
$A, B$ marked ($\mathit{endSide} = \mathit{after}$), $C$ older than
the mark blocking the growth run, $D$ newer, with the hand-derived
render pins \lean{respan\_before} ($A, B$ bold; $C, D$ plain) and
\lean{respan\_after} ($A, B, D$ bold) --- such that deleting the
plain, boundary-untouched $C$ (id $3$) changes an untouched
survivor's formatting:
\[
  \mathrm{renderMarksDocIds}\ d_{\mathrm{after}}\ [m]\ \mathsf{bold}
  \;\ne\;
  (\mathrm{renderMarksDocIds}\ d_{\mathrm{before}}\ [m]\
  \mathsf{bold}).\mathrm{filter}\ (\lambda e.\ e.1 \ne 3) :
\]
the post-delete render is \emph{not} the pre-delete render minus the
deleted character's entry, because $C$'s deletion makes $D$
contiguous with the span and the $\mathit{after}$-end growth run
reaches it.
\hfill\lean{doc\_delete\_can\_respan}
\end{proposition}

\noindent This is a membership re-span, not a backward leak
(Theorem~\ref{thm:noleak} is untouched), and the contrast with the
fused encoding is exact: the fused design has no growth rule and
proves \lean{renderIds\_del} (a character delete re-formats nothing);
the marks model trades that theorem for the paper's
expand-at-the-end behaviour, and the price is stated as a theorem
rather than hidden.

\subsection{The GC problem, and why refusal was correct}
\label{sec:marksrefusal}

The state-GC pass of \S\ref{sec:compactED}, as shipped in the runtime
(\S\ref{sec:runtime}), drops settled-dead records and re-codes
coordinates. For plain text that is sound; for this datatype, pruning
a dead anchor's record destroys exactly the birth position a mark
needs to rehome, and the runtime therefore \emph{refused} to compact
Peritext at all. The refusal was correct, and the justification is a
directed flip (D6 in the note's numbering, hand-derived). Geometry,
reused throughout this section: $R$ (id 1) and $L$ (id 2) minted
concurrently at the root, so $L$ is newer and reads first; $d$ (id 3)
a child of $L$; reading order $L\,d\,R$. Mark $[\text{start } L\
\mathit{before},\ \text{end } d\ \mathit{after}]$, $\mathit{mid} = 5$;
delete $d$; compact at a settled cut with no retention. The
never-compacted twin: the dead end anchor $d$ scans left and finds
$L$; covered $\{L\}$, so $L$ is bold. The unretained subject: $d$ has
no record, the resolver cannot place the scan and degrades to the
collapse branch; covered $\{\}$, $L$ is plain. The read flips. The
companion pins the cause: the same compactor with all mark anchors
\emph{live} reads twin-identical, so the refusal's reason is precisely
dead mark anchors, nothing else. Randomized (measured-in-model): the
no-retention control flips 351 of 600 randomized DAG trials.

\subsection{Two attacks, falsifiable forms first}
\label{sec:marksattacks}

The settled-cut contract is the text layer's (\S\ref{sec:compactED}):
every replica has delivered everything at or below the cut, and every
op concurrent with the cut that is not yet delivered is
\emph{declared} in flight (its id and anchor id). The compaction map
is also the text layer's: drop settled-dead records outside the
keep-set, renumber each sibling group of the kept tree
order-preservingly except groups receiving a declared in-flight delta
(frozen verbatim, the \lean{skipAt} guard), and let beyond-cut mints
derive their coordinate from the re-coded anchor record plus the fresh
delta (the extension law H3 realized as derive-at-ingest). The two
attacks differ only in the keep-set and the mark treatment, and each
was stated as a falsifiable reads-identical claim before any trial
ran.

\paragraph{H-A: retention roots, validated.} Keep-set $=$ live ids
$\cup$ the boundary anchor ids of every present mark record (add and
remove records both) $\cup$ declared in-flight anchors. Dead boundary
anchors survive as dead records with re-coded coordinates; the marks
map is untouched. Falsifier: any version, on any beyond-cut
continuation (text and mark ops on any replica, merged in any order,
declared stragglers delivered late, successive cuts composed), whose
render differs from the never-compacted twin. Verdict
(measured-in-model): \textbf{validated}, 2000/2000 randomized DAG
trials (4048 cuts, 2214 with declared stragglers), zero divergences.
Cost (measured-in-model): 0.296 retained dead records per mark record,
maximum 2.000 against the structural bound of 2 (a mark has two
boundaries); 30.6 percent of settled-dead records dropped under a
deliberately mark-heavy random workload (real traces mark far fewer
characters per edit and would drop a larger share).

\paragraph{H-B: early re-anchoring, refuted.} At the cut, rewrite each
\emph{dead} mark boundary to the resolver's own cut-time rehoming
target (side kept; an exhausted scan becomes the side's collapse
sentinel), then retain nothing for marks. The rewrite commits at the
cut to information the resolver would otherwise read at render time
(the dead anchor's position among \emph{later} arrivals), and the
minimal countermodel family (D1, all cells hand-derived, then
machine-confirmed cell by cell) makes that exact. Same geometry as D6;
one continuation character $n$: (a) a beyond-cut insert, id 10 (newer
than the mark), anchored at $L$; (b) a declared straggler, id 4
(\emph{older} than the mark), anchored at $L$, delivered after
compaction; (c) the same straggler anchored at $d$ itself. Covered
sets, twin versus H-B subject, per boundary combination (the mark's
boundary at $d$, the other on a survivor):

\begin{center}\small
\begin{tabular}{@{}llll@{}}
\toprule
combo (boundary at $d$) & (a) id 10 at $L$ & (b) id 4 at $L$ &
  (c) id 4 at $d$ \\
\midrule
start $\mathit{before}$ (inner) & $\{R\} = \{R\}$ & $\{R\} = \{R\}$ &
  $\{n,R\}$ vs $\{R\}$: \textbf{diverges} \\
start $\mathit{after}$ (outer) & $\{R\} = \{R\}$ &
  $\{R\}$ vs $\{n,R\}$: \textbf{diverges} & $\{n,R\} = \{n,R\}$ \\
end $\mathit{after}$ (inner) & $\{L,n\} = \{L,n\}$ &
  $\{L,n\}$ vs $\{L\}$: \textbf{diverges} & $\{L\} = \{L\}$ \\
end $\mathit{before}$ (outer) & $\{L\} = \{L\}$ & $\{L,n\} = \{L,n\}$ &
  $\{L\}$ vs $\{L,n\}$: \textbf{diverges} \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

\noindent Worked sample, end $\mathit{after}$, variant (b). Twin:
birth order $L\,n\,d\,R$; the dead end anchor $d$ scans left and finds
$n$ live, so the span ends at $n$: covered $\{L, n\}$. H-B rewrote the
end at the cut, before $n$ existed, to $L$; at render the end sits at
$L$ and end-growth does not cross $n$, whose id 4 is below the mark's
$\mathit{mid}$ 5: covered $\{L\}$. The straggler sits inside the
twin's span and outside the subject's.

\paragraph{The compensation finding.} Column (a) is the negative space
of the countermodel, and it is exact rather than lucky: with only a
beyond-cut \emph{newer} insert, all four combinations agree. Every
character that appears between the rewrite target and the dead
anchor's old position after a settled cut is either a beyond-cut mint
(id above every settled id, hence above the mark's $\mathit{mid}$, so
the newer-run growth and skip rules cross it on \emph{both} sides of
the comparison) or a declared straggler, the only source of ids below
the mark's $\mathit{mid}$. And nothing beyond-cut can land between a
settled-dead anchor and its reading-order successor without anchoring
in the dead subtree, which a settled cut makes invisible to future
minters; only a straggler anchored at the dead node itself can, which
is column (c). So the naive recency candidate (a beyond-cut newer
insert alone) \emph{provably cannot fire}: the H-B countermodel needs
a straggler older than the mark (columns b, c) or the death of the
rewrite target. The latter is D1(d), hand-derived: chain $L$ (1) at
the root, $d$ (2) a child of $L$; mark $[\text{start } L\
\mathit{before},\ \text{end } d\ \mathit{after}]$, $\mathit{mid} = 5$;
delete $d$; cut (H-B rewrites the end to $L$); beyond the cut insert
$n$ (10) under $L$, then delete $L$. Twin: birth $L\,n\,d$, live
$\{n\}$; the start rehomes right to $n$, the dead end $d$ scans left
and finds $n$: covered $\{n\}$, $n$ is bold. Subject: the rewritten
end anchor $L$ is now dead too and scans \emph{left} from $L$'s
position, where nothing lives ($n$ sits to $L$'s right, unreachable by
that scan): the mark collapses and $n$ is plain. It diverges with no
straggler anywhere: the rewrite also forgot that $d$ sat to the
\emph{right} of $L$, so the re-rehoming scans from the wrong birth
position.

\paragraph{The weaker true statement, labeled as what it is.} The
rewrite is computed once at the common settled cut, so every compacted
replica shares it, and all compacted replicas \emph{converge among
themselves}: machine-checked separately, 2000/2000 trials. This is
\textbf{licensed divergence}, a semantic change at a settled cut, and
it is never reads-identical: 156 of the 2000 randomized trials
diverged from the never-compacted twin, on top of the directed family
above. It must not be stated as H-B succeeding, and is recorded here
as the price a re-anchoring design would pay, not as a result.

\paragraph{D3: a dead run keeps its internal order.} Two boundaries
inside one dead run are observably \emph{ordered}. Chars $A$ (1) at
the root, $x$ (2) under $A$, $y$ (3) under $x$, $B$ (4) under $y$;
mark $m_1$ covers $[A..x]$ ($\mathit{mid}$ 10), $m_2$ covers $[A..y]$
($\mathit{mid}$ 11), both inner sides; delete $x$ and $y$ (one dead
run); cut; declared straggler $g$ (5) anchored at $x$, delivered after
compaction. $g$ is a newer child of $x$ than $y$, so birth order is
$A\,x\,g\,y\,B$. Twin: $m_1$'s end ($x$, dead) scans left to $A$:
covered $\{A\}$; $m_2$'s end ($y$, dead) scans left to the live $g$:
covered $\{A, g\}$. So $g$ carries $m_2$ and not $m_1$: the two
boundaries' order inside the dead run is observable. H-B rewrote both
ends to $A$ at the cut (the run's left survivor): both marks cover
$\{A\}$ and $g$ loses $m_2$. H-A retains $x$ and $y$ as dead records
whose re-coded coordinates keep their relative order, and reads
exactly as the twin; that order preservation on retained-dead pairs is
the A1 obligation the mechanization below discharges.

\subsection{The growth window: the guarded root-freeing drop}
\label{sec:markswindow}

Plain H-A retains a mark's anchors as long as the mark record exists,
and mark records are grow-only, so a heavily reformatted document
accumulates $(\mathsf{add}, \mathsf{remove})$ pairs whose anchors are
retained forever. The A3 refinement drops such a pair and frees its
retention roots: an add $m$ and a remove $r$ of the same
$\mathit{mtype}$ with \emph{equal boundary tuples} (ids and sides) and
$r.\mathit{mid} > m.\mathit{mid}$ resolve to the same covered interval
forever, and $r$ beats $m$ on every covered character, so dropping
both looks render-neutral. It is sound only under \emph{three} guards,
each with a hand-derived countermodel:
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=2em]
\item \emph{Removal settled, declared in-flights included} (the
  $\alpha$ flip): a concurrent same-type add $s$ with $m.\mathit{mid}
  < s.\mathit{mid} < r.\mathit{mid}$, still in flight at the drop, is
  suppressed by $r$ on the overlap while the pair is present and
  formats it once the pair is dropped.
\item \emph{No LWW shadowing}: no other same-type mark with
  $\mathit{mid}$ below $r.\mathit{mid}$ may exist, because spans move
  under later edits, so a no-overlap check \emph{now} does not
  persist; dropping $r$ could resurrect the older mark on a future
  overlap.
\item \emph{The growth window} (the $\beta$ flip, the discovery of
  this phase): no settled or declared id may lie strictly inside
  $(m.\mathit{mid}, r.\mathit{mid})$. A character $w$ with
  $m.\mathit{mid} < w.\mathit{id} < r.\mathit{mid}$ is grabbed by the
  add's end-growth ($w.\mathit{id} > m.\mathit{mid}$) but \emph{not}
  by the remove's ($w.\mathit{id} < r.\mathit{mid}$ fails its newer
  test), so $w$ is bold with the pair present and plain after the
  drop: the pair is not render-neutral even though its boundaries are
  identical. Beyond-cut mints are Lamport-fresh (above
  $r.\mathit{mid}$), so checking the settled and declared ids at the
  cut is sufficient.
\end{itemize}
Both unguarded flips and the guarded positive (with a genuinely dead
root freed that plain H-A would have retained) are hand-derived and
machine-checked. Guarded, on top of H-A (measured-in-model): 600/600
twin trials with 44 pair-drops fired; each drop removes two mark
records and frees their anchors for the next cut.

\subsection{The mechanization}
\label{sec:marksmech}

The note's obligations O1--O4 are mechanized in
\code{PeritextEmbed\_MarksGC.lean} (O1--O3) and
\code{PeritextEmbed\_MarksGC\_A3.lean} (O4); O5, the settled-cut
protocol half, is deliberately \emph{not} taken on, being the
already-tracked residue below.

\paragraph{O1: the keep-set is a stable-prefix map, not a new
notion.}

\begin{definition}[the keep-set stable-prefix map]
\label{def:keepspm}
A keep-set is $K = (\mathrm{live}, \mathrm{markAnchor},
\mathrm{inflight})$ (\lean{KeepSpec}), three coordinate predicates,
with $K.\mathrm{kept}\ c = \mathrm{live}\ c \vee
\mathrm{markAnchor}\ c \vee \mathrm{inflight}\ c$: the live records,
the boundary anchors of present and declared in-flight mark records
(dead anchors included --- the retention roots), and the anchors of
declared in-flight text ops. Given a relabeling $f$ and declared
mints $\mathrm{Mint}$ satisfying
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=2em]
\item[($h_{\mathrm{ext}}$)] $\mathrm{Mint}\ \pi\ d$ implies
  $f(\pi \cdot \Gamma.\mathrm{enc}(d)) = f(\pi) \cdot
  \Gamma.\mathrm{enc}(d)$, and
\item[($h_{\mathrm{ord}}$)] for $c$, $c'$ each kept or mint-shaped
  ($\exists \pi\, d$, $\mathrm{Mint}\ \pi\ d \wedge c = \pi \cdot
  \Gamma.\mathrm{enc}(d)$):
  $\mathrm{keyLt}(\mathrm{key}(f\,c))\,(\mathrm{key}(f\,c')) =
  \mathrm{keyLt}(\mathrm{key}\ c)\,(\mathrm{key}\ c')$,
\end{itemize}
$\mathrm{keepSPM}\ \Gamma\ K\ f\ \mathrm{Mint}$ is the
\emph{existing} stable-prefix bundle of Definition~\ref{def:spm} with
$\mathrm{Rest} := K.\mathrm{kept}$ and $\mathrm{MintAt} :=
\mathrm{Mint}$: $h_{\mathrm{ext}}$ is H3 and $h_{\mathrm{ord}}$ is
H2, field for field. H3's $\mathrm{Mint}$ covers fresh mints
\emph{and} declared stragglers, whose frozen sibling groups are
exactly the text layer's skipped-groups side condition, with the FAIL
companion \lean{freeze\_guard\_violation} pinning the flip (deltas
$2 < 5$ in-flight $< 9$ renumber to $1, 2$ and the frozen $5$ jumps
the ex-$9$).
\hfill\lean{KeepSpec}, \lean{keepSPM}
\end{definition}

\begin{lemma}[A1, with H1 derived]
\label{lem:keepa1}
For kept coordinates $c$, $c'$:
\[
  \mathrm{keyLt}(\mathrm{key}(f\,c))\,(\mathrm{key}(f\,c'))
  \;=\; \mathrm{keyLt}(\mathrm{key}\ c)\,(\mathrm{key}\ c') :
\]
the relabeling preserves the display comparator on the whole kept
set, retained-dead pairs included, which is exactly what the D3
observation (\S\ref{sec:marksattacks}) consumes. Injectivity on kept
pairs is derived through the bundle
(\lean{keepSPM\_injOn\_kept}), not assumed. \lean{keepSPM\_A1} is
proved with \emph{no axioms at all}.
\hfill\lean{keepSPM\_A1}
\end{lemma}

\paragraph{O2: scan factorization, a pure list lemma.}

\begin{lemma}[scan factorization]
\label{lem:scanfactor}
For every split $l_1 \cdot a \cdot l_2$ of the birth order, deleted
list $\mathit{del}$, and keep predicate $p$ with $p\,a =
\mathsf{true}$, $a \notin l_1$, and every survivor of $l_2$ kept
($\forall x \in l_2$, $x \notin \mathit{del} \Rightarrow p\,x =
\mathsf{true}$):
\[
\begin{aligned}
  &\mathrm{scanRight}\ \bigl((l_1 \cdot a \cdot
  l_2).\mathrm{filter}\ p\bigr)\ \mathit{del}\
  \bigl(\mathrm{idxOf}\ ((l_1 \cdot a \cdot l_2).\mathrm{filter}\
  p)\ a\bigr) \\
  &\qquad=\;
  \mathrm{scanRight}\ (l_1 \cdot a \cdot l_2)\ \mathit{del}\
  \bigl(\mathrm{idxOf}\ (l_1 \cdot a \cdot l_2)\ a\bigr) :
\end{aligned}
\]
the first survivor strictly right of the retained element $a$ is the
same in the filtered and the full sequence, because every dropped
element is a non-survivor the scan skips anyway. Mirror statement
\lean{scanLeft\_filter}, with the kept-survivors hypothesis on $l_1$.
No datatype content: \lean{List.filter} and \lean{find?} over an
arbitrary split.
\hfill\lean{scanRight\_filter}, \lean{scanLeft\_filter}
\end{lemma}

\paragraph{O3: the render-congruence capstone.} The load-bearing
observation: \lean{renderMarksDoc} is a function of the live id
sequence, the boundary anchors' positions relative to it, and the id
order; it never reads a coordinate. Its hypothesis structure is
displayed first:

\begin{definition}[the continuation discipline]
\label{def:contok}
$\mathrm{ContOK}\ \Gamma\ s\ \tau$, for a cut state $s$ and
continuation $\tau$, has four fields:
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=2em]
\item[(\emph{freshId})] every insert $(t, r, \ins(e, \pi, a)) \in
  \tau$ has $t \notin \mathrm{ids}(s)$;
\item[(\emph{nodupIns})] $\tau$'s insert ids are duplicate-free;
\item[(\emph{freshKeySt})] every insert of $\tau$ mints a key
  $\mathrm{key}(\pi \cdot \Gamma.\mathrm{enc}(t - a))$ distinct from
  every stored record's key;
\item[(\emph{freshKeyPair})] distinct inserts of $\tau$ mint
  distinct keys.
\end{itemize}
This is what any honest beyond-cut continuation satisfies (Lamport
freshness plus unique decodability); \emph{deriving} it, together
with the straggler declarations, from honest reachability at a
certified cut is nothing marks-specific: it is the note's O5, the
per-continuation face of the settled-cut protocol residue. The
honesty-rebasing half of that residue, the $(\ast)$ lemma, is
\emph{closed} for the text layer
(Definition~\ref{def:caf}--Theorem~\ref{thm:starclosed},
\S\ref{sec:compactED}); what remains is the protocol half --- Open
Questions~\ref{oq:starlemma} and~\ref{oq:epochdag} --- and the marks
layer adds no new obligation to it.
\hfill\lean{ContOK}
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[O3: render congruence under retention-roots
compaction]
\label{thm:marksgc}
For a stable-prefix map $F$; cut state $s$; continuation $\tau$; keep
predicate $\mathit{kp}$; deleted list $\mathit{del}$; mark list
$\mathit{marks}$; type $\mathit{mt}$; under the hypotheses:
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=2em]
\item[($h_{\mathrm{sort}}$, $h_{\mathrm{nd}}$)] $s$ is strictly
  key-sorted and its ids are duplicate-free;
\item[($h_{\mathrm{ok}}$)] $\mathrm{ContOK}\ \Gamma\ s\ \tau$
  (Definition~\ref{def:contok});
\item[($h_{\mathrm{kp}}$)] every insert of $\tau$ is kept
  ($\mathit{kp}$ true at its id);
\item[($h_{\mathrm{dom}}$)] every kept record of $s$ is at hand
  ($F.\mathrm{Dom}$ of its coordinate);
\item[($h_{\mathrm{mint}}$)] every insert $(t, r, \ins(e, \pi, a))
  \in \tau$ is a declared mint ($F.\mathrm{MintAt}\ \pi\ (t - a)$);
\item[($h_{\mathrm{dead}}$)] every dropped record's id is recorded
  dead ($\mathit{kp}$ false at $r.1$ implies $r.1 \in \mathit{del}$);
\item[($h_{\mathrm{anchor}}$)] every present mark's two boundary ids
  are kept (the retention roots),
\end{itemize}
the compacted-and-continued document renders equal to the
never-compacted twin:
\[
\begin{aligned}
  &\mathrm{renderMarksDoc}\ \bigl(\mathrm{applySeq}\
  (\mathrm{remap}_F\ (s.\mathrm{filter}\ \mathit{kp}))\
  (\tau.\mathrm{map}\ \mathrm{remap}_F),\ \mathit{del}\bigr)\
  \mathit{marks}\ \mathit{mt} \\
  &\qquad=\;
  \mathrm{renderMarksDoc}\ \bigl(\mathrm{applySeq}\ s\ \tau,\
  \mathit{del}\bigr)\ \mathit{marks}\ \mathit{mt},
\end{aligned}
\]
for every mark and every type. Kernel-clean. The proof chains the
re-coding cluster's T1 (Theorem~\ref{thm:recode1} transports the
compacted fold), coordinate invisibility of the render, commutation
of the continuation with the drop, and O2
(Lemma~\ref{lem:scanfactor}) plus the retention roots.
\hfill\lean{marksGC\_render\_congr}
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[O3, two epochs]
\label{thm:marksgc2}
For maps $F_1$, $F_2$ with $\mathrm{CompatOn}\ F_1\ F_2\
\mathrm{Rest}'\ \mathrm{MintAt}'$ (Definition~\ref{def:spmcomp});
keep predicates $\mathit{kp}_1$, $\mathit{kp}_2$; continuations
$\tau_1$, $\tau_2$; under Theorem~\ref{thm:marksgc}'s hypothesis
list taken twice: epoch~1's clauses ($h_{\mathrm{ok}_1}$,
$h_{\mathrm{kp}_1}$, $h_{\mathrm{dom}_1}$, $h_{\mathrm{mint}_1}$)
verbatim at $(s, \tau_1, \mathit{kp}_1, F_1)$, and epoch~2's at the
once-continued state and the \emph{composite}: $h_{\mathrm{ok}_2}$
at $\mathrm{applySeq}\ s\ \tau_1$; $h_{\mathrm{kp}_2}$,
$h_{\mathrm{dead}}$, $h_{\mathrm{anchor}}$ at the conjunction
$\mathit{kp}_1 \wedge \mathit{kp}_2$; $h_{\mathrm{dom}G}$,
$h_{\mathrm{mint}G}$ at $F_1.\mathrm{comp}\ F_2$'s surviving domain
$(\mathrm{Rest}', \mathrm{MintAt}')$. Conclusion: compact at a
settled cut, continue, compact again on the already re-coded
records, and deliver the twice-lagging $\tau_2$ translated through
the composite; the final render equals the never-compacted twin's
(the display elides the nested remaps; the mechanized statement
carries them in full). Kernel-clean. The composite is the existing
\lean{StablePrefixMap.comp} carried at its own \emph{surviving}
domain: a retention root freed between the epochs (by an A3
pair-drop) is exactly the rank-reclaim collision shape
\lean{naive\_composition\_collides} pins (\S\ref{sec:compactED}),
and restricting the composite's domain to the survivors is the fix
that file already provides. $n$-epoch towers follow by the same
\lean{chainSPM}/\lean{CompatOn} route.
\hfill\lean{marksGC\_render\_congr\_twoEpoch}
\end{theorem}

\paragraph{O4: the guarded drop.}

\begin{theorem}[O4: the guarded pair-drop]
\label{thm:a3drop}
For a document $d$, mark list $\mathit{marks}$, and $m, r \in
\mathit{marks}$ with $m.\mathit{op} = \mathsf{add}$, $r.\mathit{op}
= \mathsf{remove}$, $r.\mathit{mtype} = m.\mathit{mtype}$,
$m.\mathit{mid} < r.\mathit{mid}$, equal boundary tuples
($r.\mathit{startId} = m.\mathit{startId}$, $r.\mathit{endId} =
m.\mathit{endId}$, $r.\mathit{startSide} = m.\mathit{startSide}$,
$r.\mathit{endSide} = m.\mathit{endSide}$), and
$\mathit{marks}$'s $\mathit{mid}$s duplicate-free; under the three
guards:
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=2em]
\item[(G1)] \emph{removal settled, declared in-flights included}:
  the statement is over the \emph{final delivered} document and mark
  list, so a cut-time drop is licensed exactly when the settled
  marks plus the declared in-flight marks exhaust the final list's
  candidates --- beyond-cut Lamport freshness (every later
  $\mathit{mid}$ above $r.\mathit{mid}$) covers the rest, which is
  the $\alpha$ flip's discharge;
\item[(G2)] \emph{no LWW shadowing} ($h_{\mathrm{others}}$): every
  other same-type mark $o \in \mathit{marks}$ (with $o.\mathit{mid}
  \ne m.\mathit{mid}$, $o.\mathit{mid} \ne r.\mathit{mid}$) has
  $r.\mathit{mid} < o.\mathit{mid}$;
\item[(G3)] \emph{the growth window} ($h_{\mathrm{window}}$, the
  $\beta$ clause, the discovery of this phase): every birth id $c$
  of $d$ satisfies
  \[
    c \le m.\mathit{mid} \;\vee\; r.\mathit{mid} < c :
  \]
  \emph{no id in the half-open window} $(m.\mathit{mid},
  r.\mathit{mid}]$ (the formula also excludes $c = r.\mathit{mid}$):
  such a character is grabbed by the add's end-growth but not
  the remove's, so the pair is not render-neutral even with
  identical boundaries,
\end{itemize}
dropping the pair preserves the render:
\[
\begin{aligned}
  &\mathrm{renderMarksDoc}\ d\
  \bigl(\mathit{marks}.\mathrm{filter}\ (\lambda o.\ o.\mathit{mid}
  \notin \{m.\mathit{mid}, r.\mathit{mid}\})\bigr)\ \mathit{mt} \\
  &\qquad=\; \mathrm{renderMarksDoc}\ d\ \mathit{marks}\ \mathit{mt} .
\end{aligned}
\]
Only $r.\mathit{op} = \mathsf{remove}$ is load-bearing for the
verdict (the $\mathsf{add}$ hypothesis names the shape, the proof
never consults it).
\hfill\lean{a3\_guarded\_drop}
\end{theorem}

\noindent The SPOT block is PASS+FAIL shaped throughout: the guarded
positive fired \emph{through the theorem}, the freed-root payoff (the
next cut drops the ex-anchor's record and still matches the twin that
holds both marks), a surviving-rival-mark variant, and both flips
($\alpha$, $\beta$) each with a guard-refuses companion.

\paragraph{One honest flag.} The Lean model and the Python harness
diverge on one degenerate branch: a boundary anchor with \emph{no
record at all}. The Lean resolver's scan then starts past the end of
the birth order (finding the last, respectively first, survivor); the
Python harness degrades to the side's collapse sentinel. The two
disagree on the degenerate \emph{value}, but D6 flips against the twin
either way, which is the only load-bearing fact, pinned on both sides
(\lean{d6\_no\_retention\_flip}; the harness's D6), and the positive
obligations never touch the branch: retained anchors always have
records. The general lemmas and both capstones are kernel-clean; the
SPOTs use \lean{native\_decide} under the document's SPOT convention.

\subsection{The runtime: refuse becomes fire-with-certificate}
\label{sec:marksruntime}

\paragraph{The shipped compactor.} \code{compact-peritext.js}
transliterates the harness semantics. The keep-set is as above; the
re-coding, the order-preserving rank renumbering, and the in-flight
freeze guard reuse the text layer's \code{compactEliasDelta} verbatim
over the kept shadow; marks reference \emph{ids}, which re-coding
never rewrites, so the mark map itself needs no translation; retained
dead anchors stay listed in the deleted set. \code{compactiblePeritext}
plugs the hooks into \code{DistributedReplica}: \code{compactStable}
for Peritext now \emph{fires} under the same frontier certificate as
the text layer (\S\ref{sec:runtime}), where it previously refused,
and the A3 pair-drop runs first so freed roots leave the keep-set in
the same epoch.

\paragraph{The delete-must-settle refinement.} One contract refinement
goes beyond the harness. Peritext deletes are logical, so a record may
